# Onyx — Agent Instructions

Read by every coding agent (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Copilot, Gemini CLI, …). This is the only project-instructions file; there is no CLAUDE.md.

## Read this first: testing and checks (Riley, 2026-11-15)

- **Do not run automated tests before merging, not even locally or "just the relevant one".** That means no `node --test`, no `frontend/scripts/test-*.mjs` (above all the `-ui.mjs` browser tests), no pytest and no `tests/run_offline_tests.py`. The scheduled suite runs every test against each deployed revision within 30 minutes, and a red run comes back as a normal fix PR. Running them first only slows you and the other agents on this PC.
- **Do type-check frontend changes:** run `npx tsc --noEmit` in `frontend/` before opening the PR (2–15 s). It is the production build's own check, not a test, and nothing else runs it before merge.
- **Still write tests.** Add or update tests when behavior changes. The "Tests:" lists in the sections below name the tests that cover an area so you know what to update. They are not steps to run.

## Delivery accountability (Riley, 2026-11-15)

- For authorized implementation, use headless Claude Code handoffs with **Codex independently reviewing the actual resulting diff**. Give the builder bounded scope and acceptance criteria; resolve concrete review findings before release. A builder's summary or exit code is not a review.
- **Launched, code written, reviewed, merged, deployed and verified are distinct states.** Record the task, attempt, exact revisions, evidence and next action. Never mark Done from launch, a model's claim, a merge, or a successful `/login` response alone.
- The orchestrator owns supervision through verified completion or an explicit blocker, without Riley having to remind it. Persist recovery state before long-running work; use bounded retries and reconcile existing work after restart instead of launching duplicates. Notify Riley of essential blockers and required decisions.
- Use one writer per task/worktree and one owner per release transition; respect existing locks and branch ownership. A changed head invalidates its review/check receipts. Preserve private receipts and recovery history across chats and restarts; never invent missing evidence.
- Verify the actual production behavior against acceptance criteria. For relevant UI changes, include the authenticated **Today entry point, desktop and phone widths**, and the affected interaction. Distinguish a deployed revision from observed product acceptance; record unavailable checks as gaps, not passes. The testing policy above still applies.
- Follow [delivery accountability](docs/delivery-accountability.md). Existing worker mechanics live in [personal-agents feature-worker documentation](https://github.com/rileybleeker/personal-agents/blob/main/docs/feature-worker.md); these requirements do not assert that every supervision or recovery mechanism is already implemented.

Personal health data aggregation and analytics platform.
Syncs biometric data from Garmin, WHOOP, and Eight Sleep into a unified Supabase Postgres database,
visualized via a Next.js frontend with AI-powered analysis through Claude.

## Tech Stack

- **ETL**: Python 3, httpx, garminconnect, supabase-py, python-dotenv
- **Database**: Supabase (Postgres 17), schema `pds`, 19 tables + `journal` unified view + 3 HRV analysis tables (`hrv_predictions`, `hrv_model_metrics`, `hrv_analysis_results`) + Spotify (`spotify_plays`, `spotify_tracks`, `spotify_artists`, `spotify_playlists`, `spotify_daily_signature` view) + Supplements (`supplement_products`, `supplement_intake`, `supplement_intake_by_compound` view, `daily_supplement_matrix` view) + Notion personal Journal (`journal_entries` with pgvector embeddings, `search_journal_entries` RPC) + Tanita body comp (`tanita_measurements` raw + `weight_log` daily rollup)
- **Frontend**: Next.js 15, React 19, Tailwind CSS 4, Recharts 3.8, TypeScript 5
- **Frontend perf architecture (2026-06-11, see `docs/perf_report_2026-06-11.md`)**: middleware verifies session JWTs **locally** via `supabase.auth.getClaims()` (project signs with ES256; never reintroduce a per-request network `getUser()` — it cost ~50-150 ms × ~13 requests per page view). Ten read-only dashboards are **ISR pages** (`export const revalidate = 3600`, matching the hourly ETL cadence): a server `page.tsx` runs the same `lib/queries` fns and passes `initial` data into a `<Name>Loader.tsx` client wrapper hosting the `dynamic(ssr:false)` chunk; the client seeds state from props and keeps its mount fetch as a **silent revalidation** (no skeleton — never skip it, a stale snapshot must heal). Pattern contracts: (1) server prefetch args must **explicitly mirror** the client effect's default-state args (several fn defaults diverge); (2) prefetch fns must `throw` on PostgREST error so the fail-open `try/catch` actually fails open; (3) write-coupled pages (`/supplements`, `/habits` with its on-load Notion-sync POST) are deliberately NOT ISR. Heavy tables are read via **explicit all-scalar column lists** (`WHOOP_*_COLS`/`GARMIN_*_COLS`/`EIGHT_SLEEP_TRENDS_COLS` in `queries.ts`) — `raw_json`-class jsonb blobs must never ride in page-load selects (whoop_cycles is ~70 kB/row of raw_json), and when an ETL adds a scalar column it must be appended to the matching list.
- **Theme: Direction D "Instrument" (2026-11-14, replaced the Terminal theme).** Dark only: warm graphite surfaces (`surface` #1A1A18, `surface-sunken` #151513 for the side rails, `surface-card` #232321, `surface-raised` #2C2C29), one signal-orange accent `#FF6A1A` for controls, selection, focus and the primary action (text on it: `text-on-accent`), WHOOP-style status colors `up` / `mid` / `down` (#5BC46E / #F4C84E / #F0584E) that only mean state and always come with an icon or a word, Archivo for text and DM Mono (`font-mono` / `font-display`) for numbers and small labels (both vendored in `app/fonts`, see `SOURCE.txt`). Shapes: cards `rounded-card shadow-card`, buttons / inputs / nav items `rounded-control`, the primary action `bg-accent text-on-accent rounded-full`. Success marks ("Saved", "on time") use `text-up`, never the accent. `components/MetricRing.tsx` draws the 270° dial used for recovery, strain, habits and reading. All colors come from the tokens in `app/globals.css` or, for charts, `lib/chart-theme.ts` (categorical and device colors validated for color-vision separation on the card surface; Garmin / WHOOP / Eight Sleep pass for every pair). `frontend/scripts/test-theme-tokens.mjs` (CI) fails on a Terminal-palette hex, on `rounded-[4px|5px|6px]`, or when the chart palette drifts from the CSS tokens.
- **Client cache, warm-up and skeletons (2026-11-14; `lib/client-cache.ts`, `lib/client-cache-idb.ts`, `lib/warmup.ts`, `components/AppData.tsx`)**. Every client-fetched page renders at once from the shared cache and then revalidates (the mount fetch is never skipped). Rules every page follows: (1) **one key per GET**: the URL (path + query) for `/api/*`, `"<area>:<name>"` for other reads (`CACHE_KEYS`); store the raw JSON so Today, the page and the warm-up share it, and check a cached copy like a fresh answer before showing it. (2) **The server is the source of truth**: every cached read is revalidated; 404 / 410 / 204 or a fetcher answering null removes the entry; set state with the `sameJson` bailout. (3) **Persisted to IndexedDB, scoped**: the store records the Supabase user id and `CACHE_SCHEMA_VERSION`; another user or another version deletes the whole store before anything is read (bump the version when a cached shape changes). (4) **Memory only, never on disk**: finance, procurement, medical records, screen time, experiments (`/api/experiments/today` also stamps the arm as revealed, so the warm-up never calls it) and anything token / auth / session shaped (`isSensitiveKey`; add a pattern there for any new sensitive route); the `/financials*`, `/procurement`, `/screen-time` and `/health/records` server payloads live only in the router's memory cache. Mixed payloads that can quote those (the agents inbox and threads `agents:*`, `/api/status` heartbeat text, `/api/urgent-email`) and care items are memory-only too (SEC-03, 2026-11-16); `attach()` deletes any persisted row whose key is memory-only under the current rules, so a policy change needs no sign-out (schema 2 also cleared older stores). The browser's own HTTP cache is outside that wipe, so such a route that is CDN-cached sends `Cache-Control: private, no-store` and puts the CDN rule in `Vercel-CDN-Cache-Control` (Vercel consumes it), as `/api/status` does. (5) **Expiry and size**: every entry has a timestamp and a max age (7 days by default), expired entries are deleted when read and by the sweep at startup and once a day; the store is capped at 25 MB with least-recently-used eviction, a quota error evicts and retries once, nothing throws into a page. (6) **Writes**: keep the optimistic UI; `AppData` watches fetch responses, and a successful non-GET to `/api/<area>/…` (or the agents Worker) drops the `/api/<area>` and `<area>:` entries and refetches registered ones in the background (`writeInvalidates`; a page that writes its own state through, like `/habits` taps, is listed there as an exception). (7) **Session end wipes everything**: sign-out (Sidebar / MobileNav call `wipeClientData()`; Supabase `SIGNED_OUT`), any `/api` 401 or redirect to `/login`, a load without a session and every `/login` visit clear the memory cache, the IndexedDB store, Cache Storage and this app's localStorage keys (`onyx*`, `chartCard.*`; others stay). An auth error that is not proof of sign-out (offline with an expired token) only turns persistence off. **Warm-up**: nav links use `navIntentProps(href)` (no viewport prefetch; hover / focus / pointer-down warm that page) and `AppData` warms every page 2.5 s after the first page has loaded (then in an idle slot), one step per idle slot, tabs and quick links first then by local visit counts, and again after the app was hidden 10 minutes: `router.prefetch` (full for static / ISR / read-only pages, `auto` = code + skeleton for write-coupled server pages without `router.refresh()`: `/health`, `/health/records`, `/orders`, `/procurement`) plus each page's GET reads (`DATA` in `AppData.tsx`; GET only, nothing written or marked read). A new page with client fetches adds its reads there. **Never blank**: every force-dynamic route has a `loading.tsx` (`components/PageSkeleton.tsx`: real heading, theme tokens, no animation). `/` renders Today directly (no redirect). Tests: `tests/client-cache.test.cjs`, `tests/warmup.test.cjs`.
- **Continuous scroll (2026-11-14; `components/ContinuousScroll.tsx`, `lib/feed-order.ts`, `lib/feed-registry.ts`, `lib/feed-seed.ts`, `components/FeedContext.tsx`)**. Below the end of the routed page the COMPLETE next page continues, then the next, in Riley's area order; scrolling up continues into the previous page. AppShell wraps the routed page in `<ContinuousScroll>` (not on `/login`, `/auth`, `/agents`, `/chat`, `/account`, `/install`, which are also left out of the sequence). **Convention:** every page in the scroll has `app/<route>/FeedSection.tsx` (`"use client"`, default export, no props) rendering the page's full content client-side, heading included, with its data from the client cache (finance, procurement, medical records and screen time stay memory-only there, as the cache already enforces by key). A FeedSection's heading carries `data-testid="feed-page-heading"`, never `page-heading` (the smoke suite looks for `page-heading` on the routed page): clients pass it as an optional `headingTestId` prop (Loaders forward it), or read `usePageHeadingTestId()` inside `InFeed`. **`lib/feed-registry.ts`** maps route -> `() => import("@/app/<route>/FeedSection")`; a route without an entry is skipped (the routed page always keeps its own place). `page.tsx` files never change for this: a direct visit or a reload renders the routed page normally. ISR pages render their Loader with `initial` = `readFeedSeed(route)`: a memory-only `<area>:feed` cache entry the page's client writes with `writeFeedSeed` after each successful load of its DEFAULT view (same shape and args as its server prefetch); the client still revalidates silently. **Order:** `feedSequence(NAV_HREFS, parseAreaOrder(value, known), routed, hasFeedSection)`: the UI preference `layout.areas` (this device's `onyx.layout.areas` copy first; shapes accepted by `parseAreaOrder`: `{order, hidden}`, a Personalize page layout `{v, grids: {main: {order, hide}}}`, or a list of hrefs / `{href, hidden}`; ids are hrefs or `analytics.hrv` / `analytics-hrv` style), else the nav order with Today first; hidden areas are skipped. **Rules:** the scroll starts 2 s after the routed page opened (then in an idle slot), or at once on the first wheel, touch, key or pointer gesture, so the opened page's own reads are not slowed by its neighbours' (speed budget, 2026-11-15); the next page is appended about 1.5 viewports before it is reached and the previous one prepended about 1 viewport before; pages load and mount one per idle frame with a skeleton (`PageSkeleton`) until then; only the page in view, its neighbours and pages within 1.5 viewports stay mounted (the routed page always), and a page more than 3 viewports away becomes a placeholder of its measured height; native scroll anchoring is off inside the scroll (Safari has none) and the element at the top of the screen is held in place by hand whenever anything above it changes height; every page has its own error boundary; mounting a page is a React transition (rendered in short slices, never one long task), and when the element at the top of the screen is replaced the page holding it keeps the screen still instead. **Scrolling never writes (2026-11-15):** a page in the scroll makes no request other than GET when it mounts or comes into view, and no GET with a side effect: no auto-save, nothing marked read, no notification cleared, nothing answered. The shared client checks `useInFeed()` (true inside every FeedSection; a `feed` prop where a test bundles the component by name, like `TodayClient`) and skips: the `/habits` Notion sync (`POST /api/habits/sync`), the `/activities` tag suggestions (`POST /api/activities/suggest-tags`, a model call that stores its answer), Today's experiment arm (`GET /api/experiments/today` stamps it as revealed; the scroll shows the copy `/today` read this session, if any) and the Personalize layout sync (`PartGrid`). The routed page keeps its own behaviour, and taps Riley makes inside the scroll still write exactly as on the page. A new page with a mount-time write gates it the same way. The page-data GETs of the finance pages and Today's money alerts may refresh the stored derived results in `pds.finance_result_cache` on the server, exactly as a visit to the page does; that is a cache of computed numbers, not a data change. Test: `frontend/tests/feed-no-writes.test.cjs`. **Registered (every nav page but Agents, Onyx Chat and Check-in, 25):** Today, Log, Activities, Nutrition, Supplements, Caffeine, Habits, Reading, Sleep, HRV, Travel, Bland-Altman, Screen Time, Spotify, Journal, Wins, Health, Medical records, Orders, Procurement, Financials, Budgets, Cash forecast, NYC, Status; the server-rendered ones read `GET /api/{travel,care,medical,orders,procurement,screen-time,finance}/page-data` (owner session, same-origin, no-store, the page's own server functions). **Page in view** = the page across the middle of the screen: `useActiveRoute()` (Sidebar, which also scrolls the item into view; MobileNav tab bar and drawer; CommandPalette's "Here"), `document.title` and the address bar follow it. The address changes with `history.replaceState` carrying the router's own state (`__NA`), so Next does not treat it as a navigation and `router.refresh()` still refreshes the routed page; the scroll puts the address back if the router rewrites it (on the next scroll and after every router update), but never while a navigation is under way: until the new routed page's reset effect has run (`syncedForRef`), nothing is written, because the previous page's observers would put its address back over the new page (2026-11-15). A nav click navigates normally and the scroll starts again from that page; the routed page is always in the scroll's list (a list built around the page being left, which a click landing as the scroll starts could leave behind, is reset to the routed page: 2026-11-15, the new page was otherwise not rendered and the address was rewritten to the old one). `GET /api/wins` (owner session, same-origin, no-store, `getWins()`) feeds the Wins page in the scroll. **Automated suites:** `sessionStorage["onyx.feed"] = "off"` (`FEED_OFF_SESSION_KEY`) turns the scroll off in that tab; the smoke suite sets it for every test, since its checks are about the routed page alone (a neighbour's chart must not stand in for a broken one). The perf timing run keeps the scroll on (neighbours' requests are part of a page view) but takes data-ready, chart and empty-state from the routed page only: neighbours are `section[data-feed-route]`, the routed page is `[data-feed-root] > div[data-feed-route]`. Tests: `frontend/tests/feed-order.test.cjs`.
- **Frontend render conventions (perf round 3, 2026-06-11)**: (1) **every recharts series element sets `isAnimationActive={false}`** — profiling showed mount animations never affect first paint (they run post-paint), cost up to ~820 ms of jank, and fully replay on every revalidation commit; (2) **ISR clients' silent-revalidation setters use the deep-equal bailout** `setX(prev => sameJson(prev, next) ? prev : next)` (`sameJson` in `lib/format.ts`) — the refetch is byte-identical to the seed in the common case, so the guard lets React skip the whole re-render; this makes **deterministic query ordering load-bearing** (any non-unique sort key needs a secondary `.order()`); (3) **chart-dense pages wrap below-fold sections in `components/DeferredMount.tsx`** (one-section-per-frame scheduler; everything mounts unconditionally without scroll — required by the smoke suite's below-fold assertions; never wrap above-fold content; give placeholders a realistic `minHeight`); (4) **tier-2 perf matviews** (`sql/perf_tier2_matviews.sql`): `tz_log_gaps_mat` + `hrv_prediction_gaps_mat` (/api/status), `spotify_daily_signature_mat`, `recovery_vs_pace_mat`, `hrv_residuals_mat` — same drift rule as the matrix matview (drop+recreate the `_mat` in the same migration as any change to its source view; never CASCADE); refreshed by `pds.refresh_perf_matviews()` at :12/:27/:42/:57 with ONE `perf_mats` heartbeat + /status card covering all five; both refresh functions use `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY` (2026-11-15, `sql/matview_refresh_concurrently.sql`): a plain refresh held an exclusive lock until the function committed, so page reads of these matviews (and, through held PostgREST connections, unrelated reads) waited 1-5 s twice an hour. Keep a unique, non-partial index on every matview.
- **AI Chat**: Claude Sonnet 4, agentic tool-use loop with 28 tools (11 query + mark_habit_complete + log_supplement_stack + log_supplement_intake + the 4 reading tools `get_reading_status` / `log_reading_session` / `add_book_highlight` / `record_reading_quiz` + query_journal + query_journal_entries + query_eight_sleep + search_spotify_catalog + query_spotify_tracks_by_features + create_spotify_playlist + the 4 nutrition tools `query_nutrition` / `query_meals_log` / `query_vitamins` / `query_micronutrient_trend`, whose descriptions encode the food→Cronometer / supplements→Onyx split so the model never double-counts). Habit completion via chat syncs to both Supabase and Notion. `log_supplement_stack` records a whole named supplement stack ("log my morning stack") as consumed — one intake per item — via `lib/supplement-stacks.ts`. `log_supplement_intake` (2026-11-09, `lib/supplement-intake.ts`) records ONE product from the `/supplements` list by name with a custom amount and clock times ("log 15 mg of Adderall at 8:30am and noon" → two rows of 1.5 doses of the 10 mg tablet): the product resolves by name, brand or ingredient text (brand names kept in an ingredient's `notes` match), a weight amount is divided by the label quantity and a count by the serving size, each time becomes an instant in the TZ from `pds.tz_for_instant` (a time before 06:00 is the awake tail = next calendar date, so it stays on the behavioral `intake_date`), and re-sending the same timed entry is skipped. No times: today → now, a past day → `intake_time` NULL. The chat system prompt also gets the current local time + behavioral day appended per request (the model has no clock). Playlist creation goes via `lib/spotify-server.ts` which refreshes the access token on every call against `pds.ci_tokens` and writes any rotated refresh token back so the Python ETL stays in sync. `query_journal_entries` supports semantic search via Voyage AI embeddings (voyage-3-large) — frontend embeds the user's query at request time, then RPC runs cosine similarity against pre-computed entry embeddings. **Chat replies and write receipts (2026-11-13):** `/chat` renders assistant replies as Markdown with the in-repo parser `lib/chat-markdown.ts` + `components/ChatMarkdown.tsx` (GFM tables, lists, bold, code, links; no markdown dependency). It builds React elements from a tree, so there is no HTML string anywhere and raw HTML in a reply shows as text; links are kept only for http(s), mailto and in-app paths. Write tools are the `WRITE_TOOLS` list in `lib/chat-receipts.ts` (habit, supplement intake, stack, reading session, highlight, quiz, playlist). Each carries the explicit-request gate in its description (`WRITE_GATE*`, mirrored in `CHAT_SCOPE`: only on an explicit request or a direct report in the user's latest message, never inferred; there is deliberately NO confirm dialog, so "log 15 mg of X at 8:30" still works in one message) and each produces a **receipt** that the server builds from the tool's own result (`buildWriteReceipt`), returned as `receipts` next to `response` (also on a 422 / 499 / 500 after a write ran) and shown in a box under the reply (`components/ChatReceipts.tsx`): saved / no change / not saved / check this. A new write tool must be added to `WRITE_TOOLS` (a test fails otherwise) and must answer `{success: true, …}` with the fields it wrote. A stack logged from chat for an earlier day stores no clock time (same rule as `/supplements`). Tests: `frontend/scripts/test-chat.mjs` + `frontend/scripts/test-chat-markdown.mjs` (CI, via the `scripts/test-*.mjs` glob) and the browser check `node tests/chat-regression.cjs` (local only; needs a compile build for the CSS).
- **System Status**: `/status` page — source cards for every ingestion ETL + the HRV Analysis predict path + the **HRV Retrain** card (predict and retrain tracked separately because the predict path keeps writing fresh forecasts from the cached model even when the retrain pipeline crashes — the audit caught the `category`/`categories[]` rename had been breaking every retrain attempt for weeks before this card existed). Each card has a **Method** row (`automated` cyan / `semi-automated` amber-300 / `manual` amber-500) and a Cadence row, KPI summary, 20-entry sync history. The HRV Retrain card derives status from `pds.sync_log` heartbeats emitted by `hrv_analysis.py` (`source='hrv_analysis', data_type='retrain'`), with `MAX(hrv_analysis_results.computed_at)` as fallback for historical runs that predate the heartbeat. >30h since last successful retrain = `failed`, 26-30h = `partial`. **Lesson learned (audit 2026-05-26):** any pipeline that writes to Supabase needs a sync_log heartbeat on EVERY run (success or failure) — without one, silent failures escape monitoring because downstream effects (predictions, dashboards) keep looking healthy via cached state. The `METHOD` map in `api/status/route.ts` is the source of truth for the automated/semi-automated/manual category — semi-automated = cron import that requires a user-triggered export from the source app (WHOOP and MFP have no API export; user must request CSV via the source's web UI, then the IMAP cron picks it up). `GET /api/status` queries `pds.sync_log` by `(source, data_type)` key + `MAX()` date per data table. Auto-refreshes every 60s. ReccoBeats + MusicBrainz are *enrichment* subsystems (not ingestion) — their freshness is based on `sync_start` recency (heartbeat) rather than data age, because "no new items to enrich today" is healthy, not stale. `enrichmentSource()` helper in `api/status/route.ts` encodes that semantics: >12h since last heartbeat = failed, >4h = partial, otherwise success. `spotify_etl.py` writes a sync_log row for each subsystem every run (even with `records_synced=0`).
- **Auth**: Supabase Auth — email + password (primary) with magic link fallback. Single owner account; new sign-ups disabled (2026-11-10). RLS on all tables (see the access model under Database). `/account` page exposes `supabase.auth.updateUser({ password })` for self-service password changes.
  - **`/api/habits/*` boundary (2026-06-14):** these routes write `habit_journal` (+ trigger Notion) with the service-role key, so middleware enforces a valid Supabase session **OR** a constant-time `x-onyx-ci-token` shared secret (`ONYX_CI_TOKEN`, fail-closed when unset). It is **NOT** matcher-exempt anymore — do NOT re-exempt `/api/habits`, that re-opens public writes (the bug fixed here). Unauthenticated `/api/habits` → 401 JSON (not a 307 to /login a curl could swallow). The hourly `habits-sync.yml` curl sends the header; **`ONYX_CI_TOKEN` must exist in BOTH GitHub Actions secrets and Vercel env (Production)** — rotate in both + redeploy if leaked. Browser `/habits` calls use the session path (no token needed). The same carve-out (`isCiRoute()` in `middleware.ts`) also admits exactly one more path, `/api/reading/highlights/import`, for the local Kindle harvester (2026-11-07) — do not widen it to `/api/reading/*`. The Kindle harvester's Amazon order pass uses `/api/orders/amazon/import` (2026-11-12) — never widen it to `/api/orders/*` (the `/orders` PATCH must stay session-only). A third exact path, `/api/screen-time/usage`, admits the Onyx Screen Time iPhone app (2026-11-09); the v1 Shortcuts entry `/api/screen-time/event` was removed 2026-11-13 together with its route — do not re-add it.
- **Hosting**: Vercel (frontend; **Pro plan since 2026-11-12** — the Hobby cap of 100 deployments/day was exhausted by agent PR previews, which blocked production deploys; `frontend/vercel.json` `ignoreCommand` now builds `master` only, so PRs get no Vercel preview), Supabase Cloud (database)

## Commands

```bash
# ETL (runs automatically via GitHub Actions every hour on :00)
python garmin_etl.py                    # Sync last 7 days
python garmin_etl.py --backfill-training-status 930  # Re-read only training readiness + acute/chronic load
python whoop_etl.py                     # Sync last 30 days
python whoop_journal_import.py <csv>    # ARCHIVE ONLY: retired WHOOP journal importer
python whoop_journal_email.py --once   # ARCHIVE ONLY: retired WHOOP journal email importer
python whoop_journal_watcher.py        # ARCHIVE ONLY: retired watcher; do not start routinely
python eight_sleep_etl.py               # Sync last 7 days
python myfitnesspal_import.py <csv>     # Manual fallback only; Cronometer is primary
python myfitnesspal_email.py --once    # Manual fallback only; Cronometer is primary
python <etl>.py --backfill N            # Backfill N days
python spotify_etl.py --auth            # Spotify OAuth sign-in in a local browser (fallback; the normal renewal is Reconnect on the /status Spotify card)
python spotify_etl.py                   # Sync recently-played (last 50 since high-water mark)
python spotify_etl.py --refeaturize     # Backfill audio features for tracks with NULL valence
python spotify_etl.py --backfill-artists # Enrich every distinct artist in spotify_plays (one-time, after schema change)
python spotify_etl.py --refresh-genres   # Re-fetch MusicBrainz tags for spotify_artists rows with empty genres
python tanita_etl.py --auth             # One-time Health Planet OAuth bootstrap (local, copy-paste code)
python tanita_etl.py --inspect          # Print newest raw measurement (timestamp TZ verification)
python tanita_etl.py                    # Sync last 14 days of Tanita weigh-ins
python tanita_etl.py --backfill N       # Backfill N days (paged in <=80-day API windows)
gh workflow run tanita-etl.yml -f days=120      # Same backfill from Actions (local box has no .env)
gh workflow run daily-etl.yml -f days=800 -f whoop_cycles_only=true  # Deep WHOOP cycle re-read (step_count); skips Garmin + the TZ pass
gh workflow run cronometer-etl.yml -f days=125  # Wide-window Cronometer re-import from Actions

# Cronometer (automated web-export ETL — runs 2x daily via GitHub Actions)
python cronometer_etl.py --auth         # One-time bootstrap: fresh login + 1-day export, validates creds/flow
python cronometer_etl.py                # Rolling 30-day export → run_import → Supabase
python cronometer_etl.py --days 14      # Custom window
python cronometer_etl.py --dry-run      # Fetch + parse, no DB writes
python cronometer_import.py <zip|dir|csvs>  # Manual fallback: import a hand-downloaded export

# CI Token Management
python ci_token_helper.py upload garmin   # Seed/update Garmin tokens in Supabase
python ci_token_helper.py upload whoop    # Seed/update WHOOP tokens in Supabase
python ci_token_helper.py upload spotify  # Seed/update Spotify tokens in Supabase
python ci_token_helper.py upload tanita   # Seed/update Tanita tokens in Supabase
python ci_token_helper.py upload cronometer # Seed/update Cronometer session in Supabase
python ci_token_helper.py download garmin # Restore Garmin tokens from Supabase
python ci_token_helper.py download whoop  # Restore WHOOP tokens from Supabase
python ci_token_helper.py download spotify # Restore Spotify tokens from Supabase
python ci_token_helper.py download tanita  # Restore Tanita tokens from Supabase
python ci_token_helper.py download cronometer # Restore Cronometer session from Supabase

# GitHub Actions
gh workflow run daily-etl.yml           # Manually trigger ETL workflow
gh workflow run whoop-journal-email.yml # ARCHIVE ONLY: not a routine sync; see warning below

# Frontend
cd frontend && npm run dev              # Dev server on :3000
cd frontend && npm run build            # Production build
cd frontend && npm run lint             # ESLint

# Install
pip install garminconnect supabase python-dotenv httpx requests timezonefinder
pip install -r requirements-analysis.txt  # HRV analysis deps
cd frontend && npm install

# HRV Analysis Pipeline (run once or after major data changes)
python hrv_analysis.py                  # Full pipeline: data + stats + models + store
python hrv_analysis.py --skip-analysis  # Skip stat plots (faster retraining)
python hrv_predict.py --predict         # Daily prediction (run after ETL)
python hrv_predict.py --backfill-only   # Just backfill actuals + recompute metrics
gh workflow run hrv-prediction.yml      # Manually trigger daily prediction in CI
```

**Retired WHOOP journal commands:** the journal pipeline was decommissioned on 2026-06-11. Its archive table is frozen and no longer read by the app. Do not run the importer, watcher, or manual workflow as routine maintenance or incident recovery. Any deliberate archive write requires a separate review of the freeze and the intended migration; current habits use the habits sync pipeline.

## Database

- Schema: `pds`
- All tables use upsert with conflict resolution (idempotent ETL)
- **`habit_journal` is THE single behavior table (WHOOP journal merge, 2026-06-11 — `sql/whoop_journal_merge.sql`).** The WHOOP in-app journal was replaced by the habits system: the 12,360-row WHOOP history (Oct 2024 → 2026-06-08, explicit Yes/No answers) was copied in verbatim with a `source` column ('whoop'|'habit'), and the 30 questions active in the final export became Notion habits with **VERBATIM question text as titles** (the question string is the HRV pipeline's variable identity — `journal_*` slugs derive from it). Key invariants:
  - **`source` routing is by QUESTION IDENTITY, not write path**: `habit_name_map.channel` ('whoop'|'habit') drives a trigger that stamps `source='whoop'` on any insert of a WHOOP-derived question — so post-cutover habit taps keep feeding the same `journal_*` feature columns. Native habits stay `source='habit'` → `habit_*` columns.
  - **WHOOP-derived habit names are FROZEN.** DB guard triggers RAISE on rename (map + journal level), and the sync route skips + surfaces blocked renames. Renaming one requires a coordinated migration (history rewrite + `hrv_analysis.py`/`causal_inference.py` hard-coded slug updates).
  - **The WHOOP era (cycle_date ≤ 2026-06-08, source='whoop') is IMMUTABLE** — guard trigger `zzz_block_frozen_whoop_era` rejects writes from every path; API routes also refuse with a clear error. Bypass for coordinated migrations only: `SET onyx.allow_frozen_writes = 'on'`.
  - **Date semantics across the cutover**: whoop-era rows keep `cycle_date` = WHOOP bedtime-day with a separate `behaviors_date` (bedtime−6h); post-cutover rows use `cycle_date` = behavior-day with `behaviors_date = cycle_date`. The **behaviors_date axis is continuous**; `cycle_date` is not. Behaviors-day **2026-06-08 is a permanent 1-day NaN hole** (final export covered through 06-07; the (2026-06-08, question) PK slots hold frozen rows) — do NOT "fix" it.
  - **Missing = unknown (NaN), post-cutover (policy REVERSED 2026-07-19; was missing=No 2026-06-11→2026-07-19)**: `hrv_analysis.py` no longer 0-fills unlogged `journal_*`/`habit_*` days — a missing row means "not logged", not "No". Explicit No rows map to 0.0 via `is_yes`; only explicitly answered days feed Welch No-arms, Spearman, and causal confounders post-cutover. The original 0-fill predated tri-state capture (absence was then the only way to express No); with explicit Yes/No rows and backdated grids, zero-filling would manufacture fake No days for unlogged gap weeks. Native daily habits' explicit-No backfill (AI, Lumosity, first completion → 2026-06-10) still stands as real data.
  - `pds.whoop_journal` is a **frozen archive** (write-blocked by trigger; kept as immutable backup of the original rows). The native 'Journaled' habit's 13 completions were merged into 'Journaled your thoughts?' (Yes-wins on behaviors_date; 6 No→Yes flips) and the habit retired.
- `journal` view = `SELECT … FROM habit_journal` (single-table since the merge; same column contract as the old whoop+habit UNION: cycle_date, behaviors_date, question, category, answer, notes, synced_at, source)
- Habit definitions are managed in Notion (Habits DB under Project Onyx, ID: `29cc936fd5e14ae8b10a4fe5c5f7a6cd`); the 30 WHOOP-derived habits are Frequency='Ad Hoc' so the /habits Today-ring + rate KPIs aren't distorted by 30 observational/negative questions (flip individual ones to daily in Notion if desired)
- Bidirectional sync: completions from Onyx/Chat update both Supabase and Notion; Notion "Last Completed" syncs to Supabase on page load. Notion LC is always recomputed as MAX(cycle_date) over **answer='Yes'** rows (the table carries explicit No rows now), restricted to **cycle_date > 2026-06-08** for WHOOP-derived habits.
- **Tri-state habit answers (2026-06-11).** Every (habit, day) cell is `Yes` / `No` / not-logged. **Direct selection UI:** /habits heatmap cells open a fixed-position Yes/No/Clear popover on click, for corrections (the 2026-06-11 one-tap "Today's Habits" checklist and its backdate picker were removed 2026-11-15: daily answers are entered on Log's check-in, see "One place to enter data"). **Writes are OPTIMISTIC** (`setAnswer` in `HabitsClient.tsx`: local state applies immediately, reverts on a non-OK response; per-cell monotonic seq guards rapid re-taps) and the `/api/habits/complete` route responds right after the Supabase write — the Notion Last-Completed mirror runs post-response via Next 15 `after()` (best-effort; the hourly habits-sync self-heals misses). Perf siblings: `getHabitJournal` pages in parallel (count-first, was ~10 serial 1000-row roundtrips at 365d), and /habits fetches once on mount — range switches re-derive locally with no refetch/skeleton. `/api/habits/complete` accepts `answer: 'Yes' | 'No' | null` where **null DELETES the row** — row absence stays the canonical "not logged" (the missing=No pipeline fill, the (cycle_date, question) PK, and the backfill trigger all assume it; legacy `undo: true` still maps to null). Chat's `mark_habit_complete` takes an optional `answer` enum (the tool description tells the model to record 'No' only on an explicit "I didn't…"). Explicit 'No' counts exactly like not-logged in every KPI/streak/Notion-LC computation (all keyed on answer='Yes') and maps to 0.0 via pivot_journal/pivot_habits' `is_yes`. In the HRV ANALYSIS pipeline the two now differ (2026-07-19): explicit No = 0.0, not-logged = NaN (unknown) — see the missing=unknown policy bullet. `getHabitJournal` fetches Yes+No rows; the hourly Notion-LC sync SKIPs (and surfaces) any date explicitly marked 'No' instead of flipping it back to 'Yes' — a direct answer in Onyx out-ranks Notion's date-only signal. DB hardening: CHECK `habit_journal_answer_check` (`answer IN ('Yes','No')`), applied as a Supabase migration + mirrored in `sql/habit_journal_answer_check.sql`. Toward (but not yet) the parked daily check-in flow — the capture mechanism now exists; the guided prompt does not.
- **Daily check-in (`/check-in`, 2026-11-07) is the primary habit-capture surface** — the successor to the WHOOP journal's daily compliance (logging had stopped entirely after 2026-07-19). Every evening at 21:30 ET the sms-reminders Worker posts one "Check-in for <day>" card in the `check-in` conversation of `/agents` (push deep-links straight to `/check-in?date=…`); if it is still open at 9 AM the next day, the card is re-posted once. The screen (`app/check-in/CheckInClient.tsx`) shows the **core** questions (the WHOOP-derived questions + daily/weekdays habits, grouped by category, explicit Yes/No each) and a collapsed **"Also did"** section (weekly / ad-hoc native habits; blank = not logged). **Answers are written to the behavior day the check-in is FOR, never the clock day** — so finishing after midnight or the next morning lands correctly. Accepts behavioral today and the 2 days before only (catch-up tabs); older days stay unlogged (= NaN in the pipeline). **Data-backed pre-fills only, labeled with their source**: alcohol Yes when Cronometer has `alcohol_g > 0`; rest day No on a training workout (WHOOP sports other than yoga/activity/other/walking/sauna, or any non-walk Garmin activity), Yes when no workout at all, else blank; plane Yes when `pds.tz_for_instant` changes across the day. Everything else starts blank. Taps persist as a localStorage draft per day. `GET/POST /api/habits/checkin` (under the `/api/habits/*` auth boundary) writes the whole day in one upsert/delete (null = delete = not logged) and mirrors Notion Last Completed via `after()` only for habits whose value moves; answers save as they are tapped (autosave, 2026-11-15: the local draft first, then one request per touched part 600 ms after the last tap; no Save button), and once every core daytime question of the day has a saved answer the screen calls the Worker's `POST /onyx/checkin/done` once to settle the card (`dayComplete` in `lib/checkin-cells.ts`; on today that is the save that also stores the day's pre-fills; a half-answered day keeps its card; this also suppresses that evening's send if Riley checked in on their own). Active-habit fetch is shared via `lib/habits-notion.ts`. The backfill-signal trigger now also exempts **yesterday** (`affected_date < today_et - 1`) so a late check-in doesn't schedule an extra full retrain — the 12:00 UTC daily retrain covers it.
- **Check-in: less typing (2026-11-13, rules + thresholds in `lib/checkin.ts`).** The 38-question screen was abandoned after 3 days (2026-11-08..10), so: (1) **`OCCASIONAL`** — 14 questions with at most 4 Yes answers ever (dimmed lights, read non-screen in bed, vacation day, MEMOS, Case Study, History, Math, Meditated, Chess, Substack, Lumosity, Duolingo Spanish, News, BI/Python/AL/Excel) are asked only in the collapsed "Also did" section (blank = not logged = NaN in the pipeline); a code list, nothing changed in Notion — remove a name to ask it daily again. (2) **More pre-fills, same contract as before** (shown with their source, stored only when Riley saves, never written by the GET): "Viewed a screen device in bed?" from Screen Time `minutes_last_hour_before_sleep_est` (≥20 min Yes, ≤1 min No; the Yes threshold was 10 until 2026-11-13 and is printed in the Yes label — hourly data lets phone use that ended 60–90 min before sleep add up to 15 min to the estimate, so 20 means use inside the last hour itself; **only once that day's sync is complete** — `lib/checkin-screen.ts:settledLastHourMinutes`: synced after the day ended, sleep scored, sync at least an hour past sleep onset — because the estimate reads 0, not NULL, between WHOOP scoring the sleep and the phone's ~10 AM sync, which made the 9 AM catch-up card suggest No from missing data); "Slept in the same bed as usual?" from Eight Sleep (a non-`away` row dated D+1 with ≥3 h slept = Yes; No only when that night is missing and a later night has synced — checked against 126 past answers: 93/97 Yes nights had the row, 25/29 No nights did not); "Journaled your thoughts?" from `pds.journal_entries` (Yes on an entry; No only for a finished day); Duolingo / Substack / News Yes from ≥3 min in the app (never a No). (3) **One-tap answers**: "Usual kit" fills the 5 sleep-kit questions with the last answer given to each (`usualKit`), and "No for the other N" answers every blank daytime question. Both are ordinary taps (draft, then saved like any tap). (4) **Sleep is asked per finished NIGHT, never on a day tab and never for tonight.** An answer stored on behavior day D describes the night that CLOSES day D (the pipeline scores day D's answers against that sleep), so on day D itself that night has not happened. The screen therefore has two parts: a **Sleep** block (`CheckinPayload.nights` from `sleepNights(today)`: last night = behavior day today−1, always; the night before while its saved answers are incomplete), labeled "Last night · Thu night → Fri morning", with that night's kit button and pre-fills; and **Daytime** (the day tabs, whose progress counts daytime questions only). Each autosave POSTs the day under its date and each changed night under THAT NIGHT's date. Do not move the sleep questions back into a day tab. Guards (from the 2026-11-13 review): the screen reloads when it comes back after a minute away, and the POST carries the `today` the screen was drawn with and answers **409 `stale`** when the day has rolled over, so a page left open overnight cannot write "last night" onto the night before (the refused taps are dropped and must be re-answered); **a part (the day, or one night) is saved only when Riley tapped or cleared something in it** — its pre-fills go with it, untouched pre-fills stay suggestions, and the day's card is settled only when the day was sent (`savePlan` in `lib/checkin-cells.ts`, which holds the screen's pure state rules: drafts carry the stored answer they were made against, so a tap is ignored once that answer changed elsewhere; on behavioral today the day part sends only Riley's taps and clears, and the day's pre-fills are stored only in the save that completes the day counting them (every core daytime question shows an answer), which is also when the card settles; catch-up days and nights keep pre-fills-with-the-first-touch); **every write path refuses a Yes/No to a sleep question dated behavioral today or later** (`sleepDateError`, used by `/api/habits/checkin`, `/api/habits/complete` and the chat tool; clearing is allowed); the behavioral-today fallback is Eastern time − 6 h; "Read (non-screened device) while in bed?" is a sleep question shown as an optional row in the night block. Other write paths follow the same rule: the chat tool stores a sleep answer with no date on yesterday (`sleepAnswerDate`), the `/habits` "Today's Habits" checklist leaves the sleep questions out, and the hourly `/api/habits/sync` never turns a Notion "Last Completed" date into a sleep answer (a date cannot say which night; skipped quietly, 2026-11-14). Known limit: before 06:00 local the night just slept is not offered yet (behavioral today has not rolled), which is why the weekday span, not "Last night", is the main label. Catching up the night before (today−2) can schedule one extra hourly retrain, as the catch-up day tabs always could. The sleep answers saved on the old screen (cycle_date 2026-11-09 and 11-10; 2026-11-08 has none) were given 2–4 h before the night they are stored on began (saved Mon 22:07 and Wed 00:30 ET, sleep onset 02:04 and 02:43), so they most likely describe the night before. Left as stored (settled 2026-11-14): the 8 answers are identical on both days, Eight Sleep (6.6 h, 5.8 h) and Screen Time (47 and 28 min in the last hour) confirm same-bed and screen Yes for the stored nights, and the kit is the same on every answered night 11-09..13 apart from one sleep mask (11-12), so moving them a night would change no value on 11-09 and only blank 11-10. "AI" is a learning habit, not app use, so it is not derived. **"Say it once" box (2026-11-14):** a collapsed box in the Daytime part where Riley types or dictates a few sentences; `POST /api/habits/checkin/voice {date, text, today}` makes one Claude call that maps them to Yes/No for that day's daytime questions AND for **last night's** sleep questions (`night` = `sleepAnswerDate(today)`, the newest finished night; every sleep answer passes `sleepDateError`, so never tonight, and the page puts them only into the night block on screen with that date), and returns the labels of a **journal draft** of the same words (title, mood, confidence, topics, following the journal field rules in the Notion journal note below; the route's prompt mirrors them, so change both together). A screen whose `today` is old gets 409 `stale` and reloads. The page applies the answers as ordinary taps marked "from what you said", which save like taps; the route writes nothing and never stores or logs the text. The journal draft (a card under the box) keeps the words **verbatim** as the body, with editable labels, and is written only by **Save to journal** → `POST /api/journal/entry` (session only and same-origin, never a CI route; `lib/journal-entry.ts`), which creates the Notion "Entries" page with all six properties set (a repeat with the same date and title returns the page already there); `journal_etl.py` brings it into `pds.journal_entries` at :35. Once it is saved, "Journaled your thoughts?" gets a draft Yes for that day ("from the journal entry you saved"). **iPhone Shortcut hand-off:** opening `/check-in#say=<URL-encoded words>` puts the words in the box and runs Fill once on the fresh check-in (a fragment never reaches the server, and it is cleared from the address at once); the Shortcut itself lives on Riley's phone. Tests: `frontend/tests/checkin.test.cjs`, `frontend/tests/checkin-voice.test.cjs`, `frontend/tests/checkin-client.test.cjs`, `frontend/tests/journal-entry.test.cjs`.
- `habit_name_map` tracks Notion page ID → name (+ `channel`); renaming a **native** habit in Notion auto-updates all historical `habit_journal` entries via the FK's ON UPDATE CASCADE (the sync renames the map row and lets the cascade do the work); the 29 retired WHOOP questions have synthetic `whoop-archived:<md5>` map rows for FK coverage
- **Backdated habit completions trigger HRV retraining.** A Postgres trigger (`habit_journal_backfill_trigger`, defined in `sql/habit_journal_backfill_trigger.sql`) fires on every INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on `pds.habit_journal` and emits a `pds.sync_log` row (`source='habit_journal', data_type='backfill_signal'`) whenever the affected `cycle_date` is in the past (ET). **No-op UPDATEs are suppressed (2026-06-11)** — the hourly sync's idempotent Last-Completed upserts previously fired 3 signals/hour, scheduling a full retrain EVERY hour (31 in 48h); the sync route also now skips the upsert when the completion row already exists. `hrv_backfill_check.py` reads this signal alongside its row-scan checks; either path setting `backfill_detected=true` triggers a full `hrv_analysis.py` retrain on the next hourly tick. The trigger covers paths the original row-scan missed: the UPDATE branch of upsert (which doesn't bump `synced_at` because the API call doesn't include it in the patch object) and DELETE (which leaves no row for `WHERE synced_at > last_analysis` to find — relevant when Riley undoes a backdated completion). Today's writes are deliberately suppressed by the trigger; same-day mutations are picked up by the daily safety-net retrain at 12:00 UTC instead, since an hourly retrain on every habit tap would be wasteful.
- **Habits are analyzed as a first-class category in `hrv_analysis.py`** (alongside but separate from journal). The pipeline loads `source` from the unified `pds.journal` view and routes WHOOP entries through `pivot_journal` (`journal_*` columns) and habit completions through `pivot_habits` (`habit_*` columns, label preserved from Notion). After merging, unlogged days stay NaN (policy 2026-07-19; the per-habit first-completion-forward 0-fill was removed) — explicit Yes → 1.0, explicit No → 0.0, no row → NaN = "not logged = unknown". Four habit-specific `hrv_analysis_results` rows are written every run: `correlation/spearman_habit`, `habit_impact/all` (Welch's t-test mirroring `journal_impact`), `feature_importance/shap_habit`, and `model_comparison/error_modes_by_habit`. `habit_` is also in `CONTROLLABLE_FEATURE_PREFIXES` (actionable-only SHAP ranking) and in the SARIMAX/Prophet exog seed prefix list. The `/analytics/hrv` dashboard surfaces all four via dedicated Habit sub-sections in Prediction Drivers, HRV Correlates, and a sibling "Habit Impact" card next to Journal Impact. With sparse history (each habit needs ≥5 Yes-nights + ≥5 No-nights for the t-test, and ≥20 non-null days for Spearman) the chart populates incrementally as completions accumulate.
- `myfitnesspal_nutrition` stores daily nutrition totals (calories, macros, fiber, sugar, sodium) + `meals_json` JSONB for per-meal breakdown. Import via CSV export (Settings → Export Data in MFP app). `myfitnesspal_email.py` (IMAP, shares the WHOOP-journal mailbox credentials) is no longer scheduled: the `mfp-email.yml` cron was removed at the 2026-05-31 Cronometer cutover and the workflow is `workflow_dispatch` only. Manual fallback: run `myfitnesspal_import.py <csv>` (or `myfitnesspal_email.py --once`). **MFP is the pre-2026-05-31 historical archive only — see the Cronometer migration below.**
- **Cronometer is the sole new-day nutrition source (cutover 2026-05-31; replaced MyFitnessPal).**
  - **Why:** Cronometer's NCCDB/USDA backbone tracks ~62 micronutrients per food (vitamins, minerals, omega fractions, amino acids) that MFP never exported — the migration was for micronutrient + per-meal-timing coverage.
  - **Tables (`cronometer_schema.sql`):** `pds.cronometer_nutrition_daily` (one row/behavioral-day, PK `calendar_date`, from `dailysummary.csv` `Group='Total'` rows) and `pds.cronometer_servings` (one row/logged food entry, from `servings.csv`; idempotent re-import: the dates in the export are replaced in ONE transaction by `pds.replace_cronometer_servings(p_dates, p_rows)` (`sql/cronometer_replace_servings.sql`, service-role only), one RPC call per import with no fallback to separate delete + insert requests; an empty export makes no call; test `tests/test_cronometer_atomic_replace.py`). Both carry the ADR-0001 quad (`onyx_et_date`/`onyx_behavioral_date`/`onyx_local_date`/`onyx_tz_source`) + the full ~68-column nutrient set + future-proof nullable columns (beta_carotene, biotin, choline, iodine, chromium, molybdenum — not in the default export). RLS mirrors `myfitnesspal_nutrition` (anon read / service_role full).
  - **Ingestion (automated 2026-06-11): `cronometer_etl.py` + `cronometer-etl.yml`.** Cronometer has **no public API and no scheduled/emailed export** (re-verified June 2026), so the ETL replays the web app's **unofficial** export endpoints — the same flow `gocronometer`/`cronometer-mcp` use: GET `/login/` → scrape `anticsrf` → POST `/login` (sets `sesnonce` cookie) → GWT-RPC `authenticate` (user id) → GWT-RPC `generateAuthorizationToken` (nonce) → GET `/export?nonce=…&generate=servings|dailySummary&start=…&end=…`. Output CSVs are byte-identical to a manual export, so the ETL **hands them straight to `cronometer_import.run_import()`** — the same parser, idempotency (servings delete-by-date, daily upsert), behavioral dating, and `sync_log` heartbeats as before. Runs **twice daily** (`30 2` + `0 16` UTC) over a rolling **30-day** window (re-import self-heals app-side edits). **Fragility + self-healing:** the two GWT "magic values" (x-gwt-permutation + serialization-policy strong-name) change when Cronometer ships a new web build; `resolve_gwt_values()` re-derives them from the live `cronometer.nocache.js`/`cache.js` each run, with verified 2026-06-11 fallbacks (`9B5E…A7` / `08048AF8…C5`) — so a redeploy self-heals without a code change. The RPC method names + type-id signatures (`com.cronometer.shared.rpc.CronometerService`, `AuthScope/2065601159`) drift far less often; if `authenticate` returns `//EX[…]` with valid creds, re-scrape from the browser Network tab or switch to the `cronometer-mcp` PyPI package. **Session reuse:** Cronometer rate-limits logins aggressively, so the cookie jar + user id + GWT values persist to `pds.ci_tokens` (`service='cronometer'`, via `ci_token_helper.py download/upload cronometer`) and are reused; a fresh login happens only when the saved session can't mint a token. **Creds:** `CRONOMETER_EMAIL` / `CRONOMETER_PASSWORD` (`.env` + GitHub secrets); email+password account, no 2FA (2FA would block headless login). **Bootstrap (one-time, local):** `python cronometer_etl.py --auth` (fresh login + 1-day export, validates) → `python ci_token_helper.py upload cronometer`. **`cronometer_watcher.py` + `cronometer_inbox/` are retained as a manual fallback** (drop an export ZIP if you ever need to force a specific range); `cronometer_import.py <zip|dir|csvs>` is still the runnable CLI for backfills. `/status` Cronometer card is now `automated` ("Web export ETL"); freshness = `MAX(calendar_date)` + the `cronometer|daily` heartbeat. **Both heartbeats are emitted even on a zero-row export (fixed 2026-10-17)** — a logging pause must read as "ETL healthy, data stale", never as a missing heartbeat.
  - **Behavioral-day nutrient totals (2026-06-09): `pds.cronometer_nutrition_behavioral_daily`** (sql/`cronometer_nutrition_behavioral_daily.sql`) re-aggregates the full nutrient set by TRUE behavioral day — `cronometer_servings` summed by `onyx_behavioral_date` (Gold timestamp −6h rule, TZ-aware via the `user_tz_log` ladder), with a dailysummary fallback member for diary days with zero servings coverage (dedupe-guarded so the matrix join always sees one row per day). Exists because `dailysummary.csv` totals are keyed by Cronometer's diary day with no time component, so a post-midnight pre-bed meal logged truthfully would land its macros on the wrong behavioral day for HRV analysis. **Logging rule: always log Cronometer entries to the ACTUAL clock day, in real time — NEVER manually backdate a post-midnight meal.** The timestamp does the attribution; manually backdating a 1 AM entry to yesterday's diary makes the −6h rule attribute it a further day back AND corrupts meal timing. Verified live: servings sums match dailysummary totals exactly per diary day, and the 12:33 AM pizza + 1:20 AM candy of clock June 3 correctly moved to behavioral June 2 (1733.94 → 2793.94 kcal). Repointed consumers: the matrix `cn` relation (join + spine member; migration `daily_health_matrix_behavioral_cronometer_behavioral_totals`), the dietary side of `daily_micronutrient_totals` (migration `daily_micronutrient_totals_behavioral`), and frontend `getDailyNutrientsFull`. The `/nutrition` "Today's Meals" widget now filters servings by `onyx_behavioral_date` = behavioral-today (via `/api/behavioral-today`) so the list doesn't flip to an empty day at midnight during an awake tail. NOT repointed by design: `/status` freshness (tracks raw-import recency on the base table) and the raw tables themselves.
  - **Matrix integration:** `pds.daily_health_matrix_behavioral` (sql/`cronometer_matrix_view.sql`) gains a `cronometer_nutrition_daily cn` join (on `onyx_behavioral_date`; **`cn` repointed to `cronometer_nutrition_behavioral_daily` 2026-06-09**, column set unchanged) + a Cronometer spine-UNION member. New appended columns: `nutrition_calories/protein_g/carbs_g/fat_g/fiber_g/sugar_g/sodium_mg/water_ml` = `COALESCE(cn.*, mfp.*)` (Cronometer wins, MFP fills pre-cutover history), `nutrition_source` ('cronometer'|'mfp'), plus Cronometer-only micros (`vit_*`, `calcium_mg`…`selenium_mcg`, `omega3_g`/`epa_g`/`dha_g`/`ala_g`, amino acids, fat fractions, `nutrition_caffeine_mg`). **The legacy `mfp_*` columns are kept untouched** (archive); CREATE OR REPLACE appended the new columns at the tail. (The legacy `pds.daily_health_matrix`, since dropped 2026-11-08, never had nutrition columns.)
  - **Food vs supplement source-of-truth split:** **food → Cronometer, supplement pills → Onyx `/supplements`, never both.** Cronometer can't replicate Onyx's UNII per-compound rollup, per-compound causal treatments, per-dose timestamps, or DSLD/vision capture — and ~60% of Riley's stack (nootropics/adaptogens/performance compounds like theanine, creatine, ashwagandha) has no Cronometer column at all. Logging supplements in Cronometer would double-count their micros into the nutrient totals. `pds.daily_micronutrient_totals` (sql/`daily_micronutrient_totals.sql`) recombines them: per nutrient it exposes `_dietary` (Cronometer) + `_supplement` (Onyx UNII rollup, unit-normalized: mg→µg ×1000, mg→IU ×40000 for Vit D, mg→g ÷1000 for omega) + `_total`. Query/UI convenience only — NOT a HRV-pipeline input (the pipeline keeps dietary and per-compound-supplement signals separate).
- **Migration log: MFP → Cronometer (2026-05-31).** Rationale: micronutrient + automatic per-meal-timing coverage. Preserved: `pds.myfitnesspal_nutrition` + all `mfp_*` columns (never dropped), `myfitnesspal_import.py`/`myfitnesspal_email.py`/`myfitnesspal_schema` (runnable fallback), `pds.legacy_mfp_nutrition_archive` view (frozen MFP era ≤ cutover). Disabled: `mfp-email.yml` cron (schedule removed; `workflow_dispatch` kept). HRV pipeline (`hrv_analysis.py` NUTRITION_COLS + `causal_inference.py` CONTINUOUS_TREATMENTS) repointed `mfp_*`→`nutrition_*` and gained ~27 micronutrient features (family `nutrition`); `net_calories` now uses WHOOP TDEE (kJ÷4.184) since Cronometer has no MFP-style `exercise_kcal`. **Known gaps:** (1) Cronometer's branded/restaurant DB is smaller than MFP's crowd-sourced one — expect occasional missing items (add custom foods). (2) First weeks: frequents/favorites don't transfer; logging is slower until built up. (3) `mfp_exercise_kcal` is NULL for new days — drop any standalone dependency on it (WHOOP kJ is canonical TDEE). (4) **Per-entry timestamps require Cronometer Gold (purchased 2026-05-31; enable `More → Display → Show timestamps in diary`, off by default).** With Gold timestamps in the export, **automatic meal-timing is live**: `cronometer_import.py:behavioral_dates()` localizes the naive clock `Time` to the TZ Riley was in that day (via the `pds.user_tz_log` ladder — ET at home) → proper `event_time` + ADR-0001 triple (behavioral attribution automatic, no backdating). `pds.meal_timing_from_cronometer` (mirrors the live `meal_timing_daily` LATERAL pattern) aggregates first/last-meal hour, eating window, and the bedtime-anchored gap; `daily_health_matrix_behavioral`'s 5 `meal_*` columns are now `COALESCE(cronometer_gold, meal_events)`. **`pds.meal_events` (manual `/nutrition` quick-log) is demoted to a fallback/override** — it still covers pre-Gold history and any day logged without times. ⚠️ Auto-timing is only accurate if meals are logged close to when eaten (Cronometer stamps "now"); batch-logging at night corrupts the timing — that's when the `meal_events` override earns its keep. Validated end-to-end (importer TZ test + synthetic Gold round-trip → matrix COALESCE) on 2026-05-31; activates per-day as timestamped exports land.
  - **Meal timing is now sourced SOLELY from Cronometer (2026-06-14).** The WHOOP-derived habit "Ate food close to bedtime?" (HAB-21, Notion page `37dbf5b4…`, feature slug `journal_ate_food_close_to_bedtime`) was **deactivated** (Notion `Active`=false — uncheck the checkbox; do NOT rename a frozen WHOOP-derived habit) because it duplicated the quantitative `meal_last_meal_to_bedtime_min` (+ the other `meal_*`) Cronometer features. `hrv_analysis.py` now **drops `journal_ate_food_close_to_bedtime`** alongside the `journal_consumed_caffeine` drop in `build_feature_matrix` (one drop removes it from Stage-1 Spearman, Welch `journal_impact`, SHAP, error-modes-by-journal, and the causal binary auto-enumeration), and its `_lag1` entry + dead `JOURNAL_LABELS` row were removed. No `causal_inference.py` / SQL / frontend change (causal auto-enumerates by `journal_` prefix; HRV dashboard is data-driven). History preserved: all 288 `habit_journal` rows for the question (285 frozen WHOOP-era + 3 post-cutover) are retained, immutable — only future completions stop (the deactivated habit leaves the /habits checklist). Change takes effect on the next `hrv_analysis.py` retrain.
- `ci_tokens` table stores rotating OAuth tokens for GitHub Actions (Garmin + WHOOP)
- **Access model (2026-11-10, `sql/pds_owner_only_read.sql`): the anon key reads NOTHING in `pds`** (no schema USAGE — the key ships in the site's JS and the repo is public). The signed-in owner (`auth.users` id `dc041abc-…`, the only account; sign-ups are OFF in Supabase Auth) reads tables through `owner_read` RLS policies; service role = full access. Frontend: `lib/supabase.ts` uses the service role on the server (ISR prefetch) and the cookie session in the browser; `middleware.ts` accepts only the owner id. A new table the browser reads needs `GRANT SELECT … TO authenticated` + an `owner_read` policy (re-run part 1 of that file). Older schema files that grant/create policies for anon are now inert — never `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA pds TO anon`. Screen Time v2 tables (`phone_usage_*`) are service-role only, not even owner-readable. **Materialized views (`*_mat`) have no row security, so the browser never reads one (SEC-01-B, 2026-11-16):** each read is a `read*` function in `lib/queries.ts` / `lib/queries-hrv.ts` that starts with `assertServerRead`, runs on the server with the service role and reaches the browser only through `GET /api/derived/<name>` (`lib/derived-reads.ts`: fixed names and date rule; owner session, same-origin, no-store, never in `isCiRoute()`); a new matview read adds a name there, and `frontend/tests/derived-reads.test.cjs` fails on a direct read anywhere else.
- **UI preferences (2026-11-14, `sql/ui_preferences.sql`, `lib/ui-preferences.ts`, `/api/ui/preferences`)**: layout choices that follow Riley across devices, one row per key in `pds.ui_preferences` (`key` lower-case dotted, e.g. `overview.cards`; `value` a JSON object or array of at most 4 KB). Owner-readable (`owner_read`), written only by `PUT /api/ui/preferences` `{key, value}` with the service role; `GET /api/ui/preferences?key=…` answers `{key, value, updatedAt}` (value null = nothing saved). The route is owner-session only (never add `/api/ui` to `isCiRoute()`), same-origin JSON, 8 KB body cap, logs a database code only. Layout only: never store health or finance data there. Reuse it for any new synced layout setting (sidebar order, Personalize) under its own key instead of adding a table. Test: `frontend/tests/ui-preferences.test.cjs`.
- Sync operations logged to `pds.sync_log`
- WHOOP journal data is boolean-only (Yes/No) — WHOOP's CSV export never included quantity values entered in the app (e.g., "3 drinks", "200mg caffeine"). The merged history inherits this; quantities live in the dedicated logs (caffeine_events, supplement_intake, Cronometer).
- **Migration log: WHOOP journal → habits (2026-06-11).** The manual WHOOP app export → email ETL is decommissioned; all behavior tracking goes through the habits system. Preserved: `pds.whoop_journal` (frozen, write-blocked archive), raw CSVs in `journal_archive/`, the importer scripts (fallback). Removed: the `whoop-journal-email.yml` cron, the WHOOP Journal `/status` card (Habits card covers the merged table), the `whoop_cycles` → journal behaviors_date refresh trigger, `whoop_journal` from `hrv_backfill_check.py`. IMAP secrets kept (shared with `mfp-email.yml`). HRV pipeline unchanged except the post-cutover journal 0-fill — `journal_*`/`habit_*` feature identities are byte-stable across the cutover. Next step (parked): a daily check-in flow to capture explicit yes/no answers with WHOOP-journal-level compliance.
- HRV analysis tables: `hrv_predictions` (model forecasts + actuals), `hrv_model_metrics` (rolling eval), `hrv_analysis_results` (correlations, journal impact, model comparison as JSON)
- **Multi-horizon XGBoost + baseline backtest (h=1..7).** Previously only SARIMAX wrote multi-horizon rows to `pds.hrv_model_metrics`; XGBoost / baseline_naive / baseline_7d_avg / baseline_dow were `horizon_days=1` only, leaving the "Accuracy by Forecast Horizon" chart with only purple SARIMAX bars at t+2..t+7. Fixed by parameterizing `prepare_ml_data(df, horizon=1)` so the same feature matrix can be paired with the HRV of the sleep h−1 nights later (`hrv_alignment.outcome_series(df, h)`; h=1 = the same row) for any h, then iterating h ∈ [1..7] in `run_evaluation`'s walk-forward backtest (XGBoost retrains per fold per horizon; baselines compute per (i, h) — naive/7d_avg are constant across h while DOW shifts with the target weekday). Each h has its own residual std for prediction intervals. The h=1 row of each model is still stored under `eval_results[m_name]` for backward-compat with the headline "Model Comparison" table and CLI prints. Downstream analyses that conflate horizons (Diebold-Mariano paired test, error-mode-by-journal-flag, residual histograms / vs-predicted / DOW plots, rolling-30d-MAE chart) are explicitly filtered to `horizon_days == 1` — they're interpretable only for the headline tonight's-sleep model. Frontend `getHrvModelMetrics` bumped from `.limit(20)` → `.limit(100)` since one eval_date now writes ~36 rows (xgboost×7 + sarimax×7 + 3 baselines×7 + prophet×1). The chart will populate on the next `python hrv_analysis.py` run or the daily 12:00 UTC retrain. Hyperparameters: h>1 reuses h=1's tuned shape (max_depth=4, lr=0.05, n=200) without per-horizon Optuna so the multi-horizon sweep stays under a few minutes.
- **Live vs backtest accuracy rows (2026-11-11).** `pds.hrv_model_metrics` is keyed (eval_date, model, horizon_days): the retrain's walk-forward backtest uses the plain model names, and `hrv_predict.py` writes its live 30-day accuracy as `<model>_live_rolling` (h=1, real forecasts only, one per date, created after the 2026-11-11 01:00 ET alignment cutover, written once 5 nights are scored). Never write live metrics under a plain name: until 2026-11-11 the hourly job replaced the backtest h=1 row, so older eval_dates hold hybrid h=1 rows (page readers prefer `model_version 'backtest%'`).
- **HRV variable-coverage audit (2026-05-21)** — `docs/hrv_variable_coverage_audit_2026-05-21.md` enumerates every variable from every data source vs every test (Spearman, Welch journal/habit/supplement/nutrition, Granger, XGBoost/SHAP, SARIMAX/Prophet, AIPW/PSM causal). Fixes applied:
  - **BH-FDR added to `journal_impact` and `habit_impact`** (was missing, supplement/nutrition already had it). The frontend `passes_fdr` field is now populated for both — at ~57 journal questions ~3 false positives would otherwise have been reported at α=0.05.
  - **BH-FDR added to the causal layer (2026-05-27).** `causal_inference.py` was the last uncorrected test family — ~150 AIPW estimates per run at α=0.05 expected ~7-8 false-positive interventions by chance. The fix derives `p_raw` from the AIPW Wald `|ate/se_if|` vs N(0,1), pools binary + continuous treatments into one family of ~150, applies `multipletests(method='fdr_bh')` at `FDR_Q_THRESHOLD=0.05` (shared with `hrv_analysis`), and sets `p_raw`, `p_fdr_adjusted`, `passes_fdr` per result row. `meta_payload` records the correction policy. The descriptive `significant` (CI-excludes-zero) flag is preserved as a separate dimension. First validated run: 14 of 108 treatments survive FDR.
  - **SARIMAX fits on a contiguous daily index (2026-05-27).** Previously `train_sarimax` called `df.dropna(subset=[TARGET]).set_index('calendar_date')`, leaving a row-ordered series with gaps where HRV was missing — SARIMAX(1,1,1)×(1,0,1,7) then treated consecutive rows as consecutive days and biased AR(1) toward 0 (synthetic verification: dropna ar.L1=0.377 vs asfreq 0.494 vs truth 0.500). The fix reindexes to `pd.date_range(first_valid_hrv_day, last_valid_hrv_day, freq='D')` so the Kalman filter integrates over NaN gaps under `enforce_stationarity=False`/`enforce_invertibility=False`. The spine is **clamped to the observed HRV span** (not the full matrix min..max) so years of pre-monitoring NaN don't get injected. Per-horizon log is defensive: when `compute_metrics` returns `{}` (fewer than 3 valid prediction pairs) the horizon is logged as a (n_pairs, n_nan) skip and dropped from `sarimax_metrics`. Stage 3 OLS HAC at `~hrv_analysis.py:1903` cannot reindex (sm.OLS doesn't accept NaN) — the accepted compromise per the audit ticket: pass `cov_kwds={'maxlags': 7, 'use_correction': True}` (Andrews 1991 small-sample correction) and document in-comment that lag-7 here is "rows back" not "calendar days back".
  - **Phase-2 Spearman now excludes the trivial-autocorrelation HRV transforms** (`whoop_hrv_rmssd`, `whoop_recovery_score`, `hrv_lag*`, `hrv_*d_mean/std`, `hrv_z_28d`, `delta_hrv`, `hrv_vs_baseline`) — these were dominating the top-50 drivers chart with persistence rather than behavioral signal.
  - **Granger now uses BH-FDR survivors** as its input top-10 (was raw top-10 by |r|, inheriting multiple-comparison bias).
  - **`NUTRITION_COLS` extended** to match the causal layer's nutrition family (added `mfp_water_ml`, `mfp_exercise_kcal`, `net_calories`, `protein_pct`, `carb_pct`, `fat_pct`).
  - **Ordinal text columns now encoded** instead of silently dropped: `garmin_hrv.hrv_status` → `garmin_hrv_status_ord` (LOW=0, UNBALANCED=1, BALANCED=2); `garmin_training_status.training_readiness_level` → `garmin_training_readiness_level_ord` (POOR=0, LOW=1, MODERATE=2, HIGH=3). `training_readiness_score` (numeric) was referenced in `HIGH_VALUE_SPARSE_FEATURES` but not actually being fetched by the loader — fixed.
  - **Notion Journal mood/confidence/word_count promoted into the matrix** as `nj_mood_ord` (low/neutral/good/great → 0/1/2/3), `nj_confidence_ord` (low/medium/high → 0/1/2), `nj_word_count`, `nj_topic_count`, `nj_entry_count`. Uses `nj_` prefix NOT `journal_` to avoid being misread as a boolean WHOOP journal question by the Welch's t-test and causal binary-enumeration scans. Aggregated per-day via MAX mood (best of the day), MAX confidence, SUM word_count.
  - **HRV alignment test against the live matrix** at `tests/test_hrv_alignment_live.py` (replaced the synthetic `tests/test_phase2_alignment.py` 2026-11-10, which built a pre-aligned frame and never read the real matrix): row HRV = the WHOOP cycle dated to the row, day-D events fall between sleep D−1 and sleep D (real timestamps), re-keyed columns line up with the same row, every layer's outcome is row D's own HRV, no pipeline file shifts the target, alcohol canary. Runs in `hrv-retrain-on-backfill.yml` before the analysis (`ONYX_REQUIRE_LIVE=1`; a failure writes a failed `GUARD alignment_test:` heartbeat) and in `stats-pr-check.yml`; skips locally (no `.env`).
  - **Causal layer additions:** stress buckets (rest/low/medium), floors_ascended, whoop_cycle_avg/max_hr, lap_pace_cv/lap_hr_range, supplement_distinct_compounds/total_doses, eight_sleep_room_temp, and the three new nj_ ordinal columns added to `CONTINUOUS_TREATMENTS`. `_confounders_for` now logs a warning when declared confounders are missing from the matrix (previously silent). `MIN_CONTINUOUS_N=50` is now actually enforced (was declared but never referenced); `MIN_DISTINCT_DOSES` removed (descriptive dose-response stays in Phase 2 only — no causal dose-response estimator).
  - **Spotify daily-signature opt-in:** set `ONYX_INCLUDE_SPOTIFY=1` to include `sp_play_count`, `sp_total_minutes`, `sp_avg_valence`/`_energy`/`_tempo`/`_danceability`/etc. Audio-feature means are zeroed on days with `featurized_plays<5` so thin-sample days don't bias the average. Off by default because of Garmin offline-playback coverage gap (workout-heavy listening is invisible to the recently-played API).
  - **Out of scope (documented):** `pds.garmin_workouts` (planned workout templates — plans ≠ executions, not joined); raw minute-level jsonb (`raw_hrv_readings`, `raw_hr_values`, `raw_stress_values` — research roadmap item for custom RMSSD / intraday HRV).
- **Causal inference layer (Phase 2.5 of `hrv_analysis.py`).** Lives in `causal_inference.py` and runs after the descriptive stats. Treatments span every controllable variable across all data sources — see "Treatment coverage" subsection below. For each treatment the layer estimates the ATE on next-night HRV three ways:
  1. **Naive** — Welch's `mean(Y|T=1) − mean(Y|T=0)`. Same number the existing `journal_impact` / `supplement_impact` / `habit_impact` charts show; included as the unadjusted baseline.
  2. **Propensity Score Matching (PSM)** — 1:3 nearest-neighbor matching on logit propensity from a logistic model fit on the confounders, ATT estimated as the mean within-pair Y difference, CI by paired bootstrap (B=500). Common-support trimming at propensity ∈ [0.05, 0.95].
  3. **AIPW (doubly robust)** — logistic propensity + two Ridge outcome models (one per arm), combined via the AIPW influence function. **Cross-fitting uses blocked 5-fold (`_blocked_folds`: contiguous calendar blocks, training excludes `AIPW_FOLD_GAP=7` rows on each side of the test block, a fold needs `MIN_FOLD_ARM=10` days per arm) with overlap trimming** (days whose cross-fitted propensity is outside [0.05, 0.95] are dropped from the mean, not clipped). Never shuffled KFold (audit F-001, 2026-05-26: shuffled folds let the outcome model see a test day's HRV through the next day's `hrv_lag1`; the 7-row gap closes that). Never forward-only `TimeSeriesSplit` either (2026-11-11): with drifting treatment prevalence its tiny early folds were nearly one-armed, the saturated propensity clipped at 0.95 still weighted those days ×20, and the never-scored first sixth of rows was averaged in as zeros, which produced the ±50–155 ms AIPW rows (live "viewed a screen in bed" −130 ms vs naive +3 / PSM +6; same frame with blocked folds: +13). ATE = mean of per-row influence values. **Plausibility guard** (`plausibility_flags`): a row is `implausible` (with `implausible_reasons`) when |AIPW| > 1 SD of nightly HRV, when it is > 0.5 SD from both naive and PSM, when > 50% of days were trimmed, or when > 2 folds were skipped; implausible rows never pass FDR (`fdr_suppressed_by_plausibility`). Test: `tests/test_causal_aipw_synthetic.py` (known effects on a drifting, level-shifted synthetic frame; runs in `stats-pr-check.yml`). **Two CIs reported alongside each other**: (a) IF-based via `sd(ψ)/√n`, the familiar form consumers expect; (b) **block-bootstrap CI** (`_block_bootstrap_ci`, 7-day blocks × 1000 reps) which resamples contiguous time slices of ψ to preserve autocorrelation the IF SE assumes is i.i.d. The per-treatment `aipw_bb_width_ratio = width(bb_ci) / width(if_ci)` flags treatments where IF SE is too narrow; verification on 108 treatments (`audit/aipw_shift_findings.md`) found median ratio 1.13 with 6 treatments diverging >1.5× (`body_battery_drained`, `medium_stress_duration_min`, `journal_felt_recovered`, etc.) — for those, trust the BB CI. Unbiased if either the propensity or the outcome model is correct.

  **Confounder set** is pre-treatment lag-1 only (`hrv_lag1`, `hrv_7d_mean`, `whoop_day_strain_lag1`, `whoop_sleep_duration_milli_lag1`, `rolling_7d_training_load`, `sleep_debt_7d`, `day_of_week`, `is_weekend`). Supplement family additionally gets `journal_have_any_alcoholic_drinks_lag1` and `caffeine_total_mg_lag1` (unified caffeine log — the WHOOP caffeine checkbox is disregarded by policy, 2026-06-10) because supplement-conscious days tend to differ in adjacent lifestyle. **Same-night sleep/recovery/HRV-derived variables are DELIBERATELY EXCLUDED** — they are mediators on the very causal path being estimated; adjusting for them would block the effect (mediator-adjustment bias). The reported quantity is therefore the **total effect** (which includes the sleep-quality channel) — the actionable answer for "if I take magnesium tonight, what happens to my HRV tomorrow?" **Per-treatment exceptions live in one function, `adjustment_set()`** (2026-11-13; each result row stores the outcome as `confounders_used`): multi-day rolling treatments (`ROLLING_AGGREGATE_TREATMENTS`: 3-day / 7-day load, Garmin acute / chronic load and their ratio) drop `hrv_lag1`, `hrv_7d_mean`, `whoop_day_strain_lag1` (mediators inside the window), and a rolling treatment also drops an aggregate whose day window contains its own (`ROLLING_WINDOW_DAYS`: the 7-day total for the 3-day load; estimand in `ROLLING_TREATMENT_ESTIMAND`). `total_training_load` is one day's sum, so it is a daily treatment with the full set. Test: `tests/test_causal_adjustment_sets.py`.

  **Sensitivity** via the VanderWeele & Ding (2017) E-value — minimum strength on the risk-ratio scale that an unmeasured confounder would need (with both T and Y) to fully explain the estimate away. Continuous outcome → Cohen's d → RR via Chinn (2000): `RR ≈ exp(0.91·d)`, then `E = RR + √(RR·(RR−1))`. Higher = more robust to unmeasured confounding.

  **Cell-size gates:** treatments with fewer than 10 days in either arm are dropped entirely (recorded in `causal/dropped_low_n`); those with 10-19 in either arm are reported but flagged `low_n=true` (rendered with reduced opacity + ⚠ marker on the UI).

  **Storage:** five new rows in `pds.hrv_analysis_results` per run, all under `result_type='causal'`:
  - `binary_treatments` — list of binary-treatment results
  - `continuous_treatments` — list of median-split continuous-treatment results
  - `dag` — declared confounder sets + mediator exclusions + identifying assumptions (rendered as the DAG / Assumptions card on the UI for transparency)
  - `meta` — run metadata (estimator versions, bootstrap reps, fold counts, trim bounds)
  - `dropped_low_n` — treatments excluded for insufficient sample, with reason
