  **Frontend:** new "Causal Inference" section on `/analytics/hrv` (between the descriptive impact charts and the Prediction-vs-Actual section) with (a) an explanation card describing purpose + method, (b) a forest plot of binary AIPW ATEs with 95% CI error bars, (c) a naive-vs-adjusted comparison table sorted by absolute attenuation, (d) a continuous-treatment forest plot, and (e) a DAG / Assumptions card rendering the stored DAG payload. Empty until `python hrv_analysis.py` runs. Implausible rows (`lib/causal-plausibility.ts`, which re-derives the SD and disagreement rules for rows stored before the guard existed) are left out of both forest plots and listed under them with the reason; the table shows them greyed (⊘) after the ranked rows. Node test: `frontend/scripts/test-causal-plausibility.mjs`.

  **Why this is meaningfully different from the existing correlation / Welch / Granger machinery:** every other test in the pipeline answers *what's associated with* HRV. The causal layer answers *what would change HRV if intervened on*, by adjusting for the lifestyle clustering that confounds the naive comparison (e.g. alcohol nights co-occur with restaurant nights and weekend nights — naive Welch's blames alcohol for the whole pile).

  **Treatment coverage (which variables get a causal estimate):**
  - **Binary treatments** auto-enumerated from the matrix by prefix:
    - `journal_*` (every WHOOP journal yes/no question — ~50 columns when fully populated)
    - `habit_*` (Notion-managed habit completions, dynamic)
    - `supplement_*_amount` (every compound from `pds.supplement_intake_by_compound`, binarized to taken/not-taken — ~50 compounds; most flagged `low_n=true` until tracking history accumulates)
  - **Binary treatments** explicitly listed in `EXPLICIT_BINARY_TREATMENTS` (so they survive renames): `had_evening_workout`, `is_run_day`, `is_rest_day`, `negative_split`.
  - **Continuous treatments** (median-split to put them on the same scale as the binaries), declared in `CONTINUOUS_TREATMENTS`. Covers every daytime/behavioral variable from every data source:
    - *Nutrition (MFP):* calories, protein/carbs/fat (g + % of cals), fiber, sugar, sodium, water, exercise_kcal, net_calories
    - *Daytime strain / activity (WHOOP + Garmin):* WHOOP day_strain, WHOOP kilojoule, steps (Garmin `total_steps` and WHOOP `whoop_steps`), total_kcal, active_kcal, moderate/vigorous intensity minutes, highly_active/active/sedentary seconds
    - *Training load:* rolling 3d/7d, acute, chronic, ATL/CTL ratio, total_training_load. **Rest days are load 0, unworn days NaN** (2026-11-14): `total_training_load` is 0 on a day the Garmin watch was worn (the worn rule below, `garmin_worn_days` in `hrv_analysis.py`) with no activity, and stays NaN on a day it was not worn, so the load rows compare more vs less load with rest days included and "no sync" never reads as "rest". `rolling_3d/7d_training_load` sum it with `min_periods=1`: a window of worn rest days is 0, a window with no worn day is NaN (an unworn day inside an otherwise known window adds nothing). Acute / chronic load are Garmin's own values (`garmin_etl.training_load_from_status`, below). Test: `tests/test_garmin_training_load.py`.
    - *Stress (Garmin):* avg / max stress level, high-stress duration, % high stress, stress ratio
    - *Body Battery (Garmin):* charged, drained
    - *Workout timing:* minutes from last workout to bedtime, strain÷hours-to-bed
    - *Workout aggregates (Garmin):* activity_count, duration_min, distance_km, calories, max aerobic/anaerobic TE, max/avg activity HR, total elevation gain
    - *Workout aggregates (WHOOP):* workout_count, total/avg strain, total kJ, peak/avg workout HR, total time in zones 4-5 / zones 0-1
    - *HR zones (Garmin daily):* zone_2 through zone_5 seconds
    - *Recovery state:* days_since_alcohol, days_since_sauna, days_since_hard_workout, days_since_rest_day, consecutive_run_days
  - **DELIBERATELY excluded as treatments** (they are mediators or near-tautological outcomes — see the module's docstring for the full DAG argument): every same-night sleep variable (WHOOP `whoop_sleep_*`, Garmin `garmin_sleep_*`, Eight Sleep `eight_sleep_*`), every HRV-derived variable (`whoop_recovery_score`, `whoop_rhr`, `garmin_rhr`, `whoop_skin_temp`, `hrv_z_28d`), sleep timing recorded from the sleep itself (`bedtime_hour`, `wake_hour`, `sleep_midpoint_hour`), and body-composition (`weight_kg`, `bmi`) which changes too slowly for a daily ATE.
  - **`pds.garmin_workouts` (planned workout templates) is intentionally NOT joined into the HRV pipeline** — it stores scheduled-workout plans (interval targets, distances), not executions. The execution-side data (`garmin_activities` + laps) drives all training-load features. Plans without execution would be noise; executions without plans are still observed.
- `pds.hrv_predictions_latest` view — DISTINCT ON (prediction_date, model, horizon_days) returning freshest row per forecast. **Live (non-backtest) predictions win when both exist for the same date; backtest rows fall through as fallback** — necessary because the daily prediction job only writes ONE row per day (the forecast for tonight's sleep), and the ~57 historical h=1 rows from before the `*_v1` naming convention still carry `model_version='backtest_initial'` despite being genuine day-ahead forecasts. An earlier "WHERE model_version NOT LIKE 'backtest%'" filter hid all of them and emptied the Prediction-vs-Actual chart; the current view tiebreaks via `CASE WHEN model_version LIKE 'backtest%' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END` in `ORDER BY` instead. **All UI/analytics reads should go through the view**; the raw table accumulates multiple runs per day and generic fetches hit row limits fast. DDL in `sql/hrv_predictions_latest.sql`.
- `supabase-py` schema access: always use `supa.schema("pds").from_(table)` — NOT `supa.table()` which defaults to `public`
- `whoop_workouts` has no `cycle_id` column; use `workout_id` + derive `calendar_date` from `start_time` via ET-of-start (see TZ convention below)
- **Timezone convention** — governed by [ADR-0001 — Timezone & Behavioral-Day Handling](docs/adr/0001-timezone-and-behavioral-day-handling.md) ([Notion mirror](https://www.notion.so/36bbf5b44bf281c0895edb425358b458)). Three date types are carried per event:
  - `onyx_et_date` — clock day in `America/New_York` (canonical comparison key, MFP energy-balance semantics).
  - `onyx_behavioral_date` — bedtime-to-bedtime "day of Riley's life" via `(instant_local_tz − 6h)::date` (HRV pipeline, causal layer, behavior-affects-recovery analytics).
  - `onyx_local_date` — clock day in the TZ Riley was physically in (time-of-day features like `meal_last_hour`, `bedtime_hour`, travel-aware dashboard rendering).

  Every source table holding a TIMESTAMPTZ instant carries all three columns plus `onyx_tz_source` (provenance: `source_field | cycle_anchor | user_tz_log | gps_inferred | default_et_fallback`). Source date columns stay untouched (hard constraint). TZ detection ladder: WHOOP `timezone_offset` (free; already in DB) → Garmin activities offset (`start_time_local − start_time_gmt`) → cycle-anchor → `pds.user_tz_log` → ET fallback (flagged). **Fallback labels and date-only rows (`sql/tz_derivation_edge_cases.sql`, written 2026-11-14, applied only once Riley runs it):** `pds.tz_log_resolve(ts)` is the only code that writes `user_tz_log` / `default_et_fallback`; a trigger branch with only a date (no instant) calls `pds.derive_onyx_dates_for_day(date)` (three dates = the date, label = log coverage of that day), never a hand-rolled noon-ET probe. `garmin_hrv` stays on Garmin's wake-up `calendar_date` (`source_field`) because `hrv_alignment.py` does the one-day shift. Journal entries go through `pds.journal_entry_onyx_dates`; the migration has no backfill (journal_etl's hourly re-upsert re-derives journal rows), its rollback is `sql/rollback_tz_derivation_2026-11-16.sql`, `sql/tz_derivation_preapply_counts.sql` (run before applying, SELECT only) counts the stored rows the new rule would derive differently, and `sql/tz_derivation_postdeploy_checks.sql` runs right after (pass rules per check kind in its header); the WHOOP-journal and transition-flag parts are in `sql/tz_derivation_whoop_followups.sql` (not applied). Tests: `tests/test_tz_derivation_edge_cases.py`.

  **Matrix views** (ADR-0001):
  - **`pds.daily_health_matrix_behavioral`** — every source join keyed on `onyx_behavioral_date`. **This is the canonical view for HRV / causal / behavior-affects-recovery analytics.** WHOOP cycle deduplicated via `LATERAL ... LIMIT 1` (longest cycle per behavioral day) so transition-day "arrival nap + main cycle" doesn't double-count. `onyx_is_transition_day` aggregated via `bool_or` across all cycles on the day. Consumers: `hrv_analysis.py`, `causal_inference.py`, `/analytics/hrv`, `lib/queries.ts:getHealthMatrix`. DDL: `sql/adr_0001_08_daily_health_matrix_behavioral.sql`.
  - **`pds.daily_health_matrix`** (legacy clock-date view) — **DROPPED 2026-11-08** (`sql/drop_legacy_daily_health_matrix.sql`): no code readers, no DB dependents, and its WHOOP join mis-attributed travel days. Restore from `sql/deprecated/daily_health_matrix_live_2026-11-07.sql` if ever needed. Do not reintroduce clock-date matrix reads — use the behavioral view.
  - **`pds.daily_health_matrix_behavioral_mat`** — materialized copy of the behavioral view for **frontend page-load reads only** (perf, 2026-06-11; the live view's ~30-relation join cost 150-650 ms mean with 1.5-2.7 s tails via PostgREST). Refreshed every 30 min by pg_cron (`10,40 * * * *`; was every 15 min until 2026-11-11, cut to halve Disk IO — see `sql/matview_refresh_30min.sql`) through `pds.refresh_daily_health_matrix_behavioral_mat()` (a CONCURRENTLY refresh: readers never wait), which writes a `pds.sync_log` heartbeat (`matrix_mat`|`refresh`) on success AND failure and includes a pg_attribute **schema-drift tripwire** — surfaced as the "Matrix Matview" card on `/status`. **⚠ RULE: every migration that changes `pds.daily_health_matrix_behavioral` MUST drop + recreate the matview (+ unique index on `onyx_behavioral_date`, `calendar_date` index, anon/authenticated grants) in the same migration — the matview's `SELECT *` is frozen at creation. NEVER `DROP VIEW ... CASCADE` on the canonical view.** Frontend consumers: `getHealthMatrix`/`getNutrition`/`getCaffeineHrvPairs` (queries.ts), `getHistoricalHrv`/`getEnvDoseResponseData` (queries-hrv.ts), travel's `getHRVData`. The Python pipeline and the chat tools deliberately stay on the LIVE view. Full doc + recreate recipe: `sql/perf_matrix_matview.sql`.

  **`pds.user_tz_log`** — hand-maintained table of TZ transitions. Schema: `(effective_from TIMESTAMPTZ PK, tz TEXT IANA, notes TEXT)`. Insert one row per flight landing into a non-ET zone (and back). NY trips don't need entries (default). Read by `pds.tz_for_instant(ts)` which is used by every non-WHOOP / non-Garmin-activity trigger to derive local TZ.

  **`pds.tz_log_gaps`** — canary view comparing WHOOP `timezone_offset` to `pds.tz_for_instant`. Any disagreeing cycle = "Riley traveled but forgot to log it." `/status` page reads this and renders a yellow banner.

  **Behavioral-day rule** — for any instant `ts` with resolved local TZ:
  - `onyx_behavioral_date = ((ts AT TIME ZONE local_tz) − 6h)::date`
  - Generalizes the existing `whoop_journal.behaviors_date` trigger formula. Pre-midnight bedtimes attribute to today; post-midnight bedtimes (84.4% of nights for Riley) attribute back to yesterday.

  **Legacy view rules** (historical — applied in the dropped `pds.daily_health_matrix`; still relevant to `pds_legacy.daily_health_matrix_v0`):
  - **Point-in-time events** (workouts, weigh-ins): `(start_time AT TIME ZONE 'America/New_York')::date`
  - **WHOOP cycles** (bedtime-to-bedtime spans): `((start_time + INTERVAL '12 hours') AT TIME ZONE 'America/New_York')::date` — wake-day attribution.
  - **Garmin `start_time_local`**: stored as local wall-clock labeled as UTC (+00); `::date` yields ET date directly.
  - The `hrv_analysis.py` pipeline previously mirrored these rules via `to_date_str()` / `to_et_date_str()` / `to_cycle_et_date_str()` helpers; post-ADR-0001 it reads the behavioral view, so `calendar_date` in the loaded matrix IS `onyx_behavioral_date`.

  **Snapshot for A/B**: `pds_legacy.daily_health_matrix_v0` is a frozen materialization of the pre-ADR view (794 rows), retained for the Phase 2 sensitivity test and rollback if needed.

  **GPS-based TZ auto-population** (ADR-0001 Phase 4 step 4): `gps_tz_backfill.py` scans `pds.garmin_activities` for `(start_latitude, start_longitude)` and inserts new `pds.user_tz_log` rows whenever the GPS-inferred IANA TZ disagrees with what `pds.tz_for_instant` currently returns. Filters out same-UTC-offset cases (e.g. Toronto/Louisville share EDT with NY — geographically distinct but behaviorally identical). Runs automatically at the end of `garmin_etl.py` with `--since <earliest_date_this_run>` to keep the per-run cost ~5s. One-shot full-history sweep available via `python gps_tz_backfill.py --apply`.

  **WHOOP-cycle-based TZ auto-population** (ADR-0001 Phase 4 step 4 extension): `whoop_tz_backfill.py` is the companion path for trips where Garmin GPS doesn't fire (rest trip, no outdoor activity, watch off). Runs automatically at the end of `whoop_etl.py`. Every WHOOP cycle ships a `timezone_offset` (e.g. `-06:00`) — the script detects cycles whose offset disagrees with `pds.tz_for_instant`'s current resolution and inserts new `user_tz_log` rows (it reads the log once and looks each cycle up at its own instant, with the run's own proposals added as it goes; before 2026-11-14 it read the log only at the first cycle, so wide `--since` windows re-proposed trips the log already covered). Tests: `tests/test_whoop_tz_backfill.py`, `tests/test_tz_backfill_in_memory_current_tz.py`. Since WHOOP gives offset only (not IANA), the script picks the most-common IANA for that offset from Riley's MANUAL `user_tz_log` entries (history-derived), falling back to a generic offset → IANA map. Same-offset cases skip (no behavioral difference). The notes field tags every auto-insert (`whoop-auto: cycle <id> offset <off>; <provenance>; log was <prior_tz>`) so they're distinguishable from manual entries — and the history-derived defaults computed from ONLY manual entries to avoid bootstrap bias.

  **Resolution priority across the three TZ-population paths**: Manual > GPS-auto > WHOOP-auto. Manual entries are never overridden; the auto scripts only fill gaps. GPS gives exact IANA when available; WHOOP gives offset always. Together they cover essentially every realistic trip without manual maintenance — manual SQL is only needed if both devices are left at home.
- **Supplement intake — behavioral-day convention (NOT clock date).** A `pds.supplement_intake` row's `intake_date` should reflect the **day the intake belongs to behaviorally**, not the wall-clock date of the moment it was taken. A pre-bed supplement consumed at 12:05 AM ET — *before* the user has slept — belongs to the day that just ended, not the new clock date. Rationale (three converging reasons):
  1. **WHOOP cycle alignment.** WHOOP defines a "day" by bedtime-to-bedtime spans (see the `+12h` cycle rule above). The awake-tail period after midnight but before sleep is part of the *previous* cycle, not the new one. Treating supplements the same way keeps the journal × cycle × intake triple consistent.
  2. **HRV pipeline correctness.** The pipeline scores day-N behaviours against row N's own HRV — the sleep that closes behavioral day N (`hrv_alignment.py`). A pre-bed supplement affects the *immediately following* sleep, which is exactly row N's sleep, so the intake must be on row N. A naive clock-date attribution at 12:05 AM would put it on row N+1 and score it against the night after — silently mis-training the model.
  3. **Stack consistency.** An "evening stack" (mag, melatonin, etc.) that sometimes lands at 11:55 PM and sometimes at 12:05 AM should not split across two rows of `daily_health_matrix` based on a 10-minute clock crossing that has no biological meaning.

  **MyFitnessPal — log on behavioral day via manual backdate (decision 2026-05-25).** Earlier versions of this file said MFP follows "literal clock date" for energy-balance reasons. That was correct under one interpretation (clock-anchored calorie totals) but Riley's PRIMARY use of MFP is HRV/recovery analysis (mfp_* macro features feed `hrv_analysis.py`). For that use case, clock-date attribution creates the same awake-tail misalignment the ADR-0001 rewrite eliminated for every other source: a 1 AM heavy-fat meal would land on the wrong row vs. the HRV measuring its effect. Convention going forward:
  - **When logging a meal in MFP between 00:00 and ~5 AM ET that is genuinely pre-bed (your awake-tail), backdate it to yesterday in the MFP date picker.** The trip is "did I eat this meal before today's behavioral day ended?" — answer yes → log under yesterday.
  - The MFP daily-totals UI will look uneven on backdate days (yesterday shows ~2700 cal, today shows ~1800). That's the cost; the analytical correctness is the benefit.
  - The Onyx side requires no code change: the trigger sets `onyx_behavioral_date = calendar_date`, so behaviorally-logged MFP rows are correctly behavioral-attributed.
  - For pure energy-balance questions ("did I hit 180g protein on May 22?"), MFP's daily totals will reflect Riley's BEHAVIORAL day (May 22 = 10 AM May 22 → 1 AM May 23 clock-time), which is the right semantic for that question too (your fasting window starts at sleep, not at midnight).
  - For the temporal "when did I eat" question, `pds.meal_events` remains the source of truth (it has timestamps; MFP's standard CSV export does not).

  **Operational implications:**
  - `pds.supplement_intake.intake_date` is the canonical join key (behavioral day, ET).
  - `pds.supplement_intake.intake_time TIMESTAMPTZ` stores the truthful clock instant — kept independent of `intake_date` so a 12:05 AM May 21 click attributed to May 20 keeps the accurate timestamp without losing the date semantics.
  - Supplement logging (Log's Supplements section, chat, agent cards; `/supplements` itself no longer logs since 2026-11-15, see "One place to enter data"): **the behavioral attribution is automatic — log in real time.** Every logging path (`POST /api/supplements/log-intake`, `log-product`, and `logStackIntake`) defaults `intake_date` to `pds.behavioral_today_now()` — TZ-aware (travel, via `user_tz_log`) AND awake-tail-aware (−6h rule), so a 12:30 AM pre-bed dose lands on yesterday with zero manual action — while `intake_time` records the truthful clock instant. **Do NOT manually "correct" the pre-filled date to the clock date after midnight** — that breaks behavioral attribution and trips the caffeine layer's timestamp-trust quarantine. The manual date override exists for RETRO-logging only (remembered a dose from days ago); note retro-logged doses keep `intake_time` = "now", so they count toward mg totals but are excluded from timing aggregates by the trust rule. EditIntakeModal supports retroactive date adjustment for past intakes.
  - When ingesting historic supplement logs from any other source (Apple Health, manual CSV), the importer must apply the same rule.
- **HRV columns are not interchangeable across sources.** `whoop_recovery.hrv_rmssd_milli` is RMSSD in milliseconds, measured during the WHOOP-detected sleep cycle. `garmin_hrv.last_night_avg_ms` is Garmin's proprietary time-weighted average of 5-minute HRV samples during sleep — *not* RMSSD; the unit is ms but the algorithm is different. `eight_sleep_trends.avg_hrv` is undocumented by Eight Sleep. Treat each as its own variable; never average or substitute.
- **Environment Sweet Spot (`/analytics/hrv`, Environment section; fit + day inputs 2026-11-13).** Descriptive only (association, nothing adjusted for). `app/analytics/hrv/SweetSpotPanel.tsx` buckets nights by one input and shows the mean of one same-night outcome (WHOOP HRV / recovery / sleep efficiency / deep sleep) per bucket, ±1 SEM; all logic is pure in `lib/sweet-spot.ts` (test: `frontend/tests/sweet-spot.test.cjs`). Inputs: Pod room / bed temperature (Eight Sleep is wake-day keyed, so the temperature for row D's sleep is read from row D+1) and four day-D inputs read from the SAME row: `meal_last_meal_to_bedtime_min`, `nutrition_calories`, `caffeine_total_mg`, `caffeine_mg_at_bedtime`. Adding an input = one entry in `SWEET_SPOT_X` (its column joins the explicit select list `SWEET_SPOT_COLUMNS` automatically; a column that is not on the same row needs its pairing set). Thresholds: under 20 nights for the selected pair the chart is replaced by "Not enough nights yet"; the fitted curve (hand-rolled least-squares parabola on the nightly points, not the bars) is drawn from 30 nights; its peak is labelled only when the curve bends downward, the bend is at least 2 standard errors from zero, and the high point is strictly inside the observed range. `getEnvDoseResponseData()` (queries-hrv.ts) takes no arguments and returns compact short-key rows from the matview, so the ISR seed and the client revalidation cannot diverge and every toggle derives locally. **Pipeline version + adjusted view (2026-11-14):** `hrv_analysis.py` Phase 2.6 (`run_dose_response_continuous`, between the causal battery and the models, fail-open) computes every input x outcome pair on each retrain from the feature matrix (Eight Sleep already re-keyed by `al.realign_matrix`, outcome from `al.outcome_series`) and stores ONE row `pds.hrv_analysis_results` `dose_response_continuous / sweet_spot` (stored last and on its own; a failed block is stored as `status: "failed"`). Unadjusted numbers are the same arithmetic as `lib/sweet-spot.ts`; each pair also carries a confounder-ADJUSTED version: the causal layer's pre-treatment confounders for that input (`ci._confounders_for` + `ci.adjustment_set`, ffilled and day-of-week sin/cos exactly as `_prepare_treatment`), bars = least-squares bucket means at average confounders (regression with one intercept per bucket), curve = parabola fitted jointly with them; needs 50 nights with a complete confounder history. It is an adjusted association, not a causal estimate, and the panel says so. The page reads the row through the analysis batch (`loadSweetSpot` in queries-hrv.ts; `parseSweetSpotBaked` refuses another payload version, other thresholds, or an input built from another column / transform / bucket width) and reads the matview rows only when the row does not cover every pair (missing, failed, partial), computing those pairs in the browser as before; the "Adjusted" toggle is disabled with the reason when a pair has no adjusted version. Caffeine differs between the two sources: the feature matrix counts a logged-era day with no caffeine entry as 0 mg (zero-anchor), the matview leaves it empty (`pipelineCaveat`). Keep `DOSE_*` thresholds and `DOSE_RESPONSE_INPUTS` / `_OUTCOMES` equal to `SWEET_SPOT_*` / `SWEET_SPOT_X` / `SWEET_SPOT_Y`. Tests: `tests/test_dose_response_continuous.py` (synthetic), `frontend/tests/sweet-spot.test.cjs` (also checks the Python tables against the TypeScript), `frontend/scripts/test-hrv-analysis-batch.cjs` (18 panels).
- **Garmin sleep timestamps:** `garmin_sleep.sleep_start` / `sleep_end` are stored as true UTC instants (`sleepStartTimestampGMT` from the API). The previously-used `*Local` field encoded the local clock as UTC, shifting timestamps by ~4-5h.
- **Garmin empty days (2026-11-11):** Garmin returns a daily summary for every day, worn or not (goals, profile BMR, stress -1/-2, `sedentary_seconds` 86400), so `garmin_daily_summary` gets a row per day either way. "Worn" = steps > 0 or any heart-rate / body-battery / SpO2 / respiration value (`daily_summary_has_wearable_values` in `garmin_etl.py`, `GARMIN_WEARABLE_FILTER` in `lib/status-freshness.ts`; keep the two in step). A run whose fetched days are all unworn (no worn summary, no sleep / HRV / activity rows) logs a `partial` heartbeat whose message starts `No wearable data`, and the `/status` Garmin card keys freshness on the newest worn day, so an unworn watch shows red after 3 days instead of green. Test: `tests/test_garmin_empty_days.py`.
- **Garmin acute / chronic training load (2026-11-14):** `pds.garmin_training_status.acute_training_load` / `chronic_training_load` come from the training-STATUS endpoint (`garmin.get_training_status`, `mostRecentTrainingStatus.latestTrainingStatusData.<device>.acuteTrainingLoadDTO.dailyTrainingLoadAcute / dailyTrainingLoadChronic`), not the readiness payload the rest of the row (and `raw_json`) comes from. Only a device record whose `calendarDate` is the synced day counts: the endpoint reports the most recent status as of the date, so for a day the watch did not sync it can return an earlier day's record, which is treated as no data. A missing endpoint's columns are left out of the upsert, never sent as NULL. Before this both columns were NULL in every row (656 on 2026-11-14). History: `python garmin_etl.py --backfill-training-status 930` (two calls per day, writes a `garmin|training_status_backfill` heartbeat). The analysis keeps one copy of each (`acute_training_load`, `chronic_training_load`; the view's `garmin_` copies are dropped at the join). Test: `tests/test_garmin_training_load.py`.
- **Eight Sleep bed / room temperature self-heals (2026-11-12, hardened 2026-11-13).** Eight Sleep's `/intervals` collection returns only the 10 most recent sessions, so after a 10+ day pause the gap nights have no `median_bed_temp` / `median_room_temp`. After every normal sync `eight_sleep_etl.py:self_heal_temps` reads `eight_sleep_trends` rows of the last 60 days (today excluded) with a NULL temp and fills them from `/intervals/{mainSessionId}`: at most 20 lookups per run (`--selfheal-max`, 0 = off), one lookup per session even when both bed sides share it, only NULL columns are written, and when more sessions are waiting than the cap allows the starting point rotates daily. The sync itself never sends a NULL temp for a night it has no interval for (`_split_rows_by_temp`), so a wide `--days` catch-up cannot erase stored temps. Heartbeats: `eight_sleep|temp_selfheal` on every run (`records_synced` = rows healed, 0 = nothing to heal); a failed lookup or scan never fails the sync but turns both that row and the `eight_sleep|trends` row (the `/status` card) `partial` with the counts. Older gap: `gh workflow run eight-sleep-etl.yml -f selfheal_days=N`; `eight_sleep_temp_backfill.py [--apply]` is the same function with no look-back bound. Test: `tests/test_eight_sleep_selfheal.py`. **Dates (2026-11-16, `docs/eight-sleep-date-contract.md`):** `calendar_date` is the trends API `day` unchanged, the wake-up date in the stationary bed's zone (`EIGHTSLEEP_TIMEZONE`; all three `onyx_*` dates are copies of it, never re-dated in SQL); interval values attach to a day by its `mainSessionId` (else its only session; a day with several sessions and no main id gets none and keeps its stored temps), never by their own timestamp, no row is written without a trend day, an `/intervals` item without an id, or a run where no interval pairs although sessions fall in its window, turns `partial`, and a session reporting a zone with another UTC offset turns the run `partial` (`GUARD eight_sleep_bed_tz:`). `eight_sleep_temp_backfill.py --compare` is the read-only, counts-only check of stored temps against each row's main session (whole table, paged; `--max-lookups N` raises the 1000-lookup cap, since an identical re-run repeats the same lookups; history is clean only when `clean` is true: complete, `differ = 0` and no unverifiable row; it needs the Supabase and Eight Sleep credentials in the shell, which the PC does not hold (no `.env`), and never runs from Actions, whose log is public; keep its output private). The self-heal logs counts only, never a date, a temperature or a session id, reports a failed scan, lookup or crash by its type and HTTP status or database code only (`_safe_error`, never the error text), and turns the HTTP client loggers down while it runs (`quiet_http_logs`: httpx logs every request URL at INFO, and the heal's URLs carry the ids and the healed row's date). Test: `tests/test_eight_sleep_date_contract.py`.
- **WHOOP daily steps (2026-11-13, `sql/whoop_cycles_step_count.sql`).** WHOOP's v2 API returns `step_count` on every cycle (top level of the cycle, not inside `score`; null, never 0, when there is no step data), so `whoop_etl.py` reads cycles from `/v2/cycle` (same integer cycle id and score fields as `/v1/cycle`; profile and body measurement also moved to `/v2`, with a `/v1` fallback on 404/410) and stores it in the nullable `pds.whoop_cycles.step_count`. It exists because the Garmin watch is rarely worn (last on 2026-10-02) while the strap is worn daily. History was filled on 2026-11-13: all 740 cycles back to 2024-10-27 carry a count. **Which day: a cycle runs from one sleep onset to the next, so its steps are the waking day after that onset, dated from the cycle's OWN start** (`hrv_alignment.cycle_waking_day`, mirrored in `lib/steps.ts:whoopStepsDay`): the −6 h behavioral-day rule applied 12 h after the onset in the cycle's local time (`start_time` + `timezone_offset`, ET when missing), i.e. the date of local start + 6 h. For a night-time onset that is `onyx_behavioral_date` + 1, like strain and kilojoule; an onset between 06:00 and 18:00 local counts its own clock day. Cycles landing on the same day are summed. Do not use "main cycle per behavioral date + 1": when an onset is after 06:00 two cycles share a behavioral date, and longest-wins put the wrong cycle's count on the wrong day (review 2026-11-14; live: 7 such dates, 1 wrong value and 7 dropped days, all fixed by the own-start rule, which puts 0 cycles on a shared day and keeps all 739 closed cycles). Checked on live data: same clock day vs Garmin r = 0.949 over 507 days; the earlier offset scan (cycle dated D vs Garmin day D + 1 r = 0.94, every other offset from D − 2 to D + 3 between −0.11 and 0.22; WHOOP running distance r = 0.75 at D + 1, under 0.14 elsewhere) still holds. The open cycle holds the steps so far and is overwritten hourly until it closes. **Page:** the Steps tile and Daily Steps chart on `/activities` show, per day, the LARGER of the Garmin and WHOOP counts (`lib/steps.ts:mergeDailySteps`; WHOOP on a tie), because the watch is often worn for part of a day (Garmin under half of WHOOP on 112 of 505 shared days); one device per day, never summed; each bar, the tile and the chart badge name the device; a day that includes the open cycle is marked "so far". Display only: the HRV pipeline uses WHOOP alone. `step_count` is in `WHOOP_CYCLE_COLS`, and the chat's `query_whoop_cycles` rows carry `steps_day`. No new `/status` card: it rides on the WHOOP card's hourly `whoop|full_sync` heartbeat. **HRV pipeline:** the matrix view is NOT changed; `hrv_analysis.merge_whoop_steps_features` merges the column in pandas as `whoop_steps` (each closed cycle dated by `hrv_alignment.cycle_waking_day`, cycles on one day summed; a day with an open cycle or a null count stays NaN, nothing is zero-filled; loaded in its own fail-open read). It is a separate column from Garmin's `total_steps` (two devices, never blended), is in `NOT_FORECAST_PREFIXES` (today's cycle is still open when the evening forecast runs) and is THE steps `CONTINUOUS_TREATMENTS` median split (family behavior). Garmin `total_steps` was dropped as a causal treatment on 2026-11-14 (one signal per FDR family, like the caffeine rule; the watch's partial-wear days make it the weaker measurement) and stays a matrix column for Spearman, the forecast and the alignment guard. The runtime guard (`al.check_rekey_directions`) and `tests/test_hrv_alignment_live.py` fail if WHOOP steps ever line up with Garmin steps on an adjacent row. **Deep re-reads:** `gh workflow run daily-etl.yml -f days=800 -f whoop_cycles_only=true` runs `whoop_etl.py --cycles-only` (cycles endpoint only, Garmin job skipped, heartbeat `whoop|cycles_backfill`; a failed cycles read or profile check writes a `failed` row there and exits 1). Use it instead of a large plain `days`: that also re-reads years of Garmin days and runs `whoop_tz_backfill` over the whole window, which on 2026-11-13 proposed 21 new `user_tz_log` rows for old trips the log already covers (dry run; nothing was inserted). Fixed 2026-11-14: the pass now checks each cycle against the log at its own instant, and a full-history check finds 0 cycles to propose. Tests: `tests/test_whoop_steps.py`, `frontend/tests/steps.test.cjs`.
- **Spotify tables are isolated from health data by design.** `spotify_plays` + `spotify_tracks` are NOT joined into `daily_health_matrix`. Listening behavior stands on its own; any health correlation happens at view/query time only. `spotify_daily_signature` is a per-ET-date aggregate view (play counts, unique tracks/artists, mean audio features) — frontend reads go through it where possible. PK on `spotify_plays` is `(played_at, track_id)` for idempotent upserts. `played_date_et` is a stored generated column matching the ET-canonical TZ convention.
- **Spotify audio features come from ReccoBeats, not Spotify.** Spotify deprecated `/v1/audio-features` for apps registered after 2024-11-27 (this app is post-cutoff). `spotify_tracks.features_source` records provenance (`'reccobeats'` or null when unresolved). The `spotify_daily_signature` view only computes feature means over plays with non-null valence so partial coverage doesn't bias the signal.
- **Spotify OAuth scope is `user-read-recently-played playlist-modify-private`** — both ingestion (ETL) and write (playlist creation from chat or `/spotify` button) use the same refresh token in `pds.ci_tokens`. If the scope changes, re-run `python spotify_etl.py --auth` then `python ci_token_helper.py upload spotify`; old refresh tokens still work but only carry their original scope claim. The Next.js client (`lib/spotify-server.ts`) refreshes on every call and writes the blob back to `ci_tokens` so the Python ETL stays in sync; that write-back is newer-wins (next bullet).
- **Spotify sign-in renewal: Reconnect on the `/status` Spotify card (2026-11-13).** The refresh token stops working 180 days after the sign-in (see Token recovery (Spotify) under GitHub Actions ETL). The Spotify card shows a **Reconnect** control (`components/SpotifyReconnect.tsx`) when its message means "sign in again" (`needsSpotifyReconnect` in `lib/spotify-reconnect.ts`: refresh token rejected / expired / revoked, `invalid_grant`, "sign-in expires", no token) and from **day 165** after the sign-in, when a healthy card also reads Degraded and shows "Reconnect by <date>". Flow: (1) `GET /api/spotify/auth-url` returns Spotify's authorize link and sets a random state in an httpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Strict cookie (Path `/api/spotify`, 10 minutes); the card shows the link, which opens in a new tab. (2) Spotify redirects that tab to `http://127.0.0.1:8888/callback?code=…&state=…`; nothing listens there, so the tab shows a page that fails to load, which is expected. (3) The address of that tab is pasted into the card → `POST /api/spotify/exchange` (`{callback}`; the full address, the query alone or the bare code; the address and the query must carry their state) checks same-origin + JSON + a 4 KB cap, compares the pasted state with the cookie (constant-time; the cookie is cleared after one attempt, except when Spotify's token endpoint did not answer, so Finish can be pressed again; only a bare code carries no state and is accepted on the cookie being present), exchanges the code server-side, requires a refresh token and both scopes, makes ONE `recently-played` call with the new token and only then stores the blob. A wrong account, a missing scope or a failed check leaves the stored token untouched. **Both routes are owner-session only: never add `/api/spotify/*` to `isCiRoute()`** (they mint and store OAuth credentials, and the CI token is held by several devices). The redirect address is ONE constant (`SPOTIFY_REDIRECT_URI` in `lib/spotify-reconnect.ts`, the address `spotify_etl.py --auth` has always used) for both the link and the exchange; never read it from env on Vercel. Answers carry a fixed message by code (`RECONNECT_ERRORS`) and never a token, the client secret, the code or Spotify's own response. **Token blob:** `pds.ci_tokens.token_data` is TEXT holding pretty JSON (indent 2) with `access_token, token_type, expires_in, refresh_token, scope, obtained_at` (epoch s of the last refresh) plus **`authorized_at`** (epoch s of the sign-in; absent on tokens from before 2026-11-13). Every sign-in route builds exactly this blob, in this key order, and refuses a sign-in that did not grant both scopes: Reconnect (`buildTokenBlob`) and `--auth` / the workflow's `auth_code` input (`token_blob_from_response` in `spotify_etl.py`); `tests/test_ci_token_spotify_guard.py` checks the two agree. Every refresh path keeps `authorized_at`; `/api/status` reads it server-side and exposes ONLY the derived date (`reconnectBy`: the ET calendar day of expiry minus one day; the `spotify|plays` heartbeat names the same day), because that response is CDN-cacheable. **Newer-wins writes:** a later `authorized_at` always wins, then the later `obtained_at` so a run or a site call that loaded the old sign-in's token before a reconnect never writes it back over the new one; within one sign-in the site's write-back also skips the write when the stored refresh token was rotated elsewhere and Spotify returned no new one. **Both refresh writers (the site's write-back and the workflow's upload) write only through `pds.save_spotify_token`** (`sql/spotify_token_atomic_save.sql`, applied 2026-11-14), which locks the row, compares and writes in one transaction and answers an outcome code (`written` / `same` / `older_signin` / `older_refresh` / `rotated` / `invalid`), never a token; a read-then-upsert in code let a reconnect landing between the two be overwritten (Codex review #168). `isNewerBlob` / `shouldWriteBack` and `spotify_upload_decision` are the offline models the tests use; never write the Spotify row from a refresh path with a plain upsert. The Reconnect store stays a plain upsert (it is the new sign-in; its write takes the same row lock). After a reconnect the card stays red until the next hourly run; `gh workflow run spotify-etl.yml` confirms at once. Needs `SPOTIFY_CLIENT_ID` / `SPOTIFY_CLIENT_SECRET` in Vercel (the same Spotify app as the GitHub secrets). Tests: `frontend/tests/spotify-reconnect.test.cjs`, `tests/test_ci_token_spotify_guard.py`.
- **Spotify Feb 2026 API migration** affects this codebase. Use the post-migration endpoints in `lib/spotify-server.ts`: `POST /me/playlists` (NOT the removed `POST /users/{user_id}/playlists`) for create, and `POST /playlists/{id}/items` (NOT the removed `/tracks`) for add. Symptom of using the old endpoints is a bare `403 {"error":{"status":403,"message":"Forbidden"}}` with no scope hint. Migration guide: https://developer.spotify.com/documentation/web-api/tutorials/february-2026-migration-guide. Development Mode apps additionally require the app owner to have an active Spotify Premium subscription. **Batch GET endpoints (`/v1/artists`, `/v1/tracks`, `/v1/albums`, etc.) were also removed** — same bare-403 symptom; replacement is per-id `/v1/artists/{id}` etc. `spotify_etl.py:SpotifyClient.artists()` already does this with a 50ms sleep between calls.
- **`pds.spotify_playlists`** logs every playlist Onyx creates (one row per `playlist_id`) with `track_ids` JSONB, `created_via` (`'chat'` | `'button'` | `'builder'`), and the originating `prompt` if from chat or builder. Audit + UI history. Not joined to other tables.
- **Playlist generation has three entry points**, all share `lib/spotify-server.ts:createPlaylist`:
  1. `/api/spotify/create-playlist` — one-click "Create from top tracks" button on `/spotify`, no LLM (passes a known track list). `created_via='button'`.
  2. `/api/chat` — general-purpose chat with 3 Spotify tools mixed in alongside health tools. `created_via='chat'`.
  3. `/api/spotify/generate-playlist` — **dedicated SSE-streamed endpoint** behind the "Generate playlist" modal in the `/spotify` page header. Free-text prompt + structured controls (source_pool, vibes[], era, genres[]). Runs a focused agentic loop with the same 3 Spotify tools, but **gates which tools the agent sees by `source_pool`** (`history` → only `query_spotify_tracks_by_features`; `discovery` → only `search_spotify_catalog`; `mix` → both) so the mode can't drift. Streams `status` / `tool_use` / `tool_result` / `message` / `done` / `error` events. `created_via='builder'`.
- **`pds.spotify_artists`** is a dim table for artist enrichment (genres, images). Spotify's Dev Mode `GET /v1/artists/{id}` post-Feb 2026 strips `genres`, `popularity`, and `followers` from the response — only `id/name/images/href/uri` come back. So genres come from **MusicBrainz** (no API key, 1 req/sec, polite User-Agent), looked up by artist name; top match's tags (sorted by user-vote count, top 8) populate `genres` as a JSONB array. Hit rate during initial backfill: 49/51 artists matched. Two artists missed via name-format issues (`JAŸ-Z` diacritic, `¥$` collab project). Refresh empty rows with `python spotify_etl.py --refresh-genres`. Initial seed for existing artists: `python spotify_etl.py --backfill-artists` (Spotify enrich) then `--refresh-genres` (MusicBrainz tags). The regular ETL handles both for new artists. Genre tags are crowdsourced — expect some noise like "british", "2020s", "favorites" mixed in with real genres.
- **`pds.meal_events` captures clock-time meal events for HRV timing analysis.** Deliberately separate from `pds.myfitnesspal_nutrition`: MFP carries macros at daily-totals grain with **no timestamps** (the CSV export drops per-meal times); `meal_events` carries clock-instant grain with **no macros**. The two are joined at view-time. Fact table: `event_id BIGSERIAL`, `event_date DATE NOT NULL` (behavioral-day, ET — same convention as `supplement_intake`; a 12:05 AM pre-bed meal is attributed to the previous day), `event_time TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL` (truthful clock instant), `kind TEXT DEFAULT 'last_meal'` (extensible: last_meal | first_meal | snack | other), `notes`, plus auto-touched `created_at` / `updated_at`. View `pds.meal_timing_daily` aggregates one row per ET date with `last_meal_time`, `last_meal_hour` (ET decimal hour 0-23.99, e.g. 19.75 = 7:45 PM), `first_meal_hour`, `eating_window_hours`, `meal_event_count`, `last_meal_kind`, plus the **bedtime-anchored `last_meal_to_bedtime_minutes`** — computed as `whoop_sleep.start_time − last_meal_time` against the WHOOP cycle that closes the behavioral day (the cycle whose `onyx_behavioral_date` is that day). The bedtime-anchored metric is what the HRV pipeline reads because it's monotonic in physiological lateness: a 1:30 AM meal + 1:35 AM bedtime resolves to 5 minutes, whereas the raw `last_meal_hour` would invert at midnight (0.083 < 19.75) and confuse any model using it directly. **The manual `/nutrition` meal-event quick-log UI (+ read-only timing display + edit modal) was REMOVED 2026-05-31** — Cronometer Gold per-entry timestamps now drive meal-timing via `pds.meal_timing_from_cronometer` (COALESCE'd ahead of `meal_timing_daily` in `daily_health_matrix_behavioral`). The `meal_events` table, the `/api/meals` route (GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE), and `pds.meal_timing_daily` are retained as the pre-Gold historical DB fallback and for any future programmatic logging; the post-midnight behavioral-day convention (logging 00:00–04:00 ET → attribute to yesterday) still applies to any row written. `/nutrition` itself was renamed from "Nutrition / Meal Timing" to just "Nutrition" (the standalone `/meals` page still 301-redirects there). The view is now joined into `daily_health_matrix` as `meal_last_hour`, `meal_first_hour`, `meal_eating_window_hours`, `meal_event_count`, and `meal_last_meal_to_bedtime_min`; `meal_` is in `CONTROLLABLE_FEATURE_PREFIXES` (SHAP-actionable) and `meal_last_meal_to_bedtime_min` + siblings are in `CONTINUOUS_TREATMENTS` in `causal_inference.py` (nutrition family — gets the lifestyle-clustering confounder set). Spearman + SHAP coverage activates automatically once ~18 days of logs clear the 5% non-null filter; AIPW causal estimates require ≥10 days in each median-split arm (and surface with `low_n=true` until 20). Schema in `meal_schema.sql`.
- **Unified caffeine layer (2026-06-09): `pds.caffeine_events` + `pds.caffeine_timing_daily` merge BOTH caffeine channels** — supplements (`pds.supplement_intake` × caffeine-bearing ingredients: FDA UNII `3G6A5W338E` or `ingredient_group`/`name ILIKE '%caffeine%'`, mg = quantity × doses, unit-normalized) and dietary (`pds.cronometer_servings` rows with `caffeine_mg > 0` — coffee/tea/chocolate, with Cronometer Gold per-entry timestamps). Both key on the behavioral day. `caffeine_events` is the per-event grain; `caffeine_timing_daily` aggregates one row per behavioral day: timing (`first/last_caffeine_hour` ET, `caffeine_window_hours`, bedtime-anchored `last_caffeine_to_bedtime_minutes` via the main WHOOP cycle closing the day — `whoop_main_cycle_per_behavioral_date`, dedup-safe, travel-robust) + quantity (`total_caffeine_mg` / `dietary_caffeine_mg` / `supplement_caffeine_mg`) + `caffeine_mg_at_bedtime` (5h half-life decay residual at sleep onset). **Timestamp trust rule:** an event's clock instant only feeds timing aggregates when its own behavioral attribution (`((event_time AT local tz) − 6h)::date` via `pds.tz_for_instant`) matches the claimed behavioral day — quarantines retro-logs (the `/supplements` date-override keeps `intake_time` = "now", so backdated doses carry next-day timestamps that previously produced negative bedtime gaps). Untrusted events still count toward mg totals and `caffeine_intake_count` (which counts ALL events, both channels). `caffeine_mg_at_bedtime` is NULL unless EVERY event that day has a trusted timestamp (dose-weighted — partial timing would silently under-count). Matrix: the five legacy `caffeine_*` columns in `pds.daily_health_matrix_behavioral` picked up unified semantics in place, and `caffeine_total_mg`/`caffeine_dietary_mg`/`caffeine_supplement_mg`/`caffeine_mg_at_bedtime` were appended (migration `daily_health_matrix_behavioral_caffeine_unified`, doc in `sql/caffeine_matrix_columns.sql`). **Pipeline contract: `caffeine_total_mg` is THE canonical caffeine quantity** — it replaced the dietary-only `nutrition_caffeine_mg` in `NUTRITION_COLS` and `CONTINUOUS_TREATMENTS` (`nutrition_caffeine_mg` stays in the view as archive but is no longer tested — two copies of one signal would double-dip the FDR family). **The WHOOP journal "Consumed caffeine?" checkbox is DISREGARDED by policy (Riley, 2026-06-10)** — it proved unreliable (two "No" nights carried 800-1000 mg of logged caffeine). `hrv_analysis.py` drops `journal_consumed_caffeine` right after the journal merge so it reaches no test family; the zero-anchor is log-based: in-era days with no caffeine event AND other logging activity (any supplement dose, or a Cronometer nutrition entry) anchor to 0 mg — the activity gate keeps lapsed-logging stretches from manufacturing fake zeros. `strain_x_caffeine` and the supplement-family causal confounder are repointed to the unified signal. Also derives `caffeine_total_mg_lag1`. `caffeine_mg_at_bedtime` (+ `caffeine_total_mg`) are `CONTINUOUS_TREATMENTS` family `nutrition`. Frontend: **`/caffeine` page** (KPI tiles, stacked dietary/supplement mg trend with FDA 400 mg reference, caffeine-window range chart, bedtime-gap trend with 6h reference, caffeine × next-night-HRV scatter + late-vs-early comparison) reads `caffeine_timing_daily` + matrix via `getCaffeineDaily`/`getCaffeineHrvPairs` in `lib/queries.ts`. Matrix-row semantics: row N's `whoop_hrv_rmssd` IS the HRV of the sleep that follows row N's caffeine (main cycle starting at the bedtime that closes day N); the pipeline scores them on the same row. **Double-log hazard (verified live 2026-06-09):** caffeinated drink mixes exist in BOTH channels — G1M Sport is a supplement product (`custom_e2310b755bd0`, 150 mg) AND a Cronometer food; logging the same scoop in both on one day would silently double-count 150 mg. Convention: **caffeinated drink mixes → Cronometer only** (they carry carbs/electrolytes that belong in the nutrition ledger anyway); the supplement channel is for pills/pre-workout that Cronometer never sees. The zero-anchor stays era-gated (never before the first logged caffeine event, ~2026-05-19) — a 2024-25 zero arm would alias caffeine dose onto secular HRV drift (period confound). **QA surface: `pds.caffeine_data_quality`** (`sql/caffeine_data_quality.sql`) — long-format flags per behavioral day (`untrusted_timestamps`, `possible_double_log` cross-channel name-match heuristic; the journal-comparison flags were removed with the 2026-06-10 policy), rendered as the Data Quality card on `/caffeine`. **Backdated `/supplements` logs no longer stamp "now"** (fixed 2026-06-09 — the root cause of the retro-log artifacts): with no explicit time override, a past-dated intake writes `intake_time = NULL` ("time unspecified"); `lib/supplement-stacks.ts:logStackIntake` distinguishes explicit-null (preserve) from undefined (default to now) so stack backdating behaves the same. The pipeline also derives `caffeine_total_mg_7d_mean` (feature-only — deliberately NOT a causal treatment until the F-003 rolling-aggregate mediator handling applies and in-era history suffices). Schema in `sql/caffeine_timing_daily.sql`.
- **`/nutrition` meal quick-log exposes both `last_meal` and `first_meal` kinds.** A two-pill row (Kind: last meal / first meal) above the time selector toggles `mealKind` in component state and is sent as `kind` in the `POST /api/meals` body. Defaults to `last_meal` to preserve the prior single-kind UX; the button label flips to "Log first meal" when first meal is selected. The behavioral-day attribution logic (post-midnight auto-yesterday) applies identically to both kinds — a 6 AM first meal that's the day's start lands on today, a 12:30 AM pre-bed last meal lands on yesterday, as before. `first_meal` events feed `meal_first_hour` and `meal_eating_window_hours` in the matrix, both of which were already wired through the HRV pipeline (Spearman / SHAP / causal) and just lacked an ergonomic logging surface.
- **`pds.weight_log` stores daily body weight — Tanita scale ETL is the primary path (flipped 2026-06-09; was manual-only), manual `/nutrition` entry is the fallback/override.** One row per ET date (PK on `log_date`); `weight_kg NUMERIC(6,3) NOT NULL` is the canonical storage unit (matches `whoop_body_measurements.weight_kilogram`). Frontend accepts + displays pounds via `kgToLb`/`lbToKg` helpers. **`source TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'manual'`** (`'manual'` | `'tanita'`, no CHECK so future scales don't need a migration): `tanita_etl.py` rolls up the **earliest measurement per ET day** (morning weigh-in convention) with `source='tanita'`, and **never overwrites rows where `source != 'tanita'`** — manual entries take precedence. The check is made by the database write itself (2026-11-16, audit PIPE-05): the rollup sends one `{log_date, weight_kg, logged_at}` row per day to `pds.upsert_tanita_weight_days` (`sql/weight_log_tanita_rollup.sql`, service-role only), whose single `INSERT … ON CONFLICT … WHERE source = 'tanita'` keeps a manual save made during the run and skips unchanged days; it answers inserted / updated / unchanged / skipped_manual counts (logged as counts only), and there is no plain-upsert fallback while the function is missing (the run fails naming the file; raw measurements are kept). Test: `tests/test_tanita_weight_rollup.py`. A manual POST re-log of a tanita-owned day explicitly sends `source='manual'` (flipping ownership so the ETL stops touching that day; explicit because the upsert UPDATE branch doesn't apply column defaults). PATCH deliberately leaves `source` untouched. **DELETE on a tanita-owned day doesn't stick** — the measurement still exists upstream, so the next ETL run re-inserts it; the override path for a bogus scale reading (guest on the scale) is manually re-logging the correct weight for that date, which flips ownership. API at `/api/weight` (GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE — all selects include `source`). UI: "Body Weight" section on `/nutrition` with KPI tiles (the Latest tile shows a TANITA badge when scale-sourced), quick-log card, trend chart (ChartCard `source` shows TANITA / TANITA + MANUAL), and recent entries with per-row TANITA pills. **Not joined into `daily_health_matrix` today** — weight changes too slowly for the daily-ATE causal layer (already excluded in `causal_inference.py`); it's a standalone trend store. If we later want weight as a slow-moving covariate (e.g. body comp regressions), join via `LEFT JOIN pds.weight_log wl ON gds.calendar_date = wl.log_date`. Schema in `weight_schema.sql`.
- **Flights — `pds.flights` from Delta confirmation emails (`flights_email_etl.py`, 2026-11-08).** Delta has **no public API for a traveler's own bookings** (developer portal is partner-only; TripIt's public API closed to new integrations Feb 2026) and its emails carry **no schema.org FlightReservation markup**, so the ETL is a **text parser over IMAP**. It reads **BOTH Gmail accounts** (rileybleeklm@ via `IMAP_EMAIL`/`IMAP_APP_PASSWORD`, rileybleeker@ via `IMAP_EMAIL_2`/`IMAP_APP_PASSWORD_2`; `_3.._5` accepted) read-only (`BODY.PEEK`, Gmail's `\All` folder so archived mail counts — inbox-triage archives the Updates category) and handles five operational email kinds from `DeltaAirLines@t.delta.com`/`@g.delta.com`: **receipt** ("Your Flight Receipt - NAME DDMONYY") + **award_receipt** ("Congrats On Your SkyMiles Award Trip") = the full itinerary (flight, cabin, CITY names, local times, ticket #; IATA codes come from the baggage block's "Sun 01 Nov 2026 / ATL-YYZ" row per direction, else the `CITY_TO_IATA` map); **trip_details** ("Your XXX > YYY Trip Details", ~3 days before each direction, carries IATA codes + seat; TWO templates — the Oct-2026+ "Flight 1 of N" layout and the older `DEPARTURE / YYZ / "6:30 PM Thu, May 07 DL2714" / DESTINATION / ATL / "8:55 PM …"` event layout, auto-detected); **checkin** ("It's Time To Check In For Your Flight", day before; one `TRIP ONE/TWO` block per direction, each with its own date, flight as `DL 2714` / regional `9E 4958` (→ normalized to the DL-marketed number) / bare `904`, cities only → IATA via `CITY_TO_IATA` + a **learned map** from rows receipts/trip-details already labeled, and it may CREATE a row when both airports resolve — needed because corporate/Concur bookings never produce a receipt in Gmail); **schedule_change** (marks the PNR's segments `status='changed'`; the later trip_details/checkin emails carry the corrected times and flip them back); **cancellation/refund** (carries only the 13-digit TICKET number → every segment on that ticket → `cancelled`). Promotional mail (`@o.delta.com`) is ignored. **One row per flight SEGMENT**, natural key `(confirmation_number, flight_number, dep_local_date)` — every email kind carries those three, so receipt → trip_details → checkin converge on one row; **latest email wins** (a row is only overwritten by an email at least as new as the one that wrote it; a receipt also marks segments it no longer lists `superseded`). IATA → IANA tz via the `airportsdata` package; `dep_time`/`arr_time` are real instants, the ADR-0001 quad is derived from `dep_time` with the airport offset as the **source-field tier** (`dep_utc_offset`), and `arr_behavioral_date` = (arr local − 6h). Overnight arrivals: explicit "**Thu 30APR" marker, else arrival instant ≤ departure instant → +1 day. `pds.flight_email_log` is the parse ledger (one row per operational email: `parsed`/`unmatched`/`error`) — re-runs skip known Message-IDs unless `--reprocess`; `status='error'` rows are the "Delta changed their template" signal. **Consumers:** (1) the HRV feature `journal_traveled_on_a_plane` via **`pds.flight_day_unified`** — the "Traveled on a plane?" habit was **retired 2026-11-09** (see the flight-day bridge bullet; the `/check-in` plane pre-fill was removed with it); (2) **`user_tz_log` flight-auto path**: landed segments whose arrival-airport TZ has a different UTC offset than the log resolves at the landing instant get a `user_tz_log` row (`notes='flight-auto: DL108 ATL→MAD conf …'`), effective at the exact landing instant; same-offset cases skip; priority stays Manual > GPS-auto > WHOOP-auto > flight-auto (gaps only); (4) the **Flights table on `/analytics/travel`** (ISR hourly; upcoming segments first, then history; `pds.trips` below it still only lists non-ET trips, so Toronto shows up here and not there); (3) `/status` **Flights** card (badge DELTA) = heartbeat age (`flights|email`, cron every 3h → >4h partial, >12h failed) — a quiet travel month is healthy; a `partial` heartbeat also flags "only 1 of 2 accounts configured" or a parse error. **Parser QA:** `python flights_email_etl.py --file <saved .html|.txt>` prints the parsed segments; the 2026-11-08 samples (receipt, award receipt w/ overnight MAD arrival, trip details, check-in) all round-trip, and the four real segments they describe (HUBS4H ATL↔MAD Apr/May 2026, G9R752 ATL↔YYZ Nov 2026) were seeded into `pds.flights` to validate the trigger + consumers. Not joined into the HRV matrix (the plane habit already carries the signal); a future `is_flight_day` feature can read `pds.flights` directly. **Credential gotcha (2026-11-08):** the ETL logs each mailbox as `fp=<sha256[:8]>` because CI masks the address — rileybleeklm = `f9cc8009`, rileybleeker = `efbd0e49`. The legacy `IMAP_EMAIL` secret (2026-03-30) turned out to be a THIRD mailbox (`0b263cb6`), and the rileybleeklm App Password in `~/.gmail_app_password` was already revoked (AUTHENTICATIONFAILED) — so the slots ended up as: `IMAP_EMAIL_2`/`IMAP_APP_PASSWORD_2` = **rileybleeker** (working since 2026-11-08 18:57Z; that inbox holds zero Delta mail — all Delta goes to rileybleeklm), and `IMAP_EMAIL_3`/`IMAP_APP_PASSWORD_3` = **rileybleeklm** (pending a fresh App Password; 2-Step Verification must be on first). Until slot 3 works the Flights card stays `partial` and names the missing account in its error text.
- **Delivery orders — `pds.delivery_orders` from Uber Eats receipt emails (`delivery_email_etl.py`, 2026-11-09, `sql/delivery_orders_schema.sql`).** Uber has no API for a customer's own order history and the claude.ai Uber Eats connector only searches restaurants, so the ETL parses the receipt email (`noreply@uber.com`, subject "…order with Uber Eats") over IMAP, reusing the flights ETL's plumbing (imports its IMAP/HTML helpers; receipts arrive in rileybleeklm@). **The emails carry NO line items** (they sit behind the "full receipt" link) — this source gives meal TIMING and delivery COUNTS, not calories. **Two templates:** from Nov 2025 the receipt prints order time, pickup time + merchant address and delivery time ("Order completed … at …") → `time_source='receipt'`; before Nov 2025 it prints only the order DATE and the card-charge time. The charge posts 70 min after delivery in ~81% of orders (measured on 275 printed-time receipts) and the next morning in most of the rest, so `delivered_local = charge − 70 min` is kept ONLY when the implied order time lands on the weekday + daypart in the subject ("Friday evening order") → `payment_estimate`; otherwise the row keeps date + daypart only → `date_only` (behavioral date = printed date for afternoon/evening orders, NULL for "morning" ones, which could be a 1 AM awake-tail order). One row per order, natural key `(provider, order_key)` (merchant + order time, or merchant + first charge time on the old template); latest email wins; a tip-only receipt never overwrites the total; a FULL refund → `status='refunded'`; canceled receipts → `canceled`. An old-template receipt RE-SENT days later (card charged again → a different charge time, so a different key) is recognised as the same order by merchant + printed order date + daypart + total (`Store._twin`) and skipped; if the stored copy has no usable time and the new one does, the stored copy is deleted and replaced. **Estimated times (`time_source='payment_estimate'`, `raw_json.estimate_basis` says which):** (a) old template: card charge − 70 min, with a post-midnight order charged before dawn clamped to 00:00 of its printed date (so it keeps a time and lands on the previous behavioral day); (b) new template WITHOUT a courier timeline (the restaurant delivers itself, e.g. Umai): its "Order completed" stamp is a 90-minute auto-close, not a drop-off (12 of 19 such receipts sit at exactly 89–91 min vs 1 of 239 with a timeline), so order + 30 min is stored instead. `merchant_kind` (`restaurant` | `alcohol` | `store`) is a name-keyword guess (`ALCOHOL_WORDS`/`STORE_WORDS` in the ETL — extend when a new non-restaurant merchant appears; a correction for ONE merchant goes in `MERCHANT_KIND_OVERRIDES` / `MERCHANT_TZ_OVERRIDES` in the ETL, mirrored in `frontend/src/lib/delivery.ts` — NOT in an UPDATE on the table, which the next email or `--reprocess` overwrites): only `restaurant` rows count as meals. The trigger resolves the printed local wall-clock times to instants (WHOOP cycle offset → `user_tz_log` → ET) and the ADR-0001 quad from the delivery instant. **The delivery (home) address is never stored.** **`pds.delivery_daily`** = one row per behavioral day with a delivered order: counts by kind, first/last meal delivery time, `late_meal_delivery` (≥22:00 or <06:00 local), `last_meal_delivery_to_sleep_minutes` (WHOOP main-sleep onset, same join as caffeine/screen time), `untimed_meal_order_count`, `has_estimated_time`. `pds.delivery_email_log` is the parse ledger (`unmatched`/`error` = Uber changed the template). Only `parsed` rows count as done: an `error` or `unmatched` email is re-read on every run (heartbeat stays `partial` until it parses), and an `error` older than the 30-day window widens the search until it is read. `/status` **Delivery** card (badge UBER EATS) = `delivery|email` heartbeat age, same thresholds as Flights. **Time zone:** new-template receipts print the MERCHANT's clock, so the ETL derives `merchant_tz` (IANA) from the merchant address (US state / Canadian province / country maps in `delivery_email_etl.py`; split states get their dominant zone) and the trigger resolves the times in that zone, passing it to `derive_onyx_dates` as a source-field offset; old-template rows (no address) still use the WHOOP-cycle → `user_tz_log` → ET ladder. New-template receipts WITHOUT an address take `MERCHANT_TZ_OVERRIDES` or the zone of the same merchant's other receipts (when they all agree). (Added after an Atlanta order placed the evening Riley landed from Madrid was stored 6 h early — the cycle offset lags on travel-return days.) **HRV pipeline (2026-11-09, realigned 2026-11-10):** `hrv_analysis.py:merge_delivery_features()` merges `pds.delivery_daily` in pandas (not via the matrix view), fail-open: `delivery_meal_count`, `delivery_any_meal`, `delivery_late_meal`, `delivery_last_meal_to_sleep_min`. **Row placement: a delivery on behavioral day D sits on matrix row D** — row D holds the HRV of the sleep that closes day D, the sleep the meal preceded (`hrv_alignment.py`; until 2026-11-10 it was moved to row D−1 to offset the old next-row outcome). **`delivery_*` columns are excluded from the forecasting models** (`prepare_ml_data` exclude set + the SARIMAX/Prophet fallback ranking): when the evening forecast runs, today's receipts may not be parsed yet and today is never inside the zero-fill coverage window, so the newest row reads NaN where training rows read 0. They feed the Spearman and causal analyses only. Zero-fill: a no-order day is 0 only inside coverage = first delivery day → the later of the last delivery day and `delivery_covered_through` (newest `delivery|email` SUCCESS heartbeat, ET − 18 h − 1 day: a day counts as covered only 12 h after it ends, because receipts for late orders can arrive the next morning); days that may hold an unread receipt (`delivery_email_log.status <> 'parsed'`: its own behavioral day and the day before) are never zero-filled; a day with an untimed meal has a NaN late flag unless another meal that day is known late. Causal: `delivery_late_meal` + `delivery_any_meal` are `EXPLICIT_BINARY_TREATMENTS` (family nutrition — binary, not a median split, because the count is 0/1/2) and `delivery_last_meal_to_sleep_min` is a `CONTINUOUS_TREATMENTS` median split. Tests: `tests/test_delivery_features.py` (7 cases). **Results (retrain run 42962096180, 2026-11-11, after the pipeline-wide alignment fix):** late delivered meal: raw −9.0 ms, AIPW −7.3 ms (block-bootstrap 95% CI −14.3 to −1.6, p 0.014, 206 late vs 381 not) — does NOT survive BH-FDR; any delivered meal: raw −6.7, AIPW −3.3 (p 0.23); gap to sleep: +9.1 ms for an above-median gap (p 0.07). The earlier run 42763606230 (−3.5 ms, p 0.35) already had delivery on the right night; the change comes from the corrected confounders (yesterday's strain is now really yesterday's). The raw gap is partly confounding (HRV has drifted down over the same period that late orders went up). With Cronometer logging lapsed since 2026-06-29 this is currently the only food signal. **Items + estimated calories (live 2026-11-10):** the signed-in web app returns cart items — `POST https://www.ubereats.com/_p/api/getPastOrdersV1` (header `x-csrf-token: x`, body `{"lastWorkflowUUID": "<last uuid of the previous page>"}`, 10 orders/page, `meta.hasMore`) → per order `baseEaterOrder` (`uuid`, `completedAt`, `isCancelled`, `shoppingCart.items[]` with `title`/`quantity`/`price` in cents/`customizations[].childOptions.options[]`), `storeInfo.title`, `fareInfo.totalPrice`. **History only goes back ~9 months** (first run reached 2026-02-15; older orders exist only as itemless receipts). `tools/ubereats_harvest/harvest.mjs` calls it from inside the page of a persistent Chrome profile (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\\Onyx\\ubereats-harvest-profile`) and posts to **`/api/delivery/items/import`** (an exact-path entry in `isCiRoute()`), which upserts `pds.delivery_order_items` (facts; key `(provider, order_uuid, item_key)`; prices/quantities/dates coerced), then — called in a loop with `{estimate: true, limit, offset}` — estimates ONE unit of each distinct dish (`est_key` = hash of store|title|customizations) with Claude (`claude-opus-5-5`, effort low, JSON-schema structured output, 25 dishes per call) into `pds.delivery_item_nutrition` (kind food|drink|alcohol|non_food, kcal, protein/carbs/fat, alcohol_g, confidence, basis published|typical; `ignoreDuplicates` so a hand-corrected row is never overwritten). A failed batch is retried one dish at a time and a dish that fails alone is stepped past (`offset`); `{done: true}` writes the `ubereats|items` heartbeat, with the route counting `pds.delivery_items_unestimated` ITSELF (`partial` + the estimator error while anything is left). A history page that fails midway, or the 400-page cap, is a FAILED heartbeat and the fetched orders are not added to `state.json`, so the next run pages through them again. `/status` **Uber Eats Items** card (Kindle-style thresholds; both PC-harvester cards read their heartbeat from a dedicated limit-1 query because the shared 100-row sync_log window only spans ~13 h). **It does NOT run headless:** Uber's bot protection answers headless Chrome with a 403 "Just a moment…" challenge — do not try to defeat it (no stealth flags). A scheduled run uses a normal Chrome window parked off-screen (`--window-position=-32000,-32000`, launched hidden via `run-hidden.js`); Task Scheduler "Onyx Uber Eats Harvest", every 10 min (`register-task.ps1`). **Sign-in** was done by Riley in a PLAIN Chrome window on the harvester profile (QR code from the phone app); the command is in the harvester README ("Signing in again") — close that window afterwards, the harvester cannot start while it is open. First full run: 220 orders, 583 lines, 267 dishes estimated (57 high / 164 medium / 46 low confidence). **`pds.delivery_nutrition_daily`** = estimated kcal/macros per behavioral day from RESTAURANT orders (store / package-store orders are excluded — a multi-pack is not one day's intake; the 'alcohol' `merchant_kind` is only a store-NAME guess: the Moreland Package Store orders are Red Bull): 174 days, median 2,090 kcal, 24 days over 3,500 kcal. Estimates come from dish names: fine for big-vs-small days and macro shape, not gram-level; micronutrients are not estimated. **Ordered is not eaten is handled per dish (2026-11-11): `pds.delivery_order_items.eaten_fraction`** (nullable 0..1, NULL = all of it; `sql/delivery_item_eaten_fraction.sql`, applied) is multiplied into `pds.delivery_nutrition_daily` and `getDeliveryItemsForDays` (kcal = one-unit estimate x quantity x coalesce(fraction, 1)). Riley sets it with one tap, "Ate: All / 3/4 / 1/2 / 1/4 / None" (`components/DeliveryPortions.tsx`, NULL shows as a dashed All), in two places: the Today's Meals list on `/nutrition` (with a Today / Yesterday / day-before switch, since leftovers are noticed the next morning; the dish kcal and the Uber Eats day total change at once and the macro charts refetch silently) and a "Uber Eats: how much did you eat?" section on `/check-in` that lists that behavior day's delivered restaurant dishes PLUS the dishes of the 2 days before whose fraction is still NULL, grouped by day (the harvester runs every 6 h, so a dinner delivered after the 21:30 card usually lands after that check-in; it is asked the next evening — tapping All stores 1 and stops it being asked again). The section renders nothing when there is nothing to ask, loads after the questions, saves per tap, and saves on its own. Writes are serialized per dish and a failed save reverts to the last value the server confirmed (`createPortionSaver` in `lib/delivery-portion.ts`). A day whose dishes are all marked None has `est_kcal` 0 and is NOT filled into the `/nutrition` macro charts (`getNutrition` reads `est_kcal > 0`, `mergeDeliveryEstimates`): no food signal, not a 0 kcal day. Both write through **`PATCH /api/delivery/items/[itemId]`** (`{eaten_fraction: number|null}`; owner session only, NOT in `isCiRoute()`; same-origin `Origin`, JSON content type and a 256-byte body cap enforced; only null or 0..1 with at most 2 decimals; updates that one row's `eaten_fraction` and nothing else, never `delivery_item_nutrition`, whose estimate is shared by every order of the dish; 404 for an unknown row; revalidates `/nutrition`). The harvester import (`buildOrderItemRows` in `lib/delivery.ts`) never sends `eaten_fraction`, so a set portion survives re-harvests (`tests/delivery-portion.test.cjs` checks it). A multi-person order still counts in full unless the eaten share is set. The calorie estimates are not wired into the HRV pipeline. **Leftovers eaten on a later day (2026-11-14, `sql/delivery_item_leftovers.sql`, `lib/delivery-leftovers.ts`):** a dish whose delivery-day portion is set below All is asked about again on the next behavioral days, D+1 to D+3, in a "Leftovers" block under the Uber Eats section of `/check-in` and in Today's Meals on `/nutrition` ("From Tue · 3/4 left · Ate: All / 3/4 / 1/2 / 1/4 · Not today · Threw out"; shares are of what is LEFT, tapping the lit answer clears it, the block appears as soon as a portion is set below All, also on the same screen). **An unanswered leftover counts as thrown out** (Riley, 2026-11-14): nothing is added anywhere. Storage: `pds.delivery_item_leftovers` (PK `(item_id, eaten_date)`; `fraction` = share of the WHOLE cart line eaten that day, `threw_out` = the rest was discarded after that day; 0 + not thrown out = "not today"; owner_read + service role). Writes go through **`PUT /api/delivery/items/[itemId]/leftovers`** (`{date, fraction, threw_out?}`, fraction null = clear that day; owner session only, NOT in `isCiRoute()`; same request checks as the portion PATCH). Two triggers hold the rules on every write path: `delivery_item_leftovers_guard` (portion below All, day within D+1..D+3 and not after behavioral today, delivery portion + leftovers <= the whole dish, nothing after a thrown-out day) and `delivery_order_items_leftover_guard` (the delivery-day portion cannot be raised past leftovers already saved; the PATCH answers 409). Custom SQLSTATEs `OXL01`..`OXL04` map to 409 messages (`leftoverConflictMessage`). `pds.delivery_nutrition_daily` adds each leftover on the day it was eaten (same columns + `leftover_lines`), so the delivery day goes down and the next day goes up on `/nutrition`; a leftover-only day is filled into the macro charts like any delivery day. **HRV side:** `pds.delivery_uneaten_meal_orders` lists orders whose every food dish was marked None on the delivery day (an unestimated dish counts as food), matched to their receipt (same behavioral day, nearest delivery time within 2 h; live check: median gap 0.6 min, 205 of 210 within 90 min), and `pds.delivery_daily` no longer counts such a receipt as a meal (meal counts, first/last meal time, late-meal flag and gap to sleep skip it; + `uneaten_meal_order_count`). **When it was eaten (2026-11-14, `sql/delivery_leftover_time.sql`):** once an amount is picked, the leftover row shows "When? [time] · Now · Same as 7:30 PM" (Now only on the current behavioral day; Same as = the time another leftover of that day already has). The PUT body takes `time` ("HH:MM", only with an amount > 0), stored as `eaten_time` (local wall clock as entered; before 06:00 = the small hours closing that behavioral day). The guard trigger computes `eaten_at` from (`eaten_date`, `eaten_time`) with `pds.tz_for_instant` at noon ET of that day (a client never sends the instant) and refuses a time in the future (`OXL05`). `pds.delivery_daily` now also lists leftover meals (one per day, order and time, food dishes only): `leftover_meal_count` / `untimed_leftover_meal_count` are appended, and first/last meal time, `late_meal_delivery` and the gap to sleep include TIMED leftover meals; `order_count`, `meal_order_count`, `untimed_meal_order_count` and `merchants` stay receipts only. `merge_delivery_features` adds the leftover counts to the receipt counts, so a leftover meal is a delivered meal of the day it was eaten (`delivery_meal_count`, `delivery_any_meal`); an untimed one leaves that day's late flag NaN unless another meal is known late. The client saver is the same serialized `createPortionSaver`, now generic over key and value (one slot per dish and day). Tests: `frontend/tests/delivery-leftovers.test.cjs`.
- **Food by photo — `pds.photo_meals` (2026-11-14, `sql/photo_meals.sql`, `lib/photo-meal.ts`, `components/PhotoMealLogger.tsx`).** Riley takes or picks a plate photo ("Log a meal by photo" in Today's Meals on `/nutrition`, or Log > **Meal photo** = `/nutrition?log=photo`, logger drawn first). The browser downscales it (long edge <= 1568 px JPEG, <= 3 MB base64) and reads the EXIF capture time (`exifCaptureTime`: DateTimeOriginal, else DateTime; JPEG only, else "now"). **`POST /api/nutrition/photo-meals/estimate`** (`{image_base64, media_type}`; owner session, NOT in `isCiRoute()`; same-origin JSON checks via `checkPortionRequest` with its own size cap) sends it to Claude (`claude-sonnet-5-5`, the chat model, effort low, JSON-schema structured output: `is_food`, `confidence`, items `{name, portion, kcal, protein_g, carbs_g, fat_g}`), saves nothing and keeps no photo. Riley edits names, amounts, kcal/macros and the time (or "Enter by hand" with no photo), then **`POST /api/nutrition/photo-meals`** stores the meal: totals are summed by the route from the items (never taken from the browser), `est_kcal` = Claude's original total (to measure later how much Riley corrects), upsert on a browser-made `client_key` UUID (`ignoreDuplicates`, a double tap saves once). `eaten_date` = behavioral day, `eaten_time` = local wall clock (before 06:00 = the small hours closing that day); the trigger computes `eaten_at` with `pds.tz_for_instant` like leftovers and refuses a future time (`OXP01` -> 409). **`DELETE /api/nutrition/photo-meals`** `{meal_id}` removes one (the × in Today's Meals). **Counting:** `getNutrition` reads `pds.photo_meal_daily` (same `est_*` columns as `delivery_nutrition_daily`) in parallel with the matrix and Uber Eats reads; `combineEstimateDays` ADDS photo meals to the Uber Eats estimate on days with no Cronometer/MFP log (`nutrition_source` `uber_eats` / `photo` / `uber_eats+photo`; labels from `daySourceLabel` / `rangeSourceLabel`), and a Cronometer day still wins over both. The review screen warns when an Uber Eats order was delivered within 2 h of the meal time (`nearbyDeliveries`), so a delivered meal is not counted twice. Until the migration is applied every read fails open (missing relation `PGRST205` / `42P01` = no photo meals) and a save answers 503. `/status` **Meal photos** card (badge PHOTO): "unknown" until the table exists, then green with the count (manual log, so data age never turns it red). Not yet in the HRV pipeline, `pds.meal_timing_daily` or Onyx Chat's tools. Tests: `frontend/tests/photo-meal.test.cjs`.
- **Online orders — `/orders` from shipping and delivery emails (`orders_email_etl.py`, 2026-11-12, `sql/online_orders_schema.sql`).** Tracks what Riley ordered online, what is on the way, what arrived and what is overdue. No store or carrier gives Riley an API, so the emails are the source: one Gmail search per account (subject words like shipped / delivered / out for delivery / order confirmed; Promotions + Social and food-delivery, travel and digital senders excluded in `EXCLUDED_SENDER_DOMAINS`), read-only like the other email ETLs (it reuses `flights_email_etl.py`'s IMAP plumbing; Riley chose the email path on 2026-11-12 knowing the email-risk note). Each email becomes one `pds.online_order_events` row keyed by Message-ID (status ordered / shipped / out_for_delivery / delivered / delayed / exception / canceled, store, order number, tracking numbers + carrier, expected date, up to 5 item names; `ignored` / `error` rows too, so a re-run skips what it read; bump `PARSER_VERSION` to re-read everything). **Never stored: bodies, the shipping address, phone numbers, prices or payment details**; USPS numbers printed with the 420+ZIP prefix are stored without it, and item names that look like an address or the recipient's name are dropped.
