Every run then **recomputes `pds.online_orders` from all events** (pure `build_orders`): emails join when they share store + order number or a tracking number (a store's shipped email, Amazon's "package from X" email via the TBA number in its tracking link, and UPS / FedEx notices land on one row); emails with no number, and "delivered" emails that quote another number (Thorne does), join the same store's nearest open order from 21 days before to 5 after; Amazon emails without a tracking link are matched to the store by name. `confirms_delivery` = the store (or Amazon / the carrier) emailed "delivered" for at least 60 % of its last six shipped orders older than 10 days. **"Overdue" is decided at read time** in `frontend/src/lib/online-orders.ts` (`classifyOrder`): on the way until 2 days past the expected date (10 days after shipping with no date), then **Needs attention** if the store usually confirms delivery, otherwise **No delivery email** (quiet); a carrier-reported problem = attention; ordered 7+ days with no shipping email = attention only for a store that ships and only with an order number; everything drops off 30 days after shipping (45 after ordering); delivered / canceled stay listed 30 days. Riley's one-tap **Got it** (arrived) / **Hide** / **Undo** go to `PATCH /api/orders/[key]` (owner session, same-origin JSON, ≤256 bytes, `{action}` only) → `pds.online_order_marks`, stored under the order's key and matched by any alias, so a key change from a later email keeps the answer; the ETL never touches marks. **All three tables are service-role only** (RLS on, no policies, no anon / authenticated grants); `/orders` is `force-dynamic` (not ISR) and reads them server-side via `lib/online-orders-server.ts` (`server-only`). Today shows a **Packages** panel only when something needs attention or arrives today (`GET /api/orders/summary`). `/status` card **Online Orders** (badge GMAIL) = `online_orders|email` heartbeat age (>4 h partial, >12 h or failed red; partial also = an account missing or an email failed to parse). **Personal Amazon.com orders (2026-11-12)** send no email to either Gmail account (checked: the 400-day backfill found none), so the Kindle harvester's 3-hourly pass on Riley's PC (same signed-in personal-account Chrome profile) also reads the "Your Orders" list (past 3 months, up to 3 pages; `tools/kindle_harvest/amazon-orders.mjs`, `--orders-only` to run just that step) and posts order number, order date, status, status / expected date and up to 5 item names (never the total, address or recipient) to **`POST /api/orders/amazon/import`** (an exact-path entry in `isCiRoute()`; `lib/amazon-orders.ts` validates and drops any item text with an amount). The route writes INSERT-ONLY `pds.online_order_events` rows keyed `amazon-web:<order>:<status>:<date>` (store `amazon.com`), so a status's first-seen time sticks and a changed expected date (a delay) is a new event; the next `orders_email_etl.py` run folds them into `pds.online_orders` (up to 3 h later). Heartbeat `amazon|orders` on every call (failed on an expired Amazon session or a harvester error, partial when cards could not be read); `/status` card **Amazon Orders** (badge AMAZON, Kindle-style >12 h / >48 h thresholds). Stores that never email on delivery (Thorne, Flakes) can only be closed with Got it. Tests: `tests/test_orders_email_etl.py` (44, synthetic), `frontend/tests/online-orders.test.cjs` (22), `tools/kindle_harvest/amazon-orders.test.mjs` (9).
- **Phone screen time v1 — the iOS Shortcuts open/close path is RETIRED (built 2026-11-08, retired 2026-11-13).** Two Shortcuts personal automations (App → Is Opened / Is Closed) were meant to POST every app open and close to `/api/screen-time/event` → `pds.phone_app_events`, paired into `pds.phone_app_sessions` and rolled up in `pds.phone_screen_daily`. The automations were never set up on the phone and the table **never received a row**; the Screen Time app (next bullet) replaced the idea before it was used (every app without ticking each one, Apple's own numbers, pickups and notifications). Removed 2026-11-13: the `/api/screen-time/event` route, its exact-path entry in `isCiRoute()` and the `/status` **Screen Time** card (it could only ever read Unknown / Never); `docs/screen_time_shortcuts_setup.md` is marked retired. Nothing else read these objects (checked: `hrv_analysis.py`, `causal_inference.py`, the frontend and the other repos read only `pds.phone_usage_*`). **Still in the database, on purpose:** the empty table and its two views (`sql/phone_screen_time_schema.sql`, kept as the record of what exists). Dropping them is a separate step that needs Riley's approval. Do not build on them, and do not re-add the route: screen time comes from the app.
- **Phone screen time v2 — the Onyx Screen Time iPhone app (2026-11-11, `tools/screentime_ios`, `sql/phone_usage_schema.sql`).** Uses Apple's iOS 26.4 Screen Time export API (`DeviceActivityData.activityData`), which outside the EU works only in a development-signed personal build: built and signed on a GitHub macOS runner (`screentime-ios.yml`; Riley has no Mac), installed over the air from `/install` (manifest served as text/xml by the public exact-path route `/api/screen-time/manifest/<32 hex>` because Supabase Storage serves XML as text/plain; one-tap token copy via the session-only `/api/screen-time/token`). Apple cert + profile expire **2027-11-10** (see the tools README for renewal). A daily Shortcuts Time-of-Day automation runs the app's "Sync Screen Time to Onyx" action (verified to work with the phone LOCKED) → `POST /api/screen-time/usage` (CI token) → `pds.phone_usage_segments` (hourly) + `phone_usage_items` → view `pds.phone_usage_daily` (total/late-night/after-midnight minutes, pickups, notifications, per-app + per-category minutes, bedtime-anchored `_est` columns). Every app, including future installs; the first sync backfilled ~30 days (Apple's retention). Service-role only. Heartbeat `screen_time|usage`, `/status` card "Screen Time app". **Failed syncs (2026-11-11):** the app POSTs `{failure: {stage, message}}` (stages `access`/`read`/`encode`/`network`/`http`) to the same route when a sync breaks, which writes a failed heartbeat so the card goes red on that run; a rejected upload (400) or DB error (500) logs one server-side and answers `logged: true` so the app does not report it twice. Heartbeat text never echoes usage data (sync_log is owner-readable). A missing or refused token cannot be reported, so the card still ages out (>36 h red). **HRV pipeline (2026-11-11):** `hrv_analysis.py:merge_screen_time_features` puts day D on row D (same night) as `screen_total_min`, `_late_night_min`, `_after_midnight_min`, `_pickups`, `_notifications`, `_social_min`, `_last_hour_before_sleep_min`, `_during_sleep_min`; a day counts only once a sync happened after it ended (partial days NaN), unreported days NaN (never zero-filled). `screen_` is in `NOT_FORECAST_PREFIXES` (with `delivery_`) so it stays out of the forecast; `screen_during_sleep_min` is same-sleep and `screen_last_hour_before_sleep_min` needs sleep onset (`hrv_alignment.py`). Six `CONTINUOUS_TREATMENTS` (family behavior); they sit in `dropped_low_n` until `MIN_CONTINUOUS_N` days accrue and then join the FDR family automatically — no manual step. Test: `tests/test_screen_time_features.py`.
- **Air quality (PM2.5) — `pds.air_quality_daily` from Open-Meteo (`air_quality_etl.py`, 2026-11-11, `sql/air_quality_schema.sql`).** Keyless: `air-quality-api.open-meteo.com/v1/air-quality` (CAMS model analysis, ~0.4° grid outside Europe, global history from 2022-07-29), hourly `pm2_5` + `us_aqi` requested in **UTC** (`timezone=GMT`); **the ET clock day is built in the ETL from UTC hours**, exactly like `weather_etl.py` (2026-11-14): Open-Meteo applies ONE UTC offset to a whole response, the one in force when the request is made, so asking for `America/New_York` put every day of the other DST season an hour off. Each window asks for one extra UTC date so its last ET day is complete; DST days have 23 / 25 hours. Rows written before the fix need one re-sync from 2024-01-01 (`gh workflow run air-quality-etl.yml -f days=<days since 2024-01-01>`); upserts replace them in place. One row per ET day: `pm25_mean`/`pm25_max`, `us_aqi_mean`/`us_aqi_max`, `n_hours`, `latitude`/`longitude`, `source`. Hours after now are forecasts and never stored, so today's row is partial until the day ends. Location is fixed to central Atlanta (33.75, -84.39; `AIR_QUALITY_LAT`/`AIR_QUALITY_LON` or `--lat/--lon` override it — **change them when the NYC move happens**; every row carries its coordinates). It deliberately ignores Garmin GPS: a trip would silently move the whole series, and the data is regional model output, not a sensor at the apartment. Runs every 3 h (`air-quality-etl.yml`), re-upserting the last 7 days (hours get revised); an EMPTY table backfills itself from 2024-01-01, and `gh workflow run air-quality-etl.yml -f days=N` re-syncs N days. Service-role only (no browser read). Heartbeat `air_quality|daily` on every run (success / partial = API returned no PM2.5 / failed incl. a missing table, whose message names `sql/air_quality_schema.sql`); `/status` card "Air Quality" (badge OPEN-METEO; >4 h partial, >12 h red); allow-listed in `tools/watchdog_cloud.py` `SCHEDULED`. **HRV pipeline:** `hrv_analysis.py:merge_air_quality_features` puts clock day D on row D (same row as the sleep that closes behavioral day D; no re-keying, no shift) as `aqi_pm25`, `aqi_pm25_max`, `aqi_us_max`; days with <20 hourly values and days the table does not cover stay NaN (never zero); fail-open when the table is empty or missing. `aqi_` is in `NOT_FORECAST_PREFIXES` (today's row is partial when the evening forecast runs) and in `CONTROLLABLE_FEATURE_PREFIXES` (exposure is changeable even if the air is not; inert while it is out of the forecast). `aqi_pm25` is a `CONTINUOUS_TREATMENTS` median split (family behavior, common lag-1 confounders only — **no seasonal/temperature adjustment**, so read it as exploratory). Apply the SQL once, then `gh workflow run air-quality-etl.yml`; until then the card is red with that instruction. Tests: `tests/test_air_quality.py`.
- **Google Calendar — `pds.calendar_events` from the calendars' iCal feeds (`google_calendar_etl.py`, 2026-11-13, `sql/calendar_events_schema.sql`).** Read-only, one way (Riley's calendars → Onyx): this source never writes to a calendar and never reads or writes care items. **It is not the `/health` "Google Calendar sync" (care items → a calendar Onyx created; OAuth, `lib/care-google*`, `pds.ci_tokens` service `google_calendar`, see the Health section): opposite direction, no shared code, no sign-in.** Do not put that "Onyx care" calendar's address in the secret (its events are copies of care items the page already shows). Source: Google Calendar's "secret address in iCal format", one per calendar, listed in the secret **`GOOGLE_CALENDAR_ICS_SECRET`** = a JSON list of `{account, calendar, url}` (`account` / `calendar` are short labels Riley picks: letters, digits, space, `_ . -`, never an address; any ICS feed works, e.g. a published Outlook calendar; same JSON in `~/.onyx_gcal_ics.json` on the PC). Runs hourly (`google-calendar-etl.yml`, :55): one GET per feed (Google builds it per request, no ETag), expands recurrences with `icalendar` + `recurring-ical-events` inside **ET today −400 d .. +400 d** and upserts one row per instance. Stored: title, `start_at` / `end_at`, `all_day`, `start_date` / `end_date` (**inclusive**; an all-day event's exclusive DTEND minus one day; a timed event's local dates in `tz`), `tz` (the event's TZID, else the calendar's zone; NULL for all-day), location, status (`confirmed` | `tentative` | `cancelled`), the two labels, `recurring`. **Not stored: description, attendees, organizer, conference links, the UID, the feed URL.** Links, meeting ids and passcodes are also cut out of the title and location (`_scrub` in the ETL); a location that was only a meeting link is stored as "Online meeting". No `onyx_*` date columns (not a behavior source; an all-day event has no instant). Key `event_key` = sha256(account, calendar, UID, original slot)[:32]: the slot is empty for a one-off (moving it updates the same row) and the RECURRENCE-ID as a UTC instant for a series (a moved instance keeps its row). **The X-WR-TIMEZONE trap:** `recurring-ical-events` applies Google's `X-WR-TIMEZONE` header by default, which rewrites UTC-form series into the calendar zone before expansion, so they drift an hour across DST and edited instances come out twice; the ETL reads the header (display zone for UTC-form and floating times) and **removes it before expansion** (`tests/test_google_calendar.py` pins this; never drop that `cal.pop`). **Secrets (the repo and its Actions logs are public):** a feed URL is a bearer credential for the whole calendar, so it never reaches a log, an exception, a traceback or `sync_log`: `httpx` / `httpcore` loggers are at WARNING (httpx logs the full URL at INFO), every URL is registered with `::add-mask::` (GitHub masks only a secret's whole value, not the URLs inside the JSON), fetch errors are re-raised as a plain message `from None`, `retry_helper` is not used (it logs exception text), and database / parser error TEXT is never echoed (it can quote a row or a feed line) — only the type and code. Logs and `--dry-run` print counts only and name a feed by position (`calendar 1/2`); the heartbeat (owner-only) names it by label. **Removal:** the feed lists what exists now, so after a fully validated fetch + parse the calendar's rows not seen this run (inside the window, one day in from each edge) are removed; **never for a calendar whose fetch or parse failed** (a reset address answers 404, or 200 with a web page: the body must start with `BEGIN:VCALENDAR` and end with `END:VCALENDAR`); nor for one that parsed only partly (a malformed series or a skipped event: what parsed is still upserted, nothing is removed, so the unreadable events keep their last known rows, and the heartbeat is `partial`); a run that would remove more than half of a calendar's rows (once over 25) is refused → `partial`, override with the workflow input `allow_large_removal`; rows of a calendar no longer in the secret are removed. Cancelled instances are upserted as `cancelled` (so a stored one flips) and left out of every count and every read. Heartbeat `google_calendar|events` on every run: `success` / `partial` (a feed failed, a malformed series or event was skipped, a large removal was refused) / `failed` (every feed failed, the secret is missing or not valid JSON, the table is missing → the message names the SQL file). **Recovery:** `HTTP 404` for a calendar = its secret address was reset or is wrong → copy the new one (Calendar settings → the calendar → Integrate calendar) into the secret and the dotfile; to revoke a leaked address press Reset there. **Consumers:** `GET /api/calendar/events?from&to[&summary=1]` (owner session only, NOT in `isCiRoute()`, `force-dynamic`, `no-store`, range ≤ 62 days, the query is padded a day each side; reader `lib/calendar-events-server.ts`, `server-only`, service role, explicit columns; `not_connected` when the table is missing) → `/health` (the "Your calendar events" card under the connection card shows the real state: synced with labels, event counts and last sync age / not synced yet / late / sync failing / couldn't be checked, and has no button; Month + Agenda show the events after each day's care items, labelled "Google Calendar: <label> (read-only)", as entries with no care-item id; `lib/care-planner.ts` stays import-free; the events path issues GETs only) and Today's Calendar panel. Pure helpers in `lib/calendar-events.ts` (import-free; day bucketing uses the browser's zone: a timed event is listed on the day it starts, one of 24 h or more also on the later days). `/status` card **Google Calendar** (badge GOOGLE; dedicated limit-1 heartbeat read; >3 h partial, >8 h or a failed run red; heartbeat only, no event rows on that cacheable endpoint). Known limits: a declined invitation still shows (the decline is only on the attendee line, which is not read); ICS has no event type, so working-location / out-of-office entries look like ordinary events. Tests: `tests/test_google_calendar.py` (62, synthetic ICS), `frontend/tests/calendar-events.test.cjs`, `frontend/tests/health-calendar.test.cjs`, `frontend/scripts/test-care-planner.mjs`, `tests/today-regression.cjs`. Doc: `docs/health-appointments.md` ("Calendar events sync").
- **Weather — `pds.weather_daily` from Open-Meteo (`weather_etl.py`, 2026-11-13, `sql/weather_schema.sql`).** Keyless: `archive-api.open-meteo.com/v1/archive` (ECMWF IFS + ERA5, hourly; today's hours are already there, so no forecast endpoint). One row per ET clock day, metric units: `temperature_max`/`_min`/`_mean`, `apparent_temp_mean`/`_max`, `humidity_mean`, `dew_point_mean`, `pressure_mean` (surface hPa), `precipitation_sum`, `wind_speed_max`, `cloud_cover_mean`, `daylight_hours`, `sunshine_hours`, `uv_index_max` + `uv_source`, `night_temp_mean`/`_min` + `night_hours`, `n_hours`, `latitude`/`longitude`, `source`. **Same home location as air quality**: the constants and the `AIR_QUALITY_LAT`/`AIR_QUALITY_LON` overrides are imported from `air_quality_etl.py`, so one change moves both at the NYC move. **The ET day is built in the ETL from UTC hours**: Open-Meteo applies one UTC offset to a whole response (a July day requested in November comes back at GMT-5), so its own local day is an hour off in the other DST season (`air_quality_etl.py` builds its ET day the same way since 2026-11-14). Hours after now are forecasts and never stored (today is partial; DST days have 23 / 25 hours). `temperature_max`/`_min` are hourly extremes; the API's own daily minimum can read up to ~1 °C lower. **UV** is null on the archive, so it comes from the historical-forecast API (one extra request per window, best-effort: on failure the UV columns are left untouched and the heartbeat is `partial`). Runs every 3 h (`weather-etl.yml`), re-upserting the last **10** days (ERA5 replaces IFS after ~5 days); an EMPTY table backfills itself from 2024-01-01, a routine run whose newest stored day is older than 10 days resumes from that day (`routine_start`: an outage leaves no date gap and no half-written last day), and `gh workflow run weather-etl.yml -f days=N` re-syncs N days. It does not look for holes further back: **after a partial or failed backfill, run the same backfill again** (the partial heartbeat of a backfill whose UV request failed names the `days=N` to repeat; the next routine run reports success without repairing the older days). **The coordinates are never logged** (public Actions logs): neither Open-Meteo ETL prints them and httpx's per-request lines are off in both; still keep any `AIR_QUALITY_LAT`/`_LON` override coarse, since it is stored on every row. Service-role only. Heartbeat `weather|daily` on every run; `/status` card "Weather" (badge OPEN-METEO; >4 h partial, >12 h red); allow-listed in `tools/watchdog_cloud.py` `SCHEDULED`. **Page:** the Weather panel next to the PM2.5 panel on `/analytics/hrv` (`components/WeatherPanel.tsx`, `lib/weather.ts`, `GET /api/weather`, owner session only; US units; pills switch the chart between temperature, humidity, sun, rain & wind and pressure; today is left out of the daytime lines and the 30-day figures until it is over). **HRV pipeline:** `hrv_analysis.py:merge_weather_features` (pandas, fail-open) adds the `wx_*` family. Daytime columns of clock day D sit on row D (`wx_temp_max`, `wx_temp_mean`, `wx_apparent_temp_max`, `wx_humidity`, `wx_dew_point`, `wx_pressure`, `wx_precip_mm`, `wx_wind_max`, `wx_cloud_cover`, `wx_daylight_hours`, `wx_sunshine_hours`, `wx_uv_max`). **The night columns (`wx_night_temp_mean`, `wx_night_temp_min`) are the 01:00-08:00 ET hours, stored on their own clock day and moved one day EARLIER by date**, so row D carries the outdoor temperature during the sleep that closes day D (live check on 725 sleeps: 85 % of sleep time falls in that window of clock day D+1, 0.1 % in clock day D's). **The clock-day minimum is not a feature** (it usually falls near dawn, inside the previous row's sleep). **A day counts only once it is over and has all its hours**: its date is before ET today AND `n_hours` >= 23 (the shortest full ET day), the same rule in `merge_weather_features` (`WX_MIN_HOURS`, `today`) and on the panel (`isPartialDay` in `lib/weather.ts`). The hour count alone is not enough: today passes 20 hours at about 19:00 ET while its rain sum, wind maximum and means are still growing. Unfinished or incomplete days, nights with <7 hourly values, and uncovered days stay NaN; nothing is zero-filled (a 0 is a real dry or overcast day). `wx_` is in `NOT_FORECAST_PREFIXES` and NOT in `CONTROLLABLE_FEATURE_PREFIXES`. Three `CONTINUOUS_TREATMENTS` median splits (family behavior): `wx_apparent_temp_max`, `wx_sunshine_hours`, `wx_night_temp_mean` — **exploratory: there is no seasonal adjustment in the causal layer**, and temperature and sunshine are almost pure season. The weather columns are NOT yet in any confounder set, so they do not adjust `aqi_pm25` or anything else. Fixed-location model data, not where Riley was on a trip. Tests: `tests/test_weather.py`, `frontend/tests/weather.test.cjs`.

- **Pollen — `pds.pollen_daily` from Atlanta Allergy & Asthma (`pollen_etl.py`, 2026-11-14, `sql/pollen_schema.sql`).** Keyless public page `atlantaallergy.com/pollen_counts/index/YYYY/MM/DD` (the NAB-certified Atlanta station): "Total Pollen Count for MM/DD/YYYY: N" with the tree / grass / weed levels, their top contributors and the mold level of that day, plus a calendar of the month's daily totals. **Polite scraping of a clinic site** (robots.txt allows all, no reuse statement): data stays private, the routine run makes **one request** (today's page; its calendar re-upserts every count of the month, so late or corrected counts heal), the User-Agent names the tool and no person, backfill is one request per month 5 s apart (`--backfill-months N`, max 120). Every calendar total is read with the date from its own cell link, never from its position. **Dates:** `count_date` is the PUBLISHED date; the count is grains/m³ over the previous 24 h, sampled up to that morning, so it mostly describes clock day `count_date − 1`. It is deliberately not called `calendar_date`: an HRV feature (not built) must move it one day EARLIER by date, the move `hrv_alignment.wake_date_to_closing_day` makes. **Missing is missing:** the station skips most weekends and holidays and publishes some days as "N/A"; those days have no row (never a zero, a published 0 is stored as 0) and an empty cell never overwrites a stored count. Calendar rows (`total_count`, `count_level`) and the day row (levels + contributors) are upserted as separate batches, so a calendar-only run never blanks the day columns. **Layout guards:** no calendar or a calendar without the requested date = failed heartbeat (`LayoutError`); an unrecognised day block, unexpected cell text, or ≥10 past weekdays without one count = `partial` heartbeat naming the cause, with what was read still stored. Atlanta only: the series ends at the NYC move. Owner read + service role. Heartbeat `pollen|daily` on every run; `/status` card "Pollen" (badge ATL ALLERGY; >21 h partial, >27 h or failed red; newest count >4 days behind the spine = partial). **Not live yet:** Riley applies the SQL and adds `.github/workflows/pollen-etl.yml` (a protected path; the YAML, cron `20 16,21 * * *`, and the `tools/watchdog_cloud.py` `SCHEDULED` line are in the roadmap row "Pollen: Atlanta Allergy & Asthma daily count"), then runs `gh workflow run pollen-etl.yml -f months=35` once to backfill from 2024-01. Tests: `tests/test_pollen_etl.py` (synthetic fixture `tests/fixtures/pollen_atlanta_2026_04_08.html`).
- **`pds.tanita_measurements` is the raw Tanita Health Planet store** (`tanita_schema.sql`): one row per measurement instant — PK `measured_at TIMESTAMPTZ`, `weight_kg`, `body_fat_pct`, `model` (device code), `raw_json` JSONB (grouped API items, passed as dicts), ADR-0001 quad via the standard `pds.set_onyx_dates_tanita_measurements` trigger (pure-instant variant), RLS anon-read/service-full. **Health Planet API facts** (healthplanet.jp/apis/api.html, Japanese): OAuth client-application type with redirect to the API's own `success.html` → copy-paste `code` (10-min TTL) — bootstrap via `python tanita_etl.py --auth` then `ci_token_helper.py upload tanita`. Access tokens last 30 days (`expires_in=2592000`); the `refresh_token` grant is **undocumented but works** — the ETL refreshes only within 7 days of expiry and persists both returned tokens (rotation behavior unconfirmed upstream). Data endpoint `/status/innerscan.json` with `date=1` (measured, not registered), tags `6021` (weight kg) + `6022` (body fat %) — tags 6023–6029 (muscle mass, BMR, bone…) were **retired from the public API 2020-06-29**, don't design around them. One array item per tag; items sharing `(date, model)` = one physical weigh-in. **Windows >3 months are SILENTLY truncated** (the API corrects `to` to from+3mo with no error) → the ETL pages backfills in ≤80-day windows; rate limit 60 req/h. **Timestamps are TZ-naive `yyyyMMddHHmm` in DEVICE-LOCAL wall-clock time** (verified empirically 2026-06-09: a ~11:26 PM ET weigh-in returned `202606092326`, not the JST equivalent) — parsed via `TANITA_TZ = America/New_York` in `tanita_etl.py`; re-verify with `--inspect` if the scale ever changes time zones. `/status`: the manual "Weight" card was **replaced** by the automated "Tanita" card — freshness from newest `measured_at`/`onyx_et_date` + the `tanita|weight` sync_log heartbeat, daily-cadence `deriveStatus` thresholds like Eight Sleep.
- **Activity taxonomy (run / sauna + MULTI-select strength tags) — auto-categorized, with manual multi-select split + muscle groups.** Onyx classifies every ingested workout into a small user-facing taxonomy. `run` and `sauna` are **auto-derived** from device sport labels (Garmin `activity_type` contains `run`; WHOOP `sport_name` = `running`/`sauna`). **`sauna` additionally has a manual override (2026-06-14):** an `is_sauna BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE` flag (`sql/activity_is_sauna.sql`, migration `add_is_sauna_to_workouts`) on both tables, surfaced as a single on/off toggle on every **non-`weightlifting_msk` WHOOP row** on the `/activities` page (for saunas the device logged under a generic sport, or bundled with another activity). It is ETL-preserving exactly like `is_excluded` (not in the upsert payloads) and **OR's into `act_sauna`** in the pipeline. It **replaced the deactivated "Used a sauna?" habit (HAB-43)** — going forward sauna is sourced from the activity, not the habit. The lifting dimensions devices can't distinguish — WHOOP logs all resistance training as one generic `weightlifting_msk` and Garmin records no strength at all — are reported manually as **two MULTI-select dimensions** (a session can be many things at once: an upper day is push + pull, a full day touches everything):
  - **coarse split** — `split_labels TEXT[]`, elements ⊆ `{leg, pull, push}`.
  - **granular muscle groups** — `muscle_groups TEXT[]`, elements ⊆ the canonical 11-tag set `{chest, back, shoulders, biceps, triceps, forearms, quads, hamstrings, glutes, calves, core}`.

  Both columns live on `whoop_workouts` + `garmin_activities` (nullable; array-containment `<@` CHECK constraints; `sql/activity_muscle_groups.sql`, migration `add_split_labels_and_muscle_groups` 2026-06-07). The **legacy scalar `split_label TEXT`** (`sql/activity_split_label.sql`) is **kept as a passive mirror of the PRIMARY split** (= `split_labels[0]`) so un-migrated readers + rollbacks stay safe; `split_labels` is canonical and the API writes both on every save. Both arrays are set from the **`/activities` page** — each strength row shows a multi-select Split pill row + a multi-select Muscles pill row (toggle each on/off) → `POST /api/activities/categorize` (`{source, id, split_labels[], muscle_groups[]}`, partial updates allowed; passes `id` as a **string** because `whoop_workouts.workout_id` is TEXT; accepts the legacy `{split_label}` form for BC). **ETL-preserving exactly like `is_excluded`:** neither array is in the Garmin/WHOOP upsert payloads, so the hourly re-sync's `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` (which only touches payload columns) never clobbers them. The `/activities` page also shows an auto-derived category badge (run/sauna/strength) per row.
  - **LLM tag suggestions (2026-11-11) - one-tap confirm, never auto-written.** For strength-type rows with NO tags from the last 30 days, `/activities` shows a Claude-proposed split + muscle set as ghosted (dashed) chips in the same pill rows, with **Accept** (writes through the existing `POST /api/activities/categorize`, which still validates the arrays), **Dismiss**, and every chip individually toggleable before Accept. The suggestion is generated on demand by `POST /api/activities/suggest-tags` (session auth only; body `{items:[{source,id}]}`, hard cap 10 per call, one model call per request, model `claude-sonnet-5-5`, effort `low`, JSON-schema output) from the session's name, duration, strain, HR, HR-zone minutes, weekday/time and the newest 15 already-tagged strength sessions as examples. The server re-reads every requested row and ignores any that is tagged, excluded, older than 30 days or not strength (same rule as the page: `deriveActivityCategory` in `lib/activity-tags.ts`); model output is validated per item (ids, arrays, values outside the canonical sets stripped) and any model failure degrades to "no suggestion" (HTTP 200, `failed: true`, nothing cached, retried on a later load). Cache + audit table `pds.activity_tag_suggestions` (`sql/activity_tag_suggestions.sql`, PK `(source, activity_id)`, service-role only): a row with a cache entry is never sent to the model again, and `dismissed=true` means dismissed OR accepted (`POST /api/activities/suggest-tags/dismiss`; it stores a tombstone when no cache row exists). The page requests suggestions in an effect after the list has loaded - they are deliberately NOT in the ISR prefetch, and no request is made when nothing is eligible. WHOOP's typed `zone_*_milli` columns are empty in the live table, so the HR-zone minutes are read from `raw_json->score->zone_durations`. Not an ingestion source, so no `/status` card. Tests: `frontend/tests/activity-tag-suggest.test.cjs` (also fails if the tag sets drift between the lib, `/api/activities/categorize` and the SQL CHECKs).
  - **HRV integration:** `hrv_analysis.py:aggregate_activity_categories()` derives per-behavioral-day `act_run/act_sauna/act_leg_day/act_pull_day/act_push_day` + 11 `act_mg_<muscle>` flags, reading the arrays via **non-exclusive membership** (a push+pull day fires BOTH `act_push_day` and `act_pull_day`; a chest+triceps day fires `act_mg_chest` + `act_mg_triceps`; legacy `split_label` is a fallback when `split_labels` is absent). **Keyed on `onyx_behavioral_date`** (not ET-of-start) so it co-locates with the spine and its outcome (the sleep closing that day, same row) even for a post-midnight session; a day with no such session is blanket-filled to 0 (harmless — every downstream consumer drops rows lacking the HRV outcome/lagged-HRV confounders first). `days_since_sauna` reads the robust `act_sauna` (WHOOP `sport_name='sauna'` **OR the manual `is_sauna` override toggle**, OR'd in `aggregate_activity_categories`) instead of the now-dropped WHOOP-journal "used a sauna" question (`journal_used_a_sauna` is dropped in `build_feature_matrix` 2026-06-14 — habit HAB-43 deactivated in Notion with all 288 rows preserved, mirroring the HAB-21 food-habit drop; takes effect on the next retrain). **`is_sauna=true` workouts are loaded UNCONDITIONALLY** (a second `whoop_workouts` fetch that ignores the main loader's `score_state='SCORED'`/`is_excluded` filter) and unioned into the activity-taxonomy frame only — so a hand-flagged sauna WHOOP returned non-SCORED still reaches `act_sauna`, without leaking unscored rows into the strain/workout-to-sleep-gap features that share `whoop_wk`. `act_` is in `CONTROLLABLE_FEATURE_PREFIXES` + the SARIMAX/Prophet exog seed list (so `act_mg_*` inherits SHAP-actionable + exog-seed coverage for free). `causal_inference.py` registers all of `act_run/act_sauna` + the 3 split + 11 `act_mg_*` as `EXPLICIT_BINARY_TREATMENTS` (family `behavior`); **`is_run_day` was dropped as a treatment** (superseded by the WHOOP+Garmin `act_run`, which treats rest days as control vs `is_run_day`'s active-only control) but **kept as a matrix column** (`consecutive_run_days` + XGBoost use it). Stats populate after ~3 weeks of labels (cell-size gates); the muscle groups have no history so they sit in `dropped_low_n` until enough tagged sessions accrue, then enter the FDR family. Not a new ingestion source → no `/status` card or nav change (an annotation layer over existing sources, like `is_excluded`).
- **"Calories burnt" / daily energy expenditure: WHOOP is the canonical source, not Garmin.** Any UI/query/feature surfacing TDEE reads `pds.whoop_cycles.kilojoule` and converts kJ → kcal via `/4.184`. Tag each cycle to its ET cycle date via the `+12h` rule (matches the timezone convention above). Don't substitute Garmin's `total_kilocalories` even though it covers the same concept — Garmin's BMR + active calc may diverge, and Riley has confirmed WHOOP as the source of record on Onyx. Implementation reference: `frontend/src/lib/queries.ts:getWhoopCaloriesBurnt` and the Calorie Trend / Net Energy Balance charts on `/nutrition`.
- **Supplements: isolated dim/fact tables, joined in Python (NOT in `daily_health_matrix`).** `pds.supplement_products` (dim, JSONB ingredients) + `pds.supplement_intake` (fact, one row per intake event). The DB-level `pds.daily_supplement_matrix` view exists (one row per behavioral-day, with a `compounds_jsonb` map + `distinct_compounds` + `total_doses`) but is **not** LEFT JOINed into `daily_health_matrix_behavioral` — it's loaded directly by `hrv_analysis.py` (read of `supplement_intake_by_compound`) and merged in pandas. Same isolation principle as Spotify + Notion-journal: open-ended compound space + sparse coverage make a per-day SQL JOIN noisy compared to a Python merge that can filter on coverage. If you need supplements alongside other matrix variables, do `LEFT JOIN pds.daily_supplement_matrix dsm ON dsm.calendar_date = dhm.calendar_date` at query time. Compound names come from `pds.daily_supplement_matrix.compounds_jsonb` (a `{compound_name: {amount, unit, category}}` map — query specific compounds with `(compounds_jsonb->'Vitamin D'->>'amount')::numeric`). JSONB rather than hardcoded columns because the compound space is open-ended (50+ across a stack) and a user's supplement list changes — locking schema would be wrong. The underlying `pds.supplement_intake_by_compound` view explodes ingredient JSONB × dose and groups by FDA **UNII** code (when present) so cross-product summation just works: Vitamin C from a multivitamin and a standalone Vitamin C tablet roll up into one row. Product data comes from the **NIH DSLD** (Dietary Supplement Label Database) — public API at `https://api.ods.od.nih.gov/dsld/v9/`, no auth, covers vitamins/minerals/botanicals/nootropics with full ingredient lists. Seed paths: `python supplement_lookup.py search "<query>"` → `seed <dsld_id>`, or `seed-from-upc <upc>`, or the `/supplements` page's search/barcode-scan UI. The Next.js `/api/supplements/*` routes and the Python CLI share parsing logic (`frontend/src/lib/dsld.ts` mirrors `supplement_lookup.py:normalize_label`) so both produce identical rows.
- **Melatonin: journal↔supplement bridge (`sql/melatonin_unified.sql`, 2026-06-14).** The WHOOP-derived journal question "Took a melatonin supplement?" (HAB-39) was duplicative with the `/supplements` tracker, so it's **deactivated in Notion** (Active=false — name stays frozen/un-renameable, all 288 rows preserved) and **dropped from the journal family** in `build_feature_matrix` (`journal_took_a_melatonin_supplement`, mirroring the `consumed_caffeine` / `ate_food_close_to_bedtime` / `used_a_sauna` drops). To avoid losing the ~288 frozen observations, the **read-only** view `pds.melatonin_unified` unifies them with the supplement-tracker mg dose on the shared behavioral-day axis (one row/day): `melatonin_present` (binary journal Yes/No, **overridden to taken on any day a real supplement dose exists** — pill log wins; also fills the 3-day journal gap 2026-06-08..10) is loaded as `supplement_melatonin_amount` so it joins the **supplement** family/confounders/labels while staying ~49% non-null on the HRV-valid basis (clears the 5% gate → no cutover gap in SHAP/causal). `melatonin_dose_mg` (NaN on non-dose days incl. the whole pre-tracking era — **never nominal-filled**, so no fabricated zeros) is a continuous supplement treatment (`causal_inference.py`) = dose-response *among* melatonin nights. The raw `pivot_supplements` melatonin column is dropped first so there's exactly ONE melatonin column (asserted: single column, domain ⊆ {0,1}). Read-only view → frozen era untouched; **supplement is source-of-truth going forward** (log via `/supplements`; the inactive habit no longer feeds journal). Takes effect on the next retrain — melatonin relocates from the Journal sub-sections to Supplement + Causal on `/analytics/hrv`. The dead `JOURNAL_LABELS["took_melatonin"]` key was removed (the real slug was always `took_a_melatonin_supplement`).
- **Flight-day bridge (`sql/flight_day_unified.sql`, 2026-11-09).** The WHOOP-derived habit "Traveled on a plane?" was **deactivated in Notion** (Active=false — name frozen, all 308 rows preserved) because `pds.flights` now records flights automatically; it no longer appears on `/check-in`. Unlike the melatonin/sauna/caffeine drops, the **feature identity is kept**: `hrv_analysis.py:build_feature_matrix` OVERWRITES `journal_traveled_on_a_plane` (label "Air Travel", journal family, causal binary enumeration) with the read-only view `pds.flight_day_unified.flew`, one row per behavioral day: **1** = a confirmed/changed segment departs or lands that behavioral day OR the frozen habit said Yes (keeps the 6 non-Delta Yes days of Feb–May 2026); **0** = inside tracker coverage with neither (this fills the never-answered days and overrides the habit-No days that had a tracked flight); **NaN** = before coverage and unanswered. Coverage starts at the behavioral day of the earliest `parsed` row in `pds.flight_email_log` (a 1200-day IMAP backfill on 2026-11-09 reached Aug 2023 → 66 flight days vs 22 habit Yes), so a longer `flights-email.yml -f days=N` backfill widens it automatically. **Known gap: non-Delta flights after retirement read 0** — add the airline to `flights_email_etl.py` if that starts to matter. `source_flag` ('flight'|'habit'|'both'|'tracker_no_flight'|'habit_no') audits each day.
- **Supplement stacks — recurring consumption as a bill-of-materials (`supplement_stack_schema.sql`).** `pds.supplement_stack` (header: name, description, `is_active` soft-delete) + `pds.supplement_stack_item` (lines: `product_id` + `doses`, `UNIQUE(stack_id, product_id)`, `ON DELETE CASCADE`). A stack is a **template only** — logging one writes ordinary `pds.supplement_intake` rows (one per item, carrying that item's `doses`), so every rollup (`supplement_intake_by_compound`, `daily_supplement_matrix`) and the HRV/causal pipeline pick the rows up with **zero changes**. The one fact-table change is a nullable provenance column `supplement_intake.stack_id` (`ON DELETE SET NULL`) recording which stack produced an intake — enables grouped "undo all" and "logged from stack" labeling without ever breaking history when a stack is deleted. Shared batch-log logic lives in `frontend/src/lib/supplement-stacks.ts:logStackIntake` (used by both the API route and the chat tool), and it defaults `intake_date` to `pds.behavioral_today_now()` exactly like single-intake logging (awake-tail + TZ aware). Surfaces: (1) the **Stacks** card on `/supplements` (create/edit via `StackEditorModal.tsx` (autosaves: a new stack is created once it has a name and a product; the create sends a `client_key` UUID so a retried create returns the same stack, `sql/supplement_stack_client_key.sql`; every save is ONE call to `pds.save_supplement_stack` (`sql/supplement_stack_atomic_save.sql`, 2026-11-16): header + the complete item list in one transaction, checked against the `version` the editor loaded (409 `stale` → "Load the latest version"; a body with no version, i.e. an out-of-date page, is 409 `reload`), a malformed `items` is a 400 that changes nothing, a save with no clear answer (dropped connection, unknown error code, a reply that is not a saved row) is `unconfirmed`: shown as "Not confirmed" with Retry and resent with the same save id, which the function answers as a replay, never as "Nothing was changed" (after one, changing the form back to what it loaded is still sent, as the Autosaver's `unknownOutcome` option makes no value count as saved until a save answers; a failed re-read of the list after closing keeps Edit disabled, with Retry), and there is no fallback to the old multi-request save (503 until the SQL is applied, so apply it and run the read-only `sql/checks/verify_supplement_stack_atomic_save.sql` BEFORE merging; the behaviour checks `*_branch.sql` never run on production); logic in `lib/supplement-stack-save.ts` + `lib/supplement-stack-chain.ts`, test `frontend/tests/supplement-stack-save.test.cjs`); since 2026-11-15 a stack is logged on Log's Supplements section, Log it / Skip / Undo, not here); (2) the **`log_supplement_stack`** chat tool ("log my morning stack" → resolves the stack by case-insensitive name, returns the available-stacks list on a miss). API: `/api/supplements/stacks` (GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE — PATCH replaces all items on save; DELETE soft-deletes) + `/api/supplements/stacks/log` (POST → batch intake). **No `/status` card for stacks themselves** — they write to the existing `supplement_intake` table, so they aren't a new ingestion source (the supplements freshness signal is unchanged).
- **Supplement stack reminders (2026-11-11, `sql/supplement_stack_reminders.sql`, `lib/supplement-reminders.ts`).** A stack with `pds.supplement_stack.remind_time` (local `HH:MM`, NULL = off; set in the stack editor's "Reminder time" field; seeded 08:30 Morning Stack / 22:30 Night Stack) is offered as a `supplement` card with **Log it** / **Skip** buttons in `/agents`. The sms-reminders Worker does the posting and holds no Supabase credentials: it polls `POST /api/supplements/reminders/claim` (`{now?}`) and relays taps to `POST /api/supplements/reminders/respond` (`{stack_id, behavioral_date, action: 'log'|'skip'}`); both are exact-path CI-token routes in `isCiRoute()` — never widen to `/api/supplements/*`. **Claim** = an active stack whose local time (`pds.tz_for_instant(now)`, so travel works) is within 4 h after `remind_time`, with no `supplement_intake` for that stack on `pds.behavioral_date_for(now)` and no ledger row yet; the claim is an atomic `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING` into `pds.supplement_stack_reminder` (PK `(stack_id, behavioral_date)`, status `prompted`/`logged`/`skipped`, service-role only), so concurrent polls can never prompt twice, and everything that can fail runs *before* that insert. The response also lists `settled` prompts that got logged another way (`/supplements`, chat) so the Worker can tick the card. A reminder time before 06:00 local belongs to the previous behavioral day (the −6 h awake-tail rule). **Respond** is idempotent with the ledger row as the lock (status-guarded UPDATE, only the winner writes intake via `logStackIntake`; a failed write restores the row): `log` stamps `intake_time` only when the tapped day is the current behavioral day, otherwise `NULL`; a repeat or an already-logged stack answers `already_logged`; only a (stack, day) the claim created can be answered (404 otherwise). `/status` **Supplement Reminders** card (badge AGENTS) = `supplement_reminders|prompt` heartbeat age (>3 h degraded, >8 h or failed red; a day with nothing due is healthy); the claim route writes it on every call that claims something and otherwise at most every 50 minutes. Tests: `frontend/tests/supplement-reminders.test.cjs`, `frontend/tests/agent-card-actions.test.cjs`.
- **Supplements on `/log` (2026-11-15, `app/log/sections/Supplements.tsx`).** One row per active stack (reminder time, logged or not on behavioral today): **Log it** = `POST /api/supplements/stacks/log` with the stack id only (the server stamps `behavioral_today_now()` + now, so a screen left open overnight cannot write the old day), **Skip** only while that day's reminder card is still `prompted` (`POST /api/supplements/reminders/respond` with the owner session; the middleware admits a session there too), **Undo** deletes the stack's rows for the day. Log it does not answer the reminder row, so the next claim's `settled` list ticks the `/agents` card. **Log a product**: tap one of the `/supplements` products (shown without typing, most used first by single doses among the newest 200 intakes of the last 30 days from `GET /api/supplements/history` (one of the HTML's early reads; the options never wait for it), the order fixed on first display while the section is mounted, the search box filters them, a tap only selects; `lib/log-supplement-options.ts`), amount + unit, clock times, day → **`POST /api/supplements/log-product`** (owner session only, NOT in `isCiRoute()`; same-origin JSON, 2 KB cap), which calls the chat tool's `logSupplementIntake` (`lib/supplement-intake.ts`; now also takes an exact `product_id`, notes "Logged from /log"), so doses, times, the awake tail, the no-time rule and the duplicate skip are the chat tool's. **Logged today**: single products with Undo (`DELETE /api/supplements/log-intake`). Say it drafts (product / stack) are listed as suggestions and written only on the section's Save; a product suggestion can be moved into the form with Edit. `GET /api/supplements/today` also returns `stack_id` per intake and `reminders` (`[{stack_id, status}]` for that day, `null` when that read fails).
- **Reading module (`/reading`, 2026-11-07) — a gamified reading log with highlight + comment capture and a Notion mirror.** Tables in `sql/reading_schema.sql`: `pds.books` (slug, total_chapters, start/target_date, status, `notion_page_id` = the per-book reading-log page, `habit_name` = the habit stamped on session days), `pds.reading_sessions` (behavioral `session_date`, chapter range, minutes, medium, `recall` = the from-memory note, `question`), `pds.book_highlights` (passage + `comment` + `concept` tag + source, UNIQUE on (book, passage) for idempotent Kindle re-imports, SM-2-lite fields `ease`/`interval_days`/`next_review`), `pds.reading_reviews` (grades), `pds.reading_quizzes` (checkpoint scores). **Everything gamified is derived, never stored** (`lib/reading.ts:computeOverview`): XP (10/chapter, 20/recall ≥40 chars, 5/commented highlight, 15/review day, 10/quiz point, 50/on-time checkpoint), levels every 100 XP with plant-themed titles, streak over behavioral days, pace vs the linear start→target line, checkpoints at 30/65/100% of the book, 10 badges. Writes: `POST /api/reading/sessions` (with `client_key`, a UUID per new /log session, `sql/reading_sessions_client_key.sql`: a create retried after a lost answer returns the stored session, `replayed: true`, with no second XP, habit stamp or Notion entry; also upserts `habit_journal` Yes for `books.habit_name` on that day — so /habits + the HRV `habit_*` columns see reading for free — and flips `status=finished` on the last chapter) and **`PATCH`/`DELETE /api/reading/sessions/[id]`** (2026-11-15, `updateSession` / `deleteSession` in `lib/reading.ts`: the autosaving /log form edits or removes the session it created; same rules; Kindle sessions 409; an edit or removal undoes what the earlier value did: a finish this session caused goes back to reading when no other session reaches the last chapter (a moved finishing session moves the finish day), and a new day or book stamps that book's habit Yes there while the old day's Yes is removed only when this session created it (`habit_created_on`, reported by the server and sent back by the form) and no other session with the same habit is on that day; owner session only, never in `isCiRoute()`, same-origin JSON, 48 KB cap; the Notion block follows in `after()`; both create routes re-read the row after storing `notion_block_id` and update, trash or move the entry when an autosave edit or removal landed first), `/api/reading/highlights` (the /reading "Highlight + comment" form saves itself: created on the first non-empty passage, then PATCHed; a create retried after a lost answer adopts the row its own send made) (+ `/[id]` PATCH incl. passage + source / DELETE soft-archive, `/import` for pasted Kindle "Export notebook" HTML / My Clippings.txt / plain lists via `lib/kindle-import.ts`), `/api/reading/review` (grade → reschedule), `/api/reading/quiz`, `/api/reading/books`. **Notion mirror (`lib/reading-notion.ts`, best-effort in `after()`):** each session is inserted as a quote block right after the "Session log → Newest first." paragraph of the book's page, each highlight under a "Highlights" heading the code creates on first use, and the top callout's "Progress: chapter X of N" line is rewritten — `notion_block_id` on the row records the mirror; the Onyx Notion integration must be able to see the page (The Goal's page lives under Project Onyx). Chat tools: `get_reading_status` (coaching + quiz source material), `log_reading_session` (recall passed VERBATIM), `add_book_highlight`, `record_reading_quiz` (the model asks 5 questions one at a time, then records the score; 80% passes). `/status` has a manual-method "Reading" card keyed on the newest session. No in-app e-reader by design: Kindle/Audible files are DRM-locked, so capture happens here and reading happens there. **Automatic Kindle ingestion = `tools/kindle_harvest/harvest.mjs`** (Riley's PC, Task Scheduler "Onyx Kindle Harvest" every 3 h while logged on — the StreetEasy-harvester pattern, because Amazon has no highlights API and the reader needs a signed-in browser; persistent Chrome profile `%LOCALAPPDATA%\\Onyx\\kindle-harvest-profile`, one-time `node harvest.mjs --login`). **The task is registered by `tools/kindle_harvest/register-task.ps1` (2026-11-09)**: `wscript.exe run-hidden.js` launches node with no console window and passes its exit code through; runs on battery, `StartWhenAvailable` catches up runs missed during sleep, 20-min execution limit, never wakes the PC — the original bare `schtasks … node.exe` registration refused to start on battery (`0x800710E0`) and skipped sleep-missed runs, leaving the heartbeat 32 h stale. Re-run that script instead of hand-writing `schtasks`. **v2 (2026-11-08) reads the Kindle for Web READER, not the Notebook DOM:** opening `read.amazon.com/?asin=<ASIN>` makes the app fetch, and the script intercept, (a) `getAnnotations` JSON = every highlight/note with its Kindle **position** and real **timestamp**, (b) the first `renderer/render` tar whose `toc.json` (chapter → start position), `location_map.json` (`navigationUnit` = print page labels) and `metadata.json` (book length) are parsed with a minimal ustar reader (skip the `PaxHeaders.X/` stubs), and (c) the page HTML's `mostRecentPositionRead` = the Whispersync reading position. The direct `getAnnotations` URL is NOT callable with cookies alone (403 "not an ADP session request") — intercepting the app's own call is the supported path. It posts `{asin, last_position, total_positions, chapters[], items[{passage, comment, position, position_end, page, location, highlighted_at, note_at}]}` with `harvest: true`; the route upserts `pds.book_chapters` (numbered TOC entries only), bumps `books.last_position` monotonically, upserts highlights (idempotent on passage; chapter derived from position), then runs **`lib/reading-kindle.ts:syncKindleSessions`** — a pure recomputation that writes ONE `source='kindle'` session per behavioral day (unique partial index): a highlight ending within ~800 positions of a chapter's end or on its last page **completes that chapter**, a highlight inside chapter N proves N−1, the reading position works the same way, and **any day with a new highlight or note gets a session** (chapter_from/through NULL when nothing completed, `in_chapter` set, `recall` = that day's notes verbatim) — so Kindle reading is never logged by hand; manual/`chat` sessions are untouched and stay the path for audiobook/paper. Session day = the highlight's own timestamp through `pds.behavioral_date_for()` (trigger-set `book_highlights.behavioral_date`); the position is attributed to the harvest day. Opening the book in the web reader does not move the reading position. Heartbeat `kindle|highlights` success/partial/failed (an expired Amazon session or an Amazon `/checkpoint/` bounce after one 30 s retry is a failed row). Token: `~/.onyx_ci_token` (= `ONYX_CI_TOKEN`). `/status` "Kindle" card: >12 h degraded, >48 h or error = red (loose because the PC may be off). Adding a book = a `pds.books` row + `{slug, asin, titleMatch}` in `books.json`. **Run now (2026-11-16):** the `/status` Kindle card's button (`components/KindleRunNow.tsx`, `lib/kindle-run.ts`) calls `POST /api/kindle/run` (owner session only, never in `isCiRoute()`; same-origin JSON `{}`, 256-byte cap), which inserts a row into `pds.pc_run_requests` (`sql/pc_run_requests.sql`, applied; service-role only, in the `supabase_realtime` publication); a press while the newest request is unanswered (< 2 min) or still running (< 25 min) returns that one. **Event driven, no polling job on the PC (Riley's requirement):** Supabase Realtime pushes the INSERT to `tools/kindle_harvest/run-listener.mjs` (one idle Node process holding one websocket with the service key from `~/.onyx_supabase_secret`; started hidden at sign-in by a Startup-folder shortcut, set up and restarted by `register-listener.ps1`; one instance via a named-pipe lock; reconnects with backoff and after sleep), which starts the "Onyx Kindle Harvest" task (IgnoreNew keeps a pressed and a scheduled run apart) and writes `picked_up_at` + `outcome` (started / already_running / failed), then `finished_at` + `exit_code`, on the row; the button reads it via `GET /api/kindle/run?id=`. No answer in 15 s = the PC looks asleep or off. Tests: `frontend/tests/kindle-run.test.cjs`, `tools/kindle_harvest/run-listener.test.mjs`.
- **Barcode scanning is built-in.** `BarcodeScannerModal` (`@zxing/browser`) opens the rear camera, detects UPC-A/UPC-E/EAN-13, calls back with the digit string. The `/supplements` Add Product flow uses this: scan a bottle → `/api/supplements/search?q=<upc>` → if exactly one hit, single-click seed; else show candidates. Camera resources are torn down on close/detect/unmount to avoid stale streams. Works in any HTTPS browser context — including the PWA standalone shell.
- **Custom-product fallback via photo + Claude vision.** When a product isn't in DSLD (private-label, niche brand, regional SKU), the Add Product modal exposes a "Not in DSLD? Add custom →" link. Flow: snap a photo of the Supplement Facts panel → client-side resize to 1600px JPEG @ 0.85 → `POST /api/supplements/extract-from-photo` (base64) → Claude Sonnet 4 vision reads the label → returns the same `NormalizedProduct` shape DSLD produces. The route prepends a **reference table of (ingredient_group, UNII, category) tuples derived from existing `pds.supplement_products`** to the prompt, so Claude maps extracted ingredients to the user's already-canonical compound vocabulary. A server-side post-pass also backfills `unii_code` + `category` from the reference table for any row Claude returned with a matching `ingredient_group` but null UNII. The extracted product lands in an editable review form (`CustomSupplementFlow.tsx`) where every field — including per-ingredient quantity, unit, UNII, category — can be hand-corrected before save. `POST /api/supplements/custom-product` writes the row with `product_id = "custom_<hex>"` and `dsld_id = null`; `raw_json` carries the Claude usage stats + reference-table size for replay. Cross-product compound rollup in `supplement_intake_by_compound` works transparently because matched UNII codes are the join key. **The photo is never persisted** — it's sent to Anthropic as base64 in the single vision call and discarded after the response. Files: `frontend/src/app/api/supplements/extract-from-photo/route.ts`, `frontend/src/app/api/supplements/custom-product/route.ts`, `frontend/src/components/CustomSupplementFlow.tsx`.
- **Spotify play coverage is incomplete by design.** `recently-played` only contains plays that Spotify's backend received — offline playback from Spotify-licensed partner devices (Garmin watches with downloaded playlists, some car head units, older standalone wearables) does **not** report per-track telemetry back to the account, so those plays are invisible to our ETL, to Wrapped, and to Spotify-generated personalization playlists. Phone/desktop/web app plays are reported in real time and *are* captured. The `/spotify` page surfaces this as a coverage note under the page header so users interpreting the sonic profile / volume / ledger understand the dashboard under-counts Garmin-heavy workout listening. No code-level fix is possible — Spotify's partner SDK simply doesn't pipe the data.
- **`pds.journal_entries` is the personal Notion journal — distinct from `pds.journal` (the WHOOP+habit *behavior* view).** Notion DB "Entries" (ID `96541038264d45aba2a9601d9b175a7e`, parent page "Journal"). One row per Notion page; PK = `notion_page_id`. Properties: `entry_date` (ET-naive — Notion's Date property has no time component), `title`, `mood` (low/neutral/good/great), `source` (voice/remarkable/typed), `confidence` (high/medium/low), `topics` (JSONB array), `content_md` (page body as markdown), `word_count`, `embedding` vector(1024) from Voyage `voyage-3-large`, `archived` (soft-delete). Skip-if-unchanged guard: `notion_edited_at` — ETL only re-fetches blocks / re-embeds when Notion's `last_edited_time` advances. Indexes: B-tree on `entry_date` + `mood`, GIN on `topics`, HNSW (cosine) on `embedding`. RPC `pds.search_journal_entries(query_embedding, date_from, date_to, mood_filter, topic_filters, result_limit)` exposes filtered + similarity-ordered search to the chat tool. **Not auto-joined into `daily_health_matrix`** — same isolation principle as Spotify; cross-analysis happens at query time only. Notion is single write surface (read-only direction, unlike habits which is bidirectional). The app has two journal writers, both following the rules below and writing to Notion, never to the table: the /check-in journal draft (`POST /api/journal/entry`, 2026-11-14; every label reviewed by Riley and sent) and the /log **Journal** section (`POST /api/journal/file`, 2026-11-15, `lib/journal-file.ts` + `lib/journal-file-server.ts`; see "Log screen"): the body exactly as sent, Date = today in New York, Source as sent, and any of Title / Mood / Confidence / Topics left blank filled by ONE structured-output model call (`claude-sonnet-5-5`, effort low) that returns only those four labels (it never sees a way to change the body); a repeat of the same day and words within 15 minutes returns the page already there (Notion query on Date + created_time, then the page's paragraphs compared with the body). Both share the Notion calls in `lib/journal-file-server.ts`; neither logs the text. Sync via `journal_etl.py` hourly at `:35`. A Notion-hosted image is stored in `content_md` as `![caption](notion-image:<block id>)`, never as its download URL (signed, dead after about an hour); the embedding text carries only `[image: caption]`. Rows that were re-embedded and rows that were not are upserted in separate requests (`uniform_batches`; see the bulk-upsert rule under Conventions). Test: `tests/test_journal_etl.py`. Journal DB must be shared with the "SMS Reminders" Notion integration (same blocker as initial habits deployment — `project_onyx_habits.md` memory).

  **When creating a journal entry in Notion (e.g. forwarding a Claude conversation Riley dictated):** the `notion-create-pages` call **must** populate all four metadata properties — `Source`, `Confidence`, `Mood`, `Topics` — plus `Title` and `Date`. Never create a journal page with any of these unset. Parent: `{ type: "data_source_id", data_source_id: "de49562b-652b-4e95-94b1-085dc7ce8c99" }` (the Entries data source under the Journal DB).

  **Field rules** — these mirror the canonical version in the **"Reminders/Habits/Journal Entry"** Claude.ai Project, which is the source of truth for the mobile/web Claude flow. **When changing the rules here, also update that Project's custom instructions to keep both surfaces in sync.**
  - **`Source`** (select: `voice` | `typed` | `remarkable`). DEFAULT `voice` — anything originating from a Claude conversation. Override to `typed` only if the user explicitly says the entry's origin was typed text; `remarkable` only if it came from their reMarkable tablet.
  - **`Confidence`** (select: `high` | `medium` | `low`). Measures the **user's certainty about the entry's content** (NOT transcription confidence — repurposed 2026-05-24; the Notion property description was updated to match). DEFAULT `medium` whenever the entry doesn't clearly signal certainty — never leave blank. `high` for explicit conviction ("I know…", "definitely"); `low` for hedging ("I think maybe…", "could be wrong").
  - **`Mood`** (select: `low` | `neutral` | `good` | `great`). Infer from content; never leave blank. low = negative affect, neutral = informational/flat, good = positive affect, great = strong positive. If torn between adjacent values, pick the lower one.
  - **`Topics`** (multi-select). Prefer existing tags when one fits — current list: `work, relationships, dating, health, gym, running, alcohol, sobriety, mental-health, family, travel, reflection, goals, finance, sleep`. New tags are allowed only when none of the existing ones reasonably fit (don't add "fitness" when "gym" or "running" applies; don't add "anxiety" when "mental-health" applies). At least one tag per entry.
  - **`Title`** (title): short, specific, 3–8 words. Scannable in a list. Avoid generic titles like "Journal Entry" or "Note".
  - **`Date`** (date): today in `America/New_York`, date-only. Override if the user names a different date ("yesterday", explicit date).

  **Body — VERBATIM RULE.** The page body MUST be the user's raw text exactly as they said it. Do NOT summarize, paraphrase, condense, restructure, rewrite for clarity/flow/grammar, remove tangents/repetition/filler, add headings or bullet structure the user didn't provide, or add narrator framing ("Today Riley reflected on…"). The journal is a primary-source record of the user's own words. The ONLY editing allowed: sentence-ending punctuation + commas at clear speech pauses, paragraph breaks at natural topic shifts, capitalization, and unambiguous dictation-typo fixes (e.g. "their"/"there"). The `Title` is where summarization belongs — never let title-style condensation leak into the body. If the user explicitly asks for a summary ("just summarize this", "give me the gist"), summarize — otherwise the body is verbatim.

## Environment Variables

Root `.env` (Python ETL): SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY, SUPABASE_ANON_KEY,
GARMIN_EMAIL, GARMIN_PASSWORD, WHOOP_CLIENT_ID, WHOOP_CLIENT_SECRET,
EIGHTSLEEP_EMAIL, EIGHTSLEEP_PASSWORD, IMAP_HOST, IMAP_EMAIL, IMAP_APP_PASSWORD,
MFP_USERNAME, MFP_PASSWORD, SPOTIFY_CLIENT_ID, SPOTIFY_CLIENT_SECRET, SPOTIFY_REDIRECT_URI,
TANITA_CLIENT_ID, TANITA_CLIENT_SECRET, CRONOMETER_EMAIL, CRONOMETER_PASSWORD,
NOTION_API_KEY, NOTION_JOURNAL_DB, VOYAGE_API_KEY, GOOGLE_CALENDAR_ICS_SECRET (JSON list of {account, calendar, url})

`frontend/.env.local`: NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL, NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY,
SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY, ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, NOTION_API_KEY, NOTION_HABITS_DB, VOYAGE_API_KEY, SPOTIFY_CLIENT_ID, SPOTIFY_CLIENT_SECRET

## Agent Permissions

Each agent's permissions live in that agent's own settings, not in this repo (Claude Code: `~/.claude/settings.json` + the untracked `.claude/settings.local.json`). Whatever the agent, ask before `rm`, `kill`, other destructive commands, and Supabase project lifecycle operations (pause, restore, delete, branch merge/reset).

## GitHub Actions ETL

All data sources run **hourly** on a staggered schedule to spread load and avoid thundering herds:

| Workflow | File | Cron | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hourly Health ETL | `daily-etl.yml` | `0 * * * *` | Garmin + WHOOP (2 parallel jobs) |
| Eight Sleep ETL | `eight-sleep-etl.yml` | `0 19 * * *` | Eight Sleep — daily at a fixed 19:00 UTC (3 PM EDT / 2 PM EST; data only updates post-sleep) |
| MyFitnessPal email | `mfp-email.yml` | **disabled 2026-05-31** (`workflow_dispatch` only) | Manual fallback for a late MFP export; Cronometer is the nutrition source |
| WHOOP journal email | `whoop-journal-email.yml` | **decommissioned 2026-06-11** (`workflow_dispatch` only) | WHOOP journal merged into habits — see `sql/whoop_journal_merge.sql` |
| Notion Journal Sync | `journal-sync.yml` | `35 * * * *` | Notion DB query → upsert + Voyage embed → `pds.journal_entries` |
| Habits sync | `habits-sync.yml` | `45 * * * *` | Curls `POST /api/habits/sync` on Vercel |
| Care Calendar Catch-up | `care-calendar-catch-up.yml` | `20 */6 * * *` | Curls `POST /api/jobs/care-calendar-catch-up` on Vercel (CI token; exact path in middleware `isCiRoute()`): the same Google Calendar write sync as Sync now on /health. Answers `{ran: false}` until Google is connected (docs/health-google-calendar.md steps 1-3); fails the run on any non-200. |
| Spotify ETL | `spotify-etl.yml` | `50 * * * *` | Pulls recently-played; upserts plays + tracks; featurizes new tracks via ReccoBeats. Hourly (was 2h) to minimize loss from Spotify's 50-track server-side cap; emits `partial` sync_log status when a run returns exactly 50 items so /status surfaces cap-hit incidents. |
| Cronometer ETL | `cronometer-etl.yml` | `30 2 * * *` + `0 16 * * *` | Automated web-export of Cronometer nutrition (unofficial endpoints) → `run_import` → `cronometer_*` tables. Twice daily; session in `ci_tokens`. |
| Tanita ETL | `tanita-etl.yml` | `0 14 * * *` + `30 17 * * *` | Health Planet weigh-ins → tanita_measurements + weight_log rollup. Mid-morning ET (post weigh-in + phone-app sync) + early-afternoon catch-up; daily cadence — the scale yields ~1 measurement/day. |
| Flights Email ETL | `flights-email.yml` | `25 */3 * * *` | Parses Delta operational emails from BOTH Gmail accounts over IMAP (read-only, All Mail) → `pds.flights`; proposes `user_tz_log` rows for landed segments; `flights\|email` heartbeat. `workflow_dispatch` inputs `days` (backfill window) + `reprocess`. |
| Delivery Email ETL | `delivery-email.yml` | `*/15 * * * *` | Parses Uber Eats receipt emails over IMAP (read-only, All Mail) → `pds.delivery_orders`; `delivery\|email` heartbeat. `workflow_dispatch` inputs `days` (backfill window, e.g. 1500) + `reprocess`. |
| Air Quality ETL | `air-quality-etl.yml` | `12 */3 * * *` | Open-Meteo hourly PM2.5 / US AQI (no key) → daily means in `pds.air_quality_daily`; `air_quality\|daily` heartbeat. `workflow_dispatch` input `days` (backfill window). Needs `sql/air_quality_schema.sql` applied once. |
| Weather ETL | `weather-etl.yml` | `32 1-23/3 * * *` | Open-Meteo hourly weather (no key) → daily rows in `pds.weather_daily` (+ UV index from the historical-forecast API); `weather\|daily` heartbeat. `workflow_dispatch` input `days` (backfill window). Table from `sql/weather_schema.sql` (applied 2026-11-13). |
| Pollen ETL | `pollen-etl.yml` | `20 16,21 * * *` | Atlanta Allergy & Asthma pollen page (no key, one request per run) → `pds.pollen_daily`; `pollen\|daily` heartbeat. `workflow_dispatch` input `months` (backfill, one request per month). Needs `sql/pollen_schema.sql` applied once. |
| Online Orders Email | `orders-email.yml` | `5 */3 * * *` | Reads shipping / delivery emails (stores, Amazon, carriers) from BOTH Gmail accounts over IMAP (read-only) → `pds.online_order_events`, then rebuilds `pds.online_orders`; `online_orders\|email` heartbeat. `workflow_dispatch` inputs `days` + `reprocess`. First run (empty table) reads 400 days. |
| Urgent Email Labels | `urgent-email.yml` | `15 * * * *` | Reads inbox-triage's `Agent/Action` + `Agent/Deadline` labels in BOTH Gmail accounts over IMAP (read-only: EXAMINE + BODY.PEEK, no flag or label change) → `pds.inbox_emails` metadata; `urgent_email\|labels` heartbeat. Needs `sql/inbox_emails_schema.sql` applied first. |
| SimpleFIN ETL | `simplefin-etl.yml` | `30 */6 * * *` | One read-only `GET /accounts` from SimpleFIN Bridge (secret `SIMPLEFIN_ACCESS_URL`) -> `pds.finance_*` (account matching, Rocket Money cutover, rule-based categories, balance snapshots); `simplefin\|transactions` + `simplefin\|balances` heartbeats; counts-only log. `workflow_dispatch` input `days` (1-90). |
| Google Calendar ETL | `google-calendar-etl.yml` | `55 * * * *` | Reads the calendar iCal feeds in `GOOGLE_CALENDAR_ICS_SECRET` (one GET each, read-only) → one row per event instance in `pds.calendar_events` (ET today ±400 d); removes events that left the feed, never for a feed that failed; `google_calendar\|events` heartbeat. The log prints counts only (the feed URLs are secrets and the log is public). `workflow_dispatch` inputs `days` + `allow_large_removal`. Needs `sql/calendar_events_schema.sql` applied. |
| HRV prediction | `hrv-prediction.yml` | `workflow_run` after hourly ETL + `50 3 * * *` + `50 4 * * *` | Backfills actuals + predicts tonight's sleep (behavioral today's row; `prediction_date` = that behavioral date, the page shows the wake-up date). Hourly workflow_run runs give intra-day monitoring; the two scheduled crons land on 23:50 ET year-round (one per DST state — the `dst-gate` job skips the wrong-season run by checking `TZ=America/New_York date +%H == 23`). The 23:50 ET run captures the final day's imports (Habits at :45, journal at :30, MFP at :15) before ET midnight closes the day. **`hrv_predict.py` uses `et_today()` (`zoneinfo.ZoneInfo("America/New_York")`) for all date arithmetic** — a UTC `date.today()` on the runner would mis-tag the late-ET-evening run as the day-after-next. |
| HRV Analysis Retrain | `hrv-retrain-on-backfill.yml` | `20 * * * *` + `0 12 * * *` | Two triggers: (1) hourly backfill check via `hrv_backfill_check.py` — runs full `hrv_analysis.py` only if any row with `calendar_date < today-2` was updated since last `hrv_analysis_results.computed_at`. (2) Daily unconditional retrain at 12:00 UTC (~8am ET) — safety net so correlations stay fresh even if no backfill ever fires. The decision is made by the "Decide whether to retrain" step that branches on `github.event.schedule` / `github.event_name`. |

Notes:
- **Filename vs. display name**: `daily-etl.yml` kept for git history; workflow display name is **"Hourly Health ETL"**. The `hrv-prediction.yml` `workflow_run` trigger references the display name.
- **Manual trigger**: `gh workflow run <workflow-file>.yml` for any of them.
- **Token persistence**: Garmin/WHOOP tokens stored in `pds.ci_tokens`, managed by `ci_token_helper.py`.
- **Token recovery (Garmin)**: If Garmin tokens expire in CI, re-run ETL locally then `python ci_token_helper.py upload garmin`.
- **Token recovery (WHOOP)**: A 400 on token refresh has two distinct cases — read the response body. (a) `invalid_grant` = this chain's refresh token expired/was consumed: re-run `python whoop_etl.py --days 7` locally then `python ci_token_helper.py upload whoop`. (b) `invalid_request` ("missing a required parameter / otherwise malformed") with correct request shape = WHOOP invalidated previously-issued tokens server-side (happened fleet-wide overnight 2026-06-11, no changelog entry): no refresh request will ever succeed — run `python whoop_etl.py --auth` (browser login), then `python whoop_etl.py --days 7`, then `python ci_token_helper.py upload whoop`. Diagnostic tip: a Basic-auth probe of the token endpoint returning `invalid_client` "supports client_secret_post" proves the client credentials are recognized and innocent. `whoop_etl.py` exits 1 on total sync failure so the Actions run goes red — check `/status` (sync_log heartbeats) for the error trail.
- **GitHub Secrets**: SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY, GARMIN_EMAIL, GARMIN_PASSWORD, WHOOP_CLIENT_ID, WHOOP_CLIENT_SECRET, EIGHTSLEEP_EMAIL, EIGHTSLEEP_PASSWORD, EIGHTSLEEP_CLIENT_ID, EIGHTSLEEP_CLIENT_SECRET, IMAP_HOST, IMAP_EMAIL, IMAP_APP_PASSWORD, IMAP_EMAIL_2, IMAP_APP_PASSWORD_2 (second Gmail — flights ETL; each account needs its own Gmail App Password with IMAP enabled), SPOTIFY_CLIENT_ID, SPOTIFY_CLIENT_SECRET, TANITA_CLIENT_ID, TANITA_CLIENT_SECRET, CRONOMETER_EMAIL, CRONOMETER_PASSWORD, GOOGLE_CALENDAR_ICS_SECRET (JSON list of {account, calendar, url}: each calendar's secret iCal address; the URLs are credentials), SIMPLEFIN_ACCESS_URL (read-only SimpleFIN Bridge Access URL; the credentials are inside the URL).
- **Spotify bootstrap (first sign-in only, local)**: register app at developer.spotify.com → set redirect URI to `http://127.0.0.1:8888/callback` → put `SPOTIFY_CLIENT_ID` / `SPOTIFY_CLIENT_SECRET` in `.env` → run `python spotify_etl.py --auth` (opens browser) → `python ci_token_helper.py upload spotify`. The sign-in is not one-time: it runs out 180 days later, and every renewal route is in Token recovery (Spotify) below. CI uses `ci_token_helper.py download spotify` at the start of each run and uploads after only a token the run refreshed that is newer than the stored row (refresh tokens occasionally rotate).
- **Token recovery (Spotify)**: a Spotify refresh token stops working **180 days after the sign-in** and a refresh does not extend it (Spotify policy announced 2026-06-18, enforced for existing apps from 2026-07-20). First hit 2026-11-13 02:54Z, 180 days after the 2026-05-17 sign-in: `HTTP 400 invalid_grant: Refresh token expired`, then `Refresh token revoked` on every later run. Only a new sign-in fixes it; re-running cannot. A refused token (or any run that syncs nothing: no token file, token endpoint down, recently-played fetch failed) writes `failed` heartbeats for `spotify|plays`, `reccobeats|audio_features` and `musicbrainz|artist_tags` and **exits 1, so the hourly Actions run is red until it is fixed** (until 2026-11-13 it exited 0 and the run stayed green for a day). Cap-hit (`partial`) and zero-play runs stay green. Every hour of outage can lose plays for good (Spotify keeps the last 50). **Recovery (normal route, 2026-11-13; built and tested offline, not yet run against Spotify):** in a browser signed in to Spotify and to Onyx, open `/status` → **Reconnect Spotify** on the Spotify card → open the link it shows, approve if asked → copy the address of the `127.0.0.1:8888/callback?code=…` page the tab lands on (the page itself does not load) → paste it into the card → Finish → `gh workflow run spotify-etl.yml` and check it is green. No secret on the PC and no code in a workflow input; details in "Spotify sign-in renewal" under Database. All three routes below store the same token blob with a new `authorized_at`, so the newer-wins uploads described at the end of this bullet never put an older token back over it. **Recovery from a PC**, on one whose browser is signed in to Spotify, with `SUPABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, `SPOTIFY_CLIENT_ID`, `SPOTIFY_CLIENT_SECRET` in `.env` or the shell (the Spotify pair: developer.spotify.com/dashboard → the app → Settings; they are otherwise only GitHub / Vercel secrets): `python spotify_etl.py --auth` → `python ci_token_helper.py upload spotify` → `gh workflow run spotify-etl.yml` and check it is green. **Recovery with no secret on the PC** (workflow input added 2026-11-13; the exchange is tested offline but has not yet run against Spotify, so fall back to the local route if it fails): in a browser signed in to Spotify open `https://accounts.spotify.com/authorize?client_id=<client id>&response_type=code&redirect_uri=http%3A%2F%2F127.0.0.1%3A8888%2Fcallback&scope=user-read-recently-played%20playlist-modify-private` (the client id is on the same dashboard page and is not a secret), approve, and copy the address of the `127.0.0.1:8888/callback?code=…` page it lands on (the page itself does not load). Within a few minutes run `gh workflow run spotify-etl.yml -f auth_code="<that address>"`: the run exchanges the code with the client secret held in GitHub secrets (`spotify_etl.py --exchange-code`, which reads the code from the event file and masks it in the log), syncs, and stores the new token. A refused code fails the run before anything is stored. The sign-in stores `authorized_at` in the token blob (Spotify does not report a token's age); from 14 days before the 180-day mark the `spotify|plays` heartbeat turns `partial` with the same reconnect-by date the card shows, and from day 165 the `/status` card shows "Reconnect by <date>" with the Reconnect control, so renew then, not after. `invalid_client` instead of `invalid_grant` means the app id / secret is wrong, not the sign-in. `ci_token_helper.py upload spotify` skips a token file the run never refreshed, refuses an empty one, and is newer-wins against the stored row (a later `authorized_at`, then a later `obtained_at`), so the workflow's `if: always()` upload cannot put a dead token, or a refreshed token of the old sign-in, back over a new sign-in (it did the former, hourly, during the 2026-11-13 outage). Tests: `tests/test_spotify_fail_loud.py`, `tests/test_ci_token_spotify_guard.py`.
- **Tanita bootstrap (one-time, local)**: register a "client application" at healthplanet.jp/apis_account.do (redirect URI `https://www.healthplanet.jp/success.html`) → put `TANITA_CLIENT_ID` / `TANITA_CLIENT_SECRET` in `.env` → run `python tanita_etl.py --auth` (log in, allow, copy the `code` from the success.html address bar — 10-min TTL) → `python ci_token_helper.py upload tanita`. Same download/run/upload-if-always CI shape as Spotify.
- **Token recovery (Tanita)**: refresh tokens have no documented lifetime; if the CI run logs "Health Planet refresh token rejected", re-run `python tanita_etl.py --auth` locally then `python ci_token_helper.py upload tanita`.
- **Cronometer bootstrap (one-time, local)**: put `CRONOMETER_EMAIL` / `CRONOMETER_PASSWORD` in `.env` (and add both as GitHub secrets) → `python cronometer_etl.py --auth` (fresh login + 1-day export; prints AUTH OK with CSV line counts) → `python ci_token_helper.py upload cronometer` (persists the session). The ETL self-refreshes the session each run and re-uploads via `if: always()`.
- **Failure recovery (Cronometer)**: the `/status` Cronometer card goes red on any auth/fetch/import failure (the ETL emits an `error` heartbeat). Two distinct cases from the run log: (a) `GWT-RPC error (permutation may be stale)` → Cronometer shipped a build whose values `resolve_gwt_values()` couldn't auto-derive; check the regexes in `cronometer_etl.py` against the live `nocache.js`/`cache.js`, or re-scrape the permutation + serialization-policy from the browser Network tab (request to `/cronometer/app`). (b) `Cronometer login failed` → password changed or rate-limited; re-run `python cronometer_etl.py --auth` locally then `upload cronometer`.
